\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\lesssim\|(1+|v|^{2})^{r/2}\left[\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{v}^{\beta},E^{u,\varrho}(t,x)\cdot\nabla_{v}\right]f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}_{x,v}}\|f(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\lesssim\|E^{u,\varrho}(t)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}\|f(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r}}^{2},

by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the product law (
A.3
) in Sobolev spaces (since
m>1+d
) of Lemma
A.6
. All in all, we obtain

(2.3)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|f(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+\|E^{u,\varrho}(t)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}+\|E^{u,\varrho}(t)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}})\|f(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r}}^{2}\lesssim(1+\|E^{u,\varrho}(t)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}})\|f(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r}}^{2},

if
m>d/2
. Invoking the estimate (
2.2
) of Lemma
2.7
, and by integrating in time the inequality (
2.3
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|f(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r}}^{2}\leq\|f(0)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r}}^{2}+C\int_{0}^{t}\left(1+\|u(s)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}+\Lambda\left(\|\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-2})}\right)\|\varrho(s)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m+1}}\right)\|f(s)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s,

for all
t\in[0,T)
and for some constant
C>0
independent of
\varepsilon
. Using Cauchy-Schwarz and Grönwall’s inequality, this implies for all
t\in[0,T)

\displaystyle\|f(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r}}^{2}\leq\|f(0)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r}}^{2}\exp\left[C\left((1+\|u\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})})T+\sqrt{T}\Lambda\left(\|\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-2})}\right)\|\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m+1})}\right)\right],

and this concludes the proof.
∎

The estimates given by Proposition
2.3
and Proposition
2.10
show a possible loss of derivatives between
f
and
\varrho
. This constitutes the main obstacle of the analysis.

2.2.
Regularization of the system and setup of the bootstrap

To (temporarily) bypass this problem, we introduce the following regularized version of the equations. Since the pressure gradient in the force field of the Vlasov equation seems to cause estimates with a loss of derivative, we smooth out this precise term. For all
\varepsilon>0
, we consider

(
S_{\varepsilon}
)

\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{t}f_{\varepsilon}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f_{\varepsilon}+{\rm div}_{v}[f_{\varepsilon}(u_{\varepsilon}-v)]-p^{\prime}(\varrho_{\varepsilon})\nabla_{x}\left[(I-\varepsilon^{2}\Delta_{x})^{-1}\varrho_{\varepsilon}\right]\cdot\nabla_{v}f_{\varepsilon}=0&,\\[5.69054pt]
\partial_{t}\varrho_{\varepsilon}+u_{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{x}\varrho_{\varepsilon}+\frac{\varrho_{\varepsilon}}{1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}}}\mathrm{div}_{x}\left[j_{f_{\varepsilon}}-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}}u_{\varepsilon}\right]=-\frac{\varrho_{\varepsilon}}{1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}}}\mathrm{div}_{x}u_{\varepsilon}&,\\[5.69054pt]
\partial_{t}u_{\varepsilon}+(u_{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{x})u_{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{\varrho_{\varepsilon}}\nabla_{x}p(\varrho_{\varepsilon})-\frac{1}{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}})}\Big[\Delta_{x}+\nabla_{x}\mathrm{div}_{x}\Big]u_{\varepsilon}=\frac{1}{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}})}(j_{f_{\varepsilon}}-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}}u_{\varepsilon})&,\\[5.69054pt]
{f_{\varepsilon}}_{\mid t=0}=f^{\mathrm{in}},\ \ {\varrho_{\varepsilon}}_{\mid t=0}=\varrho^{\mathrm{in}},\ \ {u_{\varepsilon}}_{\mid t=0}=u^{\mathrm{in}},\end{aligned}\right.

where

\displaystyle\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}}(t,x):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f_{\varepsilon}(t,x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v,\ \ j_{f_{\varepsilon}}(t,x):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f_{\varepsilon}(t,x,v)v\,\mathrm{d}v.

Let us highlight that we have used the rewriting of the transport equation on
\varrho_{\varepsilon}
based on Lemma
2.2
.

Definition 2.11
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, we define the regularized kinetic transport operator
\mathcal{T}_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon}}
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon}}:=\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}-v\cdot\nabla_{v}+E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon}}(t,x)\cdot\nabla_{v}-d\mathrm{Id},

where

\displaystyle E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon}}(t,x):=u_{\varepsilon}(t,x)-p^{\prime}(\varrho_{\varepsilon})\nabla_{x}\left[\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\varrho_{\varepsilon}\right](t,x),\ \ \ \mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}:=(I-\varepsilon^{2}\Delta_{x})^{-1}.

Remark 2.12
.

The regularization through the operator
\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}
could appear as quite arbitrary. In view of the equivalent Penrose condition (
1.4
) appearing in Remark
1.9
, it is actually possible to consider a more general Fourier multiplier

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{\varepsilon}=m(\varepsilon D),

associated to a smooth function
m:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow(0,1]
such that

\displaystyle\forall k\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\ \ m(k)\leq\frac{C}{(1+|k|^{2}}.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, the Vlasov equation satisfied by
f
in
(

S
ε

)
can be recasted as

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon}}f=0.

Relying on the elliptic regularity provided by
\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}
, we now have the following estimates for the regularized force field
E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon}}
.

Lemma 2.13
.

Let
\varepsilon>0
and
T>0
. If
\varrho\geq c>0
on
[0,T]
, then for all
k>3+d/2
and
t\in[0,T]

(2.4)

\displaystyle\|E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon}}(t)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{k}}\lesssim\|u(t)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{k}}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Lambda\left(\|\varrho(t)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{k-2}}\right)\|\varrho(t)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{k}}.

Thanks to the regularization, we can overcome the loss of derivative exhibited by the estimate of Proposition
2.10
, up to some factor which is diverging when
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
.

Proposition 2.14
.

For all
r\geq 0
,
m>3+d/2
,
c>0
, there exists
C>0
such that for all
\varepsilon>0
,
T>0
and all smooth functions
(f,\varrho,u)
satisfying

\mathcal{T}_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}(f)=0\quad\text{on}\quad[0,T],

and
\varrho\geq c
on
[0,T]
, there holds, for all
t\in[0,T]

\displaystyle\|f(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r}}^{2}\leq\|f(0)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r}}^{2}\exp\left[C\left((1+\|u\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})})T+\frac{\sqrt{T}}{\varepsilon}\Lambda\left(\|\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-2})}\right)\|\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})}\right)\right].

Proof.

The proof is the same as for Proposition
2.10
, using (
2.4
) instead of (
2.2
) to conclude.
∎

We shall also need the following condition about the pointwise bounds for the densities.

Definition 2.15
.

Let
T>0
. For any nonnegative functions
f(t,x,v)
and
\varrho(t,x)
on
[0,T]
, we define the property

(B
{}^{\mu,\theta}_{\Theta}(T)
)

\displaystyle\forall t\in[0,T],\ \ \rho_{f}(t)\leq\frac{\Theta+1}{2},\ \ \ \frac{\mu}{2}\leq\varrho(t),\ \ \ \frac{\underline{\theta}}{2}\leq(1-\rho_{f}(t))\varrho(t)\leq 2\overline{\theta},

where
\Theta,\mu,\underline{\theta},\overline{\theta}
are given in the statement of Theorem
1.5
.

We will be able to propagate the condition (
B

(
T
)

Θ

μ
,
θ

) thanks to the following lemmas, giving some rough pointwise control for the local particle density and the fluid density.

Lemma 2.16
.

Assume that
\rho_{f^{\mathrm{in}}}<\Theta
. For any
\ell,r>1+d/2
, any smooth solution
f(t,x,v)
to the Vlasov equation in (

S
ε

) satisfies for all
T>0

\displaystyle\|\rho_{f}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Theta+T\|f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{\ell}_{r})},

\displaystyle\|1-\rho_{f}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Theta+T\|f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{\ell}_{r})},

where
\Theta
has been introduced in the statement of Theorem
1.5
. Furthermore, if
\Theta+T\|f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{\ell}_{r})}<1
, then for all
t\in[0,T]
and
x\in\mathbb{T}^{d}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\rho_{f}(t,x)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{1-\Theta-T\|f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{\ell}_{r})}},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\rho_{f}(t,x)}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\frac{1}{\Theta+T\|f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{\ell}_{r})}}.

Proof.

Integrating the Vlasov equation with respect to velocity, one gets the conservation law
\partial_{t}\rho_{f}+\mathrm{div}_{x}\,j_{f}=0
. We thus have

\displaystyle\rho_{f}(t)=\rho_{f^{\mathrm{in}}}+\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{div}_{x}(j_{f})(s)\,\mathrm{d}s,

therefore by using Sobolev embedding, we get for all
t\in[0,T]

\displaystyle\|\rho_{f}(t)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\leq\|\rho_{f^{\mathrm{in}}}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}+\int_{0}^{t}\|\mathrm{div}_{x}(j_{f})(s)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq\Theta+T\|j_{f}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{\ell}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))},

if
\ell>1+d/2
. We obtain the conclusion by using Lemma
2.1
. The last estimates stated in the lemma directly follow.
∎

Lemma 2.17
.

Assume that
0<\underline{\theta}\leq(1-\rho_{f^{\mathrm{in}}})\varrho^{\mathrm{in}}\leq\overline{\theta}
. Let
T>0
. For
\ell>1+d/2
, any smooth solution
(f(t,x,v),\varrho(t,x),u(t,x))
to (

S
ε

) satisfies for all
t\in[0,T]

\displaystyle\underline{\theta}\exp\left(-T\left\|u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{\ell})}\right)\leq(1-\rho_{f}(t))\varrho(t)\leq\overline{\theta}\exp\left(T\left\|u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{\ell})}\right),

where
\underline{\theta},\overline{\theta}
have been introduced in the statement of Theorem
1.5
.

Proof.

The proof is a straighforward application of the method of characteristics applied to
(1-\rho_{f})\varrho
, as the solution to the continuity equation

\displaystyle\partial_{t}((1-\rho_{f})\varrho)+\mathrm{div}_{x}\,((1-\rho_{f})\varrho u)=0.

We obtain the conclusion in view of the assumption on
(1-\rho_{f^{\mathrm{in}}})\varrho^{\mathrm{in}}
.
∎

The following proposition then shows that, thanks to the regularization, we can actually build for all
\varepsilon>0
a local solution to the regularized system (

S
ε

).

Proposition 2.18
.

There exist
r_{0}>0
and
m_{0}>0
such that the following holds. For all
\varepsilon>0
, the system (

S
ε

) is locally well-posed in Sobolev spaces, that is if
r>r_{0}
,
m>m_{0}
and if

\displaystyle(f^{\mathrm{in}},\varrho^{\mathrm{in}},u^{\mathrm{in}})\in\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r}\times\mathrm{H}^{m}\times\mathrm{H}^{m},

satisfies

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq f^{\mathrm{in}},\ \ \ \rho_{f^{\mathrm{in}}}<\Theta<1,\ \ \ 0<\mu\leq\varrho^{\mathrm{in}},\ \ \ 0<\underline{\theta}\leq(1-\rho_{f^{\mathrm{in}}})\varrho^{\mathrm{in}}\leq\overline{\theta},

for some fixed constants
\Theta,\mu,\underline{\theta},\overline{\theta}
, then there exist
T=T(\varepsilon)>0
and a unique solution
(f_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon},u_{\varepsilon})
to the regularized system
(

S
ε

)
on
(0,T(\varepsilon)]
such that

(f_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon},u_{\varepsilon})\in\mathscr{C}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r})\times\mathscr{C}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})\times\mathscr{C}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})\cap\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m+1}),

and starting at
(f^{\mathrm{in}},\varrho^{\mathrm{in}},u^{\mathrm{in}})
. Furthermore, the condition (
B

(
T
)

Θ

μ
,
θ

) is satisfied by
(f_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon})
with
T=T(\varepsilon)
.

Proof.

The proof is mainly based on the a priori estimates we have just derived, through a classical approximation procedure. Because of the regularization on the gradient of
\varrho_{\varepsilon}
in the Vlasov equation, the procedure is fairly standard. For the reader’s convenience, we write the proof in Section
B
of the Appendix.
∎

Here ever after and until the end of this article, we consider exponents
r>0
and
m>0
which can be taken large enough. They will be chosen later on, in the end of the proof.

We now introduce the following quantity, in view of the expected result of Theorem
1.5
.

Definition 2.19
.

For any functions
f(t,x,v),\varrho(t,x)
and
u(t,x)
, we set

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{m,r}(f,\varrho,u,T):=\|f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}\left(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{m-1}_{r}\right)}+\|\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})}+\|u\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})\cap\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m+1})},

where
T>0
.

The proof of Theorem
1.5
will rely on a bootstrap argument. Let
\varepsilon>0
. From Proposition
2.18
, consider the maximal time of existence
T^{\ast}_{\varepsilon}
to the system (

S
ε

). By definition, Proposition
2.18
ensures that

\displaystyle\forall T<T^{\ast}_{\varepsilon},\ \ \mathcal{N}_{m,r}(f_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon},u_{\varepsilon},T)<+\infty,\ \ \text{and}\ \ \eqref{def:Bound-HAUTBAS}\ \ \text{holds}.

So we can consider the time

\displaystyle T_{\varepsilon}=T_{\varepsilon}(R):=\sup\big\{T\in[0,T^{\ast}_{\varepsilon}[,\ \ \mathcal{N}_{m,r}(f_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon},u_{\varepsilon},T)\leq R\ \ \text{and}\ \ \eqref{def:Bound-HAUTBAS}\ \ \text{holds}\big\},

where
R>0
will be chosen large enough and independent of
\varepsilon
. By continuity, we observe that
T_{\varepsilon}>0
if
R
is taken large enough and independent of
\varepsilon
. In particular, for all
t\in[0,T_{\varepsilon}]
, we have

(2.5)

\displaystyle 0<\frac{1-\Theta}{2}\leq 1-\rho_{f}(t),\ \ \frac{1}{1-\rho_{f}(t)}\leq\frac{2}{1-\Theta}.

Our main goal is to prove that
R
can be chosen large enough so that there exists
T(R)>0
independent of
\varepsilon
such that

\displaystyle\forall\varepsilon\in(0,1),\ \ T(R)\leq T_{\varepsilon}.

Such a lower bound independent of
\varepsilon
will pave the way for a compactness argument when
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
, leading to the existence of a solution for (
TS
) on
[0,T(R)]
. In what follows, we will work on the interval of time
[0,T_{\varepsilon}]
.

We have the following trichotomy:

•

either
T_{\varepsilon}^{\ast}=+\infty
and
T_{\varepsilon}=T_{\varepsilon}^{\ast}
, then there is nothing to do because
\mathcal{N}_{m,r}(f_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon},u_{\varepsilon},T)\leq R
for all times
T>0
;

•

either
T_{\varepsilon}^{\ast}<+\infty
and
T_{\varepsilon}=T_{\varepsilon}^{\ast}
, we shall see soon enough that this is impossible at this leads to a contradiction;

•

else,
T_{\varepsilon}<T_{\varepsilon}^{\ast}
and
\mathcal{N}_{m,r}(f_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon},u_{\varepsilon},T_{\varepsilon})=R
.

Let us show how to exclude the second case. We need the following lemma.

Lemma 2.20
.

Let
\varepsilon>0
. If
m>3+d/2
and
r\geq 1+d/2
, we have for all

T\in[0,T_{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle\|\varrho_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{\infty})}\lesssim\|\varrho_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-2})}\leq\Lambda\left(T,R,\|\varrho^{\mathrm{in}}\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m-2}},\right).

Proof.

From Proposition
2.3
, we know that for all
T\in[0,T_{\varepsilon})
and
t\in[0,T]

\displaystyle\|\varrho_{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m-2}}\leq\|\varrho^{\mathrm{in}}\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m-2}}e^{C_{m-2}(T,u_{\varepsilon},f_{\varepsilon})T}\exp\left[Te^{C_{m-2}(T,u_{\varepsilon},f_{\varepsilon})T}\mathcal{Q}_{m-2}(T,u_{\varepsilon},f_{\varepsilon})\right],

provided that
m-2,r>1+d/2
, and where

\displaystyle C_{m-2}(T,u_{\varepsilon},f_{\varepsilon})\leq R+2R^{2}\left\|\frac{1}{1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{\infty})},\ \ \mathcal{Q}_{m-2}(T,u_{\varepsilon},f_{\varepsilon})\leq R+2R^{2}\left\|\frac{1}{1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-2})}.

because
\mathcal{N}_{m,r}(f_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon},u_{\varepsilon},T)\leq R
for all
T\in[0,T_{\varepsilon})
.
By Sobolev embedding and the bound (
2.5
), this means that for
r>1+d/2
and
m>1+d/2+2
, we have for all
T\in[0,T_{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle\|\varrho_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{\infty})}\lesssim\|\varrho_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-2})}\leq\Lambda\left(T,R,\|\varrho^{\mathrm{in}}\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m-2}},\left\|\frac{1}{1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-2})}\right).

To conclude, we only have to understand the last term in the previous function
\Lambda
: by Lemma
A.5
, there exists a continuous nonnegative nondecreasing function
\mathrm{C}_{m}
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-2})}

\displaystyle\leq 1+\mathrm{C}_{m}\left(\left\|\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{\infty})}\right)\left\|\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-2})}\lesssim\Lambda\left(R\right),

thanks to Lemma
2.1
and the fact that
\mathcal{N}_{m,r}(f_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon},u_{\varepsilon},T)\leq R
. This concludes the proof.
∎

Remark 2.21
.

A careful inspection of the proof of Proposition
2.3
reveals that for all
k\leq m-2
with
k>3+d/2
,
r>1+d/2
and

T\in[0,T_{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle\|\varrho_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{k})}\lesssim\Lambda(\|\varrho^{\mathrm{in}}\|_{\mathrm{H}^{k}})+T\Lambda\left(T,R\right).

As a corollary, we can now exclude the second case written above: if
T_{\varepsilon}^{\ast}<+\infty
and
T_{\varepsilon}=T_{\varepsilon}^{\ast}
, then according to Proposition
2.14
and Lemma
2.20
,

\displaystyle\underset{t\in[0,T_{\varepsilon}^{\ast})}{\sup}\,\|f_{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r}}^{2}\leq\|f^{\mathrm{in}}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r}}^{2}\exp\left[C\left((1+R)T_{\varepsilon}+\frac{\sqrt{T_{\varepsilon}}}{\varepsilon}\Lambda\left(T,R,\|\varrho^{\mathrm{in}}\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m-2}}\right)\right)\right]<+\infty.

The previous inequality means that the solution could be continued beyond
T_{\varepsilon}^{\ast}
which is impossible by maximality of
T_{\varepsilon}^{\ast}
. This case is thus impossible.

From now on, we assume that
T_{\varepsilon}<T_{\varepsilon}^{\ast}
and
\mathcal{N}_{m,r}(f_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon},u_{\varepsilon},T_{\varepsilon})=R
. In view of our bootstrap stategy, we need to estimate
\mathcal{N}_{m,r}(f_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon},u_{\varepsilon},T)
for all
T<T_{\varepsilon}
.

In the end of this section, we show that the terms
\|f_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}\left(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{m-1}_{r}\right)}
and
\|u_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})\cap\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m+1})}
can be handled by energy estimates. The main part of the upcoming analysis will be to provide a uniform control in
\varepsilon
for the term
\|\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\left(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r}\right)}
.

In the following lemma, we give an estimate independent of
\varepsilon
for the term
\|f_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}\left(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{m-1}_{r}\right)}
in
\mathcal{N}_{m,r}(f_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon},u_{\varepsilon},T)
, for all
T<T_{\varepsilon}
.

Lemma 2.22
.

For
m>1+d/2+2
and
r>1+d/2
, the solution
(f_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon},u_{\varepsilon})
to (

S
ε

) satisfies for all

T\in[0,T_{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle\|f_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}\left(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{m-1}_{r}\right)}\leq\|f^{\mathrm{in}}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m-1}_{r}}+T^{\frac{1}{4}}\Lambda\left(T,R\right).

Proof.

Following the same steps leading to (
2.3
) in the proof of Proposition
2.10
, we have for all
t\in[0,T_{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|f_{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m-1}_{r}}^{2}\lesssim(1+\|E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon}}(t)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m-1}})\|f_{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m-1}_{r}}^{2},

since
m-1>d/2
, therefore

\displaystyle\|f_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}\left(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{m-1}_{r}\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|f^{\mathrm{in}}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m-1}_{r}}^{2}+\|f_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}\left(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{m-1}_{r}\right)}^{2}\Bigg(T+\int_{0}^{T}\|E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon}}(s)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m-1}}\,\mathrm{d}s\Bigg).

Using now the estimate (
2.2
) from Lemma
2.7
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|\mathrm{E}_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon}}(s)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m-1}}\,\mathrm{d}s\lesssim\sqrt{T}\|u_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-1})}+\sqrt{T}\Lambda\left(\|\varrho_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-2})}\right)\|\varrho_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})}.

We thus infer that for all
T\in[0,T_{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle\|f_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}\left(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{m-1}_{r}\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|f^{\mathrm{in}}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m-1}_{r}}^{2}+C\|f_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}\left(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{m-1}_{r}\right)}^{2}\Bigg(T+\sqrt{T}\|u_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-1})}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+\sqrt{T}\Lambda\left(\|\varrho_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-2})}\right)\|\varrho_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})}\Bigg),

where
C>0
is independent of
\varepsilon
. Since
\mathcal{N}_{m,r}(f_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon},u_{\varepsilon},T)\leq R
for all
T\in[0,T_{\varepsilon})
,
this gives

\displaystyle\|f_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}\left(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{m-1}_{r}\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|f^{\mathrm{in}}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m-1}_{r}}^{2}+CR^{2}\Bigg(T+\sqrt{T}R+\sqrt{T}\Lambda\left(\|\varrho_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-2})}\right)R\Bigg).

To obtain a uniform bound in
\varepsilon
for the term
\Lambda\left(\|\varrho_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-2})}\right)
,
we use Lemma
2.20
, leading to the conclusion of the lemma.
∎

We conclude this section with an estimate for the term
\|u_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})\cap\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m+1})}
in
\mathcal{N}(f_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon},u_{\varepsilon},T)
. To this end we will crucially rely on the smoothing provided by the differential operator
\Delta_{x}+\nabla_{x}\mathrm{div}_{x}
from the Navier-Stokes equation on
u_{\varepsilon}
. Indeed, we have the following lemma.

Lemma 2.23
.

The differential operator
-\Delta_{x}-\nabla_{x}\mathrm{div}_{x}
is elliptic.

Proof.

The operator
-\Delta_{x}-\nabla_{x}\mathrm{div}_{x}
is associated to the matrix Fourier multiplier
L(k)=|k|^{2}\mathrm{I}_{d}+k\otimes k
(
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
). One can then prove (see e.g.
[
DT22
]
) that

\displaystyle 2^{-3\frac{d}{2}}|k|^{2d}\leq|\mathrm{det}\,L(k)|,

which yields the desired ellipticity.
∎

We are now able to prove the following proposition.

Proposition 2.24
.

For
m>2+d/2
and
r\geq 0
, for all
\varepsilon>0
, the solution
(f_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon},u_{\varepsilon})
to (

S
ε

) satisfies for all
T\in[0,T_{\varepsilon})

\|u_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})}+\|u_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m+1})}\\
\lesssim\left(1+T^{1/2}\Lambda(T,R)\right)\left(\|u^{\mathrm{in}}\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}+T^{1/2}\Lambda(T,R)+\Lambda(T,R)\|\varrho_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})}\right).

Proof.

First, we rewrite the equation on
u_{\varepsilon}
as

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u_{\varepsilon}-\sigma\big((1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}})\varrho_{\varepsilon}\big)\Big(\Delta_{x}+\nabla_{x}\mathrm{div}_{x}\Big)u_{\varepsilon}=F,

with

F:=-(u_{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{x})u_{\varepsilon}-\nabla_{x}\pi(\varrho_{\varepsilon})+\sigma\big((1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}})\varrho_{\varepsilon}\big)(j_{f_{\varepsilon}}-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}}u_{\varepsilon}),

where

\sigma(s):=\frac{1}{s},\,\pi(s)=\int_{0}^{s}\frac{p^{\prime}(\tau)}{\tau}\,\mathrm{d}\tau
. We then apply
\partial_{x}^{\beta}
in the equation for
|\beta|\leq m-1
, which gives

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\partial^{\beta}_{x}u_{\varepsilon})-\sigma\big((1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}})\varrho_{\varepsilon}\big)\Big(\Delta_{x}+\nabla_{x}\mathrm{div}_{x}\Big)(\partial^{\beta}_{x}u_{\varepsilon})=\partial^{\beta}_{x}F+\Big[\partial^{\beta}_{x},\sigma\big((1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}})\varrho_{\varepsilon}\big)\Big]((\Delta_{x}+\nabla_{x}\mathrm{div}_{x})u_{\varepsilon}),

and then multiply the equation with
-\Big(\Delta_{x}+\nabla_{x}\mathrm{div}_{x}\Big)(\partial^{\beta}_{x}u)
so that, by integrating on
\mathbb{T}^{d}
and with an integration by parts:

\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\left(|\nabla_{x}\partial^{\beta}_{x}u_{\varepsilon}|^{2}+|\mathrm{div}_{x}\partial^{\beta}_{x}u_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\right)\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\sigma\big((1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}})\varrho_{\varepsilon}\big)|\Big(\Delta_{x}+\nabla_{x}\mathrm{div}_{x}\Big)(\partial^{\beta}_{x}u_{\varepsilon})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\\
=-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\Big(\Delta_{x}+\nabla_{x}\mathrm{div}_{x}\Big)(\partial^{\beta}_{x}u_{\varepsilon})\cdot\partial_{x}^{\beta}F\,\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\Big[\partial^{\beta}_{x},\sigma\big((1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}})\varrho_{\varepsilon}\big)\Big]((\Delta_{x}+\nabla_{x}\mathrm{div}_{x})u_{\varepsilon})\cdot(\Delta_{x}+\nabla_{x}\mathrm{div}_{x})(\partial^{\beta}_{x}u_{\varepsilon})\,\mathrm{d}x.

Thanks to the Cauchy-Schwarz and Young inequalities, and after integration in time, we get for all
\eta>0
and
t\in(0,T)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}|\nabla_{x}\partial^{\beta}_{x}u_{\varepsilon}(t)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\left(\sigma\big((1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}})\varrho_{\varepsilon}\big)-\eta\right)|(\Delta_{x}+\nabla_{x}\mathrm{div}_{x})\partial_{x}^{\beta}u_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\left(|\nabla_{x}\partial^{\beta}_{x}u^{\mathrm{in}}|^{2}+|\mathrm{div}_{x}\partial^{\beta}_{x}u^{\mathrm{in}}|^{2}\right)\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{2\eta}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}|\partial_{x}^{\beta}F|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s+\frac{1}{2\eta}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\left|\Big[\partial^{\beta}_{x},\sigma\big((1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}})\varrho_{\varepsilon}\big)\Big]((\Delta_{x}+\nabla_{x}\mathrm{div}_{x})u_{\varepsilon})\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s.

Let us deal with the last term: by the commutator inequality from Proposition
A.1
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\left|\Big[\partial^{\beta}_{x},\sigma\big((1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}})\varrho_{\varepsilon}\big)\Big]((\Delta_{x}+\nabla_{x}\mathrm{div}_{x})u_{\varepsilon})\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq M\left(\left\|\nabla_{x}\sigma\big((1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}})\varrho_{\varepsilon}\big)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\left\|(\Delta_{x}+\nabla_{x}\mathrm{div}_{x})u_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m-2}}^{2}+\left\|\sigma\big((1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}})\varrho_{\varepsilon}\big)\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m-1}}^{2}\left\|(\Delta_{x}+\nabla_{x}\mathrm{div}_{x})u_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq M\left(\left\|\sigma^{\prime}\big((1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}})\varrho_{\varepsilon}\big)\nabla_{x}((1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}})\varrho_{\varepsilon})\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\left\|u\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}^{2}+\left\|\sigma\big((1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}})\varrho_{\varepsilon}\big)\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m-1}}^{2}\left\|(\Delta_{x}+\nabla_{x}\mathrm{div}_{x})u_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\right),

for some constant
M>0
independent of time. Combining the Sobolev embedding (with
m>2+d/2
) and Remark
A.4
, we get

\frac{1}{2\eta}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\left|\Big[\partial^{\beta}_{x},\sigma\big((1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}})\varrho_{\varepsilon}\big)\Big]((\Delta_{x}+\nabla_{x}\mathrm{div}_{x})u_{\varepsilon})\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s\\
\leq M\frac{1}{2\eta}\Lambda\left(\left\|(1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}})\varrho_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-1})}\right)\int_{0}^{t}\left\|u_{\varepsilon}(\tau)\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\tau,

for another constant
M>0
. Note that by Remark
2.5
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|(1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}})\varrho_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-1})}\leq\Lambda(T,R,\|u_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})})\leq\Lambda(T,R).

All in all, we get for all
\eta>0
and
t\in(0,T)

\frac{1}{2}\left\|\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\beta}u_{\varepsilon}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\left(\sigma\big((1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}})\varrho_{\varepsilon}\big)-\eta\right)|(\Delta_{x}+\nabla_{x}\mathrm{div}_{x})\partial_{x}^{\beta}u_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s\\
\leq\|u^{\mathrm{in}}\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}^{2}+\frac{1}{\eta}\|F\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-1})}^{2}+M\frac{\Lambda(T,R)}{2\eta}\int_{0}^{t}\left\|u_{\varepsilon}(\tau)\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

Thanks to the condition (
B

(
T
)

Θ

μ
,
θ

), we can choose
\eta=1/4\overline{\theta}
so that

\displaystyle\forall(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{T}^{d},\ \ \frac{1}{4\overline{\theta}}<\sigma\big((1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}})\varrho_{\varepsilon}\big)(t,x)-\eta.

Summing for all
|\beta|=m
and invoking the elliptic regularity for the operator
-\Delta_{x}-\nabla_{x}\mathrm{div}_{x}
given by Lemma
2.23
, we get for all
t\in(0,T)

\displaystyle\left\|u_{\varepsilon}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}^{2}\leq\left\|u_{\varepsilon}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}^{2}+\|u_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m+1})}^{2}\lesssim\|u^{\mathrm{in}}\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}^{2}+\|F\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-1})}^{2}+\Lambda(T,R)\int_{0}^{t}\left\|u_{\varepsilon}(\tau)\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

By Grönwall’s lemma, we deduce for all
t\in(0,T)

\displaystyle\left\|u_{\varepsilon}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}^{2}\leq\Lambda(T,R)\left(\|u^{\mathrm{in}}\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}^{2}+\|F\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-1})}^{2}\right),

which then implies, by using again the previous inequality, that for all
t\in(0,T)

\displaystyle\left\|u_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})}^{2}+\|u_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m+1})}^{2}\lesssim(1+T\Lambda(T,R))\left(\|u^{\mathrm{in}}\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}^{2}+\|F\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-1})}^{2}\right).

To conclude, let us now estimate the norm of the source term
F
. We have

\displaystyle\|F\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-1})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\Bigg(\|(u_{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{x})u_{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m-1}}^{2}+\|\nabla_{x}\pi(\varrho_{\varepsilon}(\tau))\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m-1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\left\|\frac{1}{(1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}})\varrho_{\varepsilon}}(j_{f_{\varepsilon}}-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}}u_{\varepsilon})(\tau)\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m-1}}^{2}\Bigg)\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\left(\|u_{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m-1}}^{2}\|u_{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}^{2}+\|\pi(\varrho_{\varepsilon}(\tau))\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}^{2}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+T\left\|\frac{1}{(1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}})\varrho_{\varepsilon}}(j_{f_{\varepsilon}}-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}}u_{\varepsilon})\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-1})}^{2}.

In the rest of the proof, we shall make a constant use of the condition (
B

(
T
)

Θ

μ
,
θ

).
By Proposition
A.3
in the Appendix, we have

\displaystyle\|\pi(\varrho_{\varepsilon})\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda(\|\varrho_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}})\|\varrho_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}},

from which we infer thanks to Sobolev embedding (taking
m>2+\frac{d}{2}
)

\displaystyle\|F\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-1})}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim T\left\|u_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})}^{4}+\Lambda(\|\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{\infty})})\|\varrho_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})}^{2}+T\left\|\frac{1}{(1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}})\varrho_{\varepsilon}}(j_{f_{\varepsilon}}-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}}u_{\varepsilon})\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-1})}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim TR^{4}+\Lambda(\|\varrho_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-2})})\|\varrho_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})}^{2}+T\left\|\frac{1}{(1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}})\varrho_{\varepsilon}}(j_{f_{\varepsilon}}-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}}u_{\varepsilon})\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-1})}^{2},

since
\mathcal{N}_{m,r}(f_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon},u_{\varepsilon},T)\leq R
for all
T\in[0,T_{\varepsilon})
. The first term is then adressed thanks to Lemma
2.20
and it remains to estimate the last term. For this one, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{(1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}})\varrho_{\varepsilon}}(j_{f_{\varepsilon}}-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}}u_{\varepsilon})\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-1})}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{1}{(1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}})\varrho_{\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-1})}\left\|(j_{f_{\varepsilon}}-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}}u_{\varepsilon})\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-1})}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda(T,R)\left\|\frac{1}{(1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}})\varrho_{\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-1})},

thanks to Lemma
2.1
and
\mathcal{N}_{m,r}(f_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon},u_{\varepsilon},T)\leq R
for all
T\in[0,T_{\varepsilon})
. By Remark
A.4
, we then have by Sobolev embedding

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{(1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}})\varrho_{\varepsilon}}(j_{f}-\rho_{f}u)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-1})}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda(T,R,\|(1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}})\varrho_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{\infty})})\|(1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}})\varrho_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-1})}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda(T,R,\|(1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}})\varrho_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-1})})

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda(T,R,\|u_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})})

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda(T,R),

where we have also used Remark
2.5
. This eventually concludes the proof.

∎

Remark 2.25
.

By looking at the previous proof, we have for

T\in[0,T_{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle\|u_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{k})}^{2}+\|u\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{k+1})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|u^{\mathrm{in}}\|_{\mathrm{H}^{k}}^{2}+T\Lambda(T,R)+\Lambda(T,R)\|\varrho_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{k})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|u^{\mathrm{in}}\|_{\mathrm{H}^{k}}^{2}+T\Lambda(T,R)+T\Lambda(T,R)\|\varrho_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{{\infty}}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{k})}^{2},

for all
k>2+d/2
such that
k\leq m-2
, therefore by Remark
2.21
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|u_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{k})}+\|u_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{k+1})}\lesssim\left(1+T^{1/2}\Lambda(T,R)\right)\left(\|u^{\mathrm{in}}\|_{\mathrm{H}^{k}}+\|\varrho^{\mathrm{in}}\|_{\mathrm{H}^{k}}\right)+T^{1/2}\Lambda(T,R).

So far, Lemma
2.22
and Proposition
2.24
show that it remains to control the second term in
\mathcal{N}(f_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon},u_{\varepsilon},T)
, that is
\|\varrho_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})}
, to perform a bootstrap argument. This will constitute the heart of our analysis and will be the purpose of the remaining sections.

3.
Trajectories and straightening change of variable

In this short section, we study the trajectories associated to a Vlasov equation with friction and force field
\mathrm{F}(t,x)
.
We show that for small times, their geometry can be simplified thanks to a straightening change of variable in velocity. Loosely speaking, this allows to boil down the dynamics to that associated with free-transport with friction. This procedure will be useful in Section
5
.

Let
T>0
. Given
\mathrm{F}(s,x)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
a given vector field defined on
[0,T]\times\mathbb{T}^{d}
and satisfying

\displaystyle\mathrm{F}\in\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{W}^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d})),

we can consider, thanks to the Cauchy-Lipschitz theorem, the solution
s\mapsto\left(\mathrm{X}^{s;t}(x,v),\mathrm{V}^{s;t}(x,v)\right)\in\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
of the following system of ODE:

\left\{\begin{aligned} \frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}\mathrm{X}^{s;t}(x,v)&=\mathrm{V}^{s;t}(x,v),\\
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}\mathrm{V}^{s;t}(x,v)&=-\mathrm{V}^{s;t}(x,v)+\mathrm{F}(s,\mathrm{X}^{s;t}(x,v)),\\
\mathrm{X}^{t;t}(x,v)&=x,\\
\mathrm{V}^{t;t}(x,v)&=v.\end{aligned}\right.

Later on, we will apply this to
\mathrm{F}=E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon}}
, which has been defined in the beginning of Section
2.2
(see the end of the current section). Integrating the previous system of ODE, we have

(3.1)

\displaystyle\mathrm{X}^{s;t}(x,v)

\displaystyle=x+(1-e^{t-s})v+\int_{t}^{s}(1-e^{\tau-s})\mathrm{F}\left(\tau,\mathrm{X}^{\tau;t}(x,v)\right)\,\mathrm{d}\tau,

(3.2)

\displaystyle\mathrm{V}^{s;t}(x,v)

\displaystyle=e^{t-s}v+\int_{t}^{s}e^{\tau-s}\mathrm{F}\left(\tau,\mathrm{X}^{\tau;t}(x,v)\right)\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

Considering the full kinetic transport operator

\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{F}}=\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}-v\cdot\nabla_{v}+\mathrm{F}(t,x)\cdot\nabla_{v}-d\mathrm{Id},

the method of characteristics shows that a smooth function
f(t,x,v)
satisfying

\left\{\begin{aligned} \mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{F}}f=0,\\[5.69054pt]
f_{\mid t=0}=f^{\mathrm{in}},\end{aligned}\right.

can be represented as

\displaystyle f(t,x,v)=e^{dt}f^{\mathrm{in}}\left(\mathrm{X}^{0;t}(x,v),\mathrm{V}^{0;t}(x,v)\right).

Note also that for all
t,s\in[0,T]
, the map

\displaystyle(x,v)\mapsto\left(\mathrm{X}^{s;t}(x,v),\mathrm{V}^{s;t}(x,v)\right)

is a diffeomorphism from
\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
to itself, which Jacobian value is
e^{d(s-t)}
.

The main goal of this section is to prove that for short times, and modulo a
straightening change of variable
in velocity, it is possible to come down to the free dynamics with friction associated to the transport operator

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{fric}}=\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}-v\cdot\nabla_{v}-d\mathrm{Id}.

This corresponds to the previous system of ODE with
\mathrm{F}=0
, and for which the solution
(\mathrm{X}^{s;t}_{\mathrm{fric}},\mathrm{V}^{s;t}_{\mathrm{fric}})
is

\displaystyle\mathrm{X}^{s;t}_{\mathrm{fric}}=x+(1-e^{t-s})v,\ \ \mathrm{V}^{s;t}_{\mathrm{fric}}=e^{t-s}v.

Namely, we have the following lemma.

Lemma 3.1
.

Let
T>0
and
k\geq 1
. Let
\mathrm{F}\in\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{W}^{k,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))
be a vector field such that

\displaystyle\|\mathrm{F}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{W}^{k,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}\leq\Lambda(T,\mathrm{R}),

for some
\mathrm{R}>0
.
There exists
\overline{T}(\mathrm{R})>0
such that for all
x\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
and
s,t\in[0,\min(\overline{T}(\mathrm{R}),T)]
, there exists a diffeomorphism
\psi_{s,t}(x,\cdot):\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}
satisfying for all
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

\displaystyle\mathrm{X}^{s;t}\left(x,\psi_{s,t}(x,v)\right)=x+(1-e^{t-s})v,

which furthermore verifies the estimates

(3.3)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{C}\leq\mathrm{det}\left(\mathrm{D}_{v}\psi_{s,t}(x,v)\right)

\displaystyle\leq C,

(3.4)

\displaystyle\underset{s,t\in[0,T]}{\sup}\,\left\|\partial_{x,v}^{\beta}\left(\psi_{s,t}(x,v)-v\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq\varphi(T)\Lambda(T,\mathrm{R}),\ \ |\beta|\leq k,

(3.5)

\displaystyle\underset{s,t\in[0,T]}{\sup}\,\left\|\partial_{x,v}^{\beta}\partial_{s}\psi_{s,t}(x,v)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq\varphi(T)\Lambda(T,\mathrm{R}),\ \ |\beta|\leq k-1,

for some
C>0
and some nondecreasing continuous function
\varphi:\mathbb{R}^{+}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
vanishing at
0
.

Proof.

We follow the approach of
[
HKNR21
]
. We observe that if we set

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathrm{X}}^{s;t}(x,v):=\dfrac{1}{1-e^{t-s}}\left[\mathrm{X}^{s;t}(x,v)-x-(1-e^{t-s})v\right],

it comes down to prove that for
s,t
small enough, the mapping
\phi^{s,t,x}:v\mapsto v+\widetilde{\mathrm{X}}^{s;t}(x,v)
is a small Lipschitz perturbation of the identity: denoting its inverse
\psi_{s,t}(x,\cdot)
, it will satisfy

(3.6)

\displaystyle v=\psi_{s,t}(x,v)+\widetilde{\mathrm{X}}^{s,t}(x,\psi_{s,t}(x,v)),

and the first conclusion of the lemma will follow. We introduce the remainder

\displaystyle\mathrm{Y}^{s;t}(x,v)=\mathrm{X}^{s;t}(x,v)-x-(1-e^{t-s})v,

which, in view of (
3.1
), satisfies for all
s,t\in[0,T]

\displaystyle\mathrm{Y}^{s;t}(x,v)

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{t}(e^{\tau-s}-1)\mathrm{F}\left(\tau,\mathrm{X}^{\tau;t}(x,v)\right)\,\mathrm{d}\tau

(3.7)

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{t}(e^{\tau-s}-1)\mathrm{F}\left(\tau,x+(1-e^{t-\tau})v+\mathrm{Y}^{\tau;t}(x,v)\right)\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

We now have

(3.8)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathrm{X}}^{s;t}(x,v)=\frac{1}{e^{t-s}-1}\int_{s}^{t}(e^{\tau-s}-1)\mathrm{F}\left(\tau,x+(1-e^{t-\tau})v+\mathrm{Y}^{\tau;t}(x,v)\right)\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

Thus, estimates on
\mathrm{Y}
and its derivatives obtained thanks to (
3
) shall provide estimates on
\widetilde{\mathrm{X}}
and its derivatives via (
3.8
).

Let us assume that
s\leq t
(the case
s\geq t
can be treated similarly). First, we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}\mathrm{Y}^{s;t}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{s}^{t}(e^{\tau-s}-1)\|\nabla_{x}\mathrm{F}\left(\tau,x+(1-e^{t-\tau})v+\mathrm{Y}^{\tau;t}(x,v)\right)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{x,v}}(1+\|\nabla_{x}\mathrm{Y}^{\tau;t}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{x,v}})\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\leq\int_{s}^{t}(e^{\tau-s}-1)\|\nabla_{x}\mathrm{F}(\tau)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{x}}(1+\|\nabla_{x}\mathrm{Y}^{\tau;t}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{x,v}})\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\leq(e^{T}-1)T^{1/2}\|\nabla_{x}\mathrm{F}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{\infty})}\left(1+\underset{\tau\leq t}{\sup}\,\|\nabla_{x}\mathrm{Y}^{\tau;t}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{x,v}}\right).

By the assumption on the vector field
\mathrm{F}
, we get

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}\mathrm{Y}^{s;t}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq(e^{T}-1)T^{1/2}\Lambda(T,\mathrm{R})\left(1+\underset{\tau\leq t}{\sup}\,\|\nabla_{x}\mathrm{Y}^{\tau;t}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{x,v}}\right).

In the following of the proof,
\varphi:\mathbb{R}^{+}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
will stand for a generic continuous function, vanishing at
0
, that may change from line to line. This yields

(3.9)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}\mathrm{Y}^{s;t}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq\underset{\tau\leq t}{\sup}\,\|\nabla_{x}\mathrm{Y}^{\tau;t}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq\frac{(e^{T}-1)T^{1/2}\Lambda(T,\mathrm{R})}{1-(e^{T}-1)T^{1/2}\Lambda(T,\mathrm{R})}\lesssim\varphi(T)\Lambda(T,\mathrm{R}),

for
T
small enough. In a similar way, we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{v}\mathrm{Y}^{s;t}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{s}^{t}(e^{\tau-s}-1)\|\nabla_{x}\mathrm{F}(\tau)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{x,v}}(1-e^{t-\tau}+\|\nabla_{v}\mathrm{Y}^{\tau;t}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{x,v}})\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\leq(e^{T}-1)T^{1/2}\Lambda(T,R)\left(1+\underset{\tau\leq t}{\sup}\,\|\nabla_{v}\mathrm{Y}^{\tau;t}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{x,v}}\right),

therefore

(3.10)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{v}\mathrm{Y}^{s;t}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{x,v}}\lesssim\varphi(T)\Lambda(T,\mathrm{R}),

for
T
small enough. We then deduce the following estimates. There holds

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}\widetilde{\mathrm{X}}^{s;t}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{e^{t-s}-1}\int_{s}^{t}(e^{\tau-s}-1)\|\nabla_{x}\mathrm{F}(\tau)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{x,v}}(1+\|\nabla_{x}\mathrm{Y}^{\tau;t}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{x,v}})\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{1/2}\Lambda(T,R)(1+\varphi(T)\Lambda(T,\mathrm{R})),

thanks to (
3.9
), as well as

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{v}\widetilde{\mathrm{X}}^{s;t}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{e^{t-s}-1}\int_{s}^{t}(e^{\tau-s}-1)\|\nabla_{x}\mathrm{F}(\tau)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{x,v}}(1-e^{\tau-t}+\|\nabla_{v}\mathrm{Y}^{\tau;t}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{x,v}})\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{1/2}\Lambda(T,\mathrm{R})(1+\varphi(T)\Lambda(T,\mathrm{R})),

thanks to (
3.10
). We have proven that for
T
small enough, we have

(3.11)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}\widetilde{\mathrm{X}}^{s;t}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{x,v}}+\|\nabla_{v}\widetilde{\mathrm{X}}^{s;t}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq\varphi(T)\Lambda(T,\mathrm{R}).

For
T
small enough, we therefore obtain the existence of the desired diffeomorphism
\psi_{s,t}(x,\cdot)
. We also have

\displaystyle 0<\left|\mathrm{det}\left(\nabla_{v}\psi_{s,t}^{x}(v)\right)\right|=\left|\mathrm{det}\left(\mathrm{Id}+\nabla_{v}\widetilde{\mathrm{X}}^{s,t}(x,\psi_{s,t}^{x}(v))\right)\right|^{-1}.

We thus infer the uniform bound (
3.3
) from the estimate (
3.11
) and the continuity of
M\mapsto|\mathrm{det}(M)|
, reducing
\overline{T}(\mathrm{R})
if necessary.

Let us finally prove the estimates (
3.4
)–(
3.5
). For (
3.4
), we proceed by induction on the length of
\alpha
. In view of (
3.6
), we obtain the result for
|\alpha|=0
.
For the case
|\alpha|=1
, we differentiate the identity (
3.6
) and get, with
\nabla=\nabla_{x}
or
\nabla=\nabla_{v}

\displaystyle\nabla(v-\psi_{s,t}^{x}(v))=\nabla\widetilde{\mathrm{X}}^{s;t}(x,\psi_{s,t}^{x}(v))-\nabla\widetilde{\mathrm{X}}^{s;t}(x,\psi_{s,t}^{x}(v))\nabla(v-\psi_{s,t}^{x}(v)),

therefore thanks to (
3.11
) (reducing again
\overline{T}(\mathrm{R})
if necessary), we have

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\left(\psi_{s,t}^{x}(v)-v\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq\frac{\|\nabla\widetilde{\mathrm{X}}^{s;t}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{x,v}}}{1-\|\nabla\widetilde{\mathrm{X}}^{s;t}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{x,v}}}\leq\varphi(T)\Lambda(T,\mathrm{R}).

This yields the result for
|\alpha|=1
.
If
1<|\alpha|\leq k
and if the result holds for all
|\widetilde{\alpha}|<|\alpha|
, we apply
\partial_{x,v}^{\alpha}
in (
3.6
) and use the Faà di Bruno’s formula:

\displaystyle\partial_{x,v}^{\alpha}\left(\psi_{s,t}^{x}(v)-v\right)=\sum_{\mu,\nu}C_{\mu,\nu}\partial_{x,v}^{\mu}\widetilde{\mathrm{X}}^{s,t}(\mathrm{z}(x,v))\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq|\beta|\leq|\alpha|\\
1\leq j\leq 2d\end{subarray}}(\partial_{x,v}^{\beta}\mathrm{z}(x,v)_{j})^{\nu_{\beta_{j}}},\ \ \mathrm{z}(x,v):=(x,\psi_{s,t}^{x}(v)),

where the sum is taken on
(\mu,\nu)
such that
1\leq|\mu|\leq|\alpha|
and
\nu_{k}\in\mathbb{N}\setminus{\{0\}}
with

\displaystyle\forall 1\leq j\leq 2d,\ \sum_{1\leq|\beta|\leq|\alpha|}\nu_{\beta_{j}}=\mu_{j},\ \ {\text{and}}\ \ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq|\beta|\leq|\alpha|\\
1\leq j\leq 2d\end{subarray}}\nu_{\beta_{j}}\beta=\alpha.

We proceed as in the case
|\alpha|=1
. We isolate the terms with multi-indices
\mu
such that
|\mu|=1
(giving associated
\nu_{\beta_{j}}=1
for all
1\leq j\leq 2d
): the terms
\partial_{x,v}^{\alpha}\psi_{s,t}
(given by
\nu_{\alpha_{j}}=1
) in the product are treated as above, while derivatives of order strictly less than
|\alpha|
are bounded thanks to the induction hypothesis. This procedure is allowed provided that uniform bounds (in time) of the same type for
\|\widetilde{\mathrm{X}}^{s,t}\|_{\mathrm{W}^{k,\infty}_{x,v}}
(
k\leq|\alpha|
) hold true.

Such bounds are obtained by performing the same induction at the level of
\mathrm{Y}^{s;t}
first (using the same principle as before with (
3
))
and then for
\widetilde{\mathrm{X}}^{s,t}
(arguing as before with (
3.8
)).

Concerning the estimate (
3.5
), we have by (
3.6
)

\displaystyle\partial_{s}\psi_{s,t}^{x}(v)=-\partial_{s}\widetilde{\mathrm{X}}^{s,t}(x,\psi_{s,t}^{x}(v))-\nabla_{v}\widetilde{\mathrm{X}}^{s,t}(x,\psi_{s,t}^{x}(v))\partial_{s}\psi_{s,t}^{x}(v),

and by (
3.8
),

\displaystyle\partial_{s}\widetilde{\mathrm{X}}^{s;t}(x,v)=-\frac{e^{t-s}}{1-e^{t-s}}\widetilde{\mathrm{X}}^{s;t}(x,v)-\frac{1}{1-e^{t-s}}\int_{s}^{t}e^{\tau-s}\mathrm{F}\left(\tau,x+(1-e^{t-\tau})v+\mathrm{Y}^{\tau;t}(x,v)\right)\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

Since

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\mathrm{X}}^{s;t}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq T\Lambda(T,\mathrm{R}),

and
\frac{Te^{T}}{e^{T}-1}
is bounded in a neighborhood of
0
, we obtain an estimate on
\|\partial_{s}\widetilde{\mathrm{X}}^{s;t}(x,v)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{x,v}}
and then on
\|\partial_{s}\psi_{s,t}^{x}(v)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{x,v}}
as before. Using the same induction procedure as for (
3.4
), we finally obtain (
3.5
).
∎

Remark 3.2
.

From the
\mathrm{W}^{k,\infty}_{x,v}
-bounds we have obtained on
\mathrm{Y}^{s,t}
along the proof, and because

\displaystyle\mathrm{Y}^{s;t}(x,v)=\mathrm{X}^{s;t}(x,v)-x-(1-e^{t-s})v,

we can infer that

(3.12)

\displaystyle\underset{s,t\in[0,T]}{\sup}\,\left\|\partial_{x,v}^{\beta}\left(\mathrm{X}_{s,t}(x,v)-x-(1-e^{t-s})v\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}_{v})}

\displaystyle\leq\varphi(T)\Lambda(T,R),\ \ |\beta|\leq k.

Likewise, by considering

\displaystyle\mathrm{W}^{s,t}(x,v)=\mathrm{V}^{s,t}(x,v)-e^{t-s}v,

one can obtain the estimate

(3.13)

\displaystyle\underset{s,t\in[0,T]}{\sup}\,\left\|\partial_{x,v}^{\beta}\left(\mathrm{V}_{s,t}(x,v)-e^{t-s}v\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}_{v})}

\displaystyle\leq\varphi(T)\Lambda(T,R),\ \ |\beta|\leq k.

Let us conclude this section by showing that in Lemma
3.1
, one can consider

\displaystyle(T,\mathrm{F})=(T_{\varepsilon},E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon}}),

for a given
\varepsilon>0
, where
(T_{\varepsilon},E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon}})
have been defined in the set-up of the bootstrap argument of Section
2.2
. Let us prove that the assumptions of Lemma
3.1
indeed hold with
k=m-2-d/2
. By the estimate (
2.2
) from Lemma
2.7
, we have for all
t\in(0,T_{\varepsilon})
and
\ell<m-1-d/2

\displaystyle\left\|E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon}}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{W}^{\ell,\infty}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon}}(t)\right\|_{H^{m-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u_{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m-1}}+\Lambda\left(\|\varrho_{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m-2}}\right)\|\varrho_{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}.

Appealing to Lemma
2.20
, and using
\mathcal{N}_{m,r}(f_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon},u_{\varepsilon},T_{\varepsilon})\leq R
for all
T\leq T_{\varepsilon}
, we deduce

\displaystyle\|E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon}}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{W}^{k,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}\leq\Lambda(T,R),

for
k=m-2-d/2
.

4.
Averaging operators related to the dynamics with friction

For any smooth vector field
G(t,s,x,v)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we consider the following integral operator
\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{free}}_{G}
acting on functions
H(t,x)
:

\displaystyle\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}[H](t,x)

\displaystyle:=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}[\nabla_{x}H](s,x-(t-s)v)\cdot G(t,s,x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s.

This operator, featuring an apparent loss of derivative in space, was systematically studied in
[
HKR16
]
. It was proven in
[
HKR16
, Proposition 5.1 and Remark 5.1]
that this loss is only apparent, provided that the kernel is sufficiently smooth and decaying in velocity. The statement goes as follows.

Proposition 4.1
.

Let
T>0
. If
p>1+d
and
\sigma>d/2
then for all
H\in\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))

\displaystyle\Big\|\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}[H]\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}\lesssim\underset{0\leq s,t\leq T}{\sup}\|G(t,s)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{p}_{\sigma}}\left\|H\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}.

As already noted in
[
HKR16
]
, this smoothing estimate is reminiscent of (but different from) the so-called kinetic averaging lemmas. Namely, Proposition
4.1
provides the gain of one full derivative.

Averaging lemmas are well-known to provide powerful regularity and compactness results in the study of kinetic equations. Loosely speaking, moments in velocity of the solutions appear to gain some regularity compared to the solutions themselves, which are just transported along the flow of the equation. We refer to
[
GPS85
,
Ago84
,
GLPS88
]
for the introduction of the averaging lemmas, and to
[
DL89b
,
PS98
,
GSR02
,
JV04
,
ASR11
,
AM14
,
JLT22
,
AL21
]
for several extensions of such results.

A thorough comparison between standard kinetic averaging lemmas and the estimate from Proposition
4.1
can be found in
[
HK19
]
. We finally refer to
[
HKNR18
]
for the use of Proposition
4.1
for a sligthly different purpose, as well as to
[
Cha23
]
for an extension of this proposition.

In this section, we prove crucial smoothing estimates adaptated to kinetic equations with friction, in the spirit of Proposition
4.1
. First, we define the corresponding integral operator.

Definition 4.2
.

For any smooth vector field
G(t,s,x,v)
, we define the following integral operators acting on functions
H(t,x)
by

\displaystyle\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{fric}}[H](t,x)

\displaystyle:=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}[\nabla_{x}H](s,x+(1-e^{t-s})v)\cdot G(t,s,x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s,

where
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{T}^{d}
.

Assuming that the kernel
G
is sufficiently smooth and decaying in velocity, we will prove several continuity and regularization estimates for
\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}
and
\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{fric}}
(see Propositions
4.4
–
4.5
–
4.7
below).

In what follows,
\mathcal{F}_{x,v}
will refer to the Fourier transform on
\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
defined as

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{x,v}h(k,\xi)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{-i(k\cdot x+v\cdot\xi)}h(x,v)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}v,\ \ (k,\xi)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}.

Our first result is the following.

Proposition 4.3
.

There exists
C>0
such that the followings holds. Suppose that
G_{[q]}(t,s,x,v)
is a kernel of the form

\displaystyle G_{[q]}(t,s,x,v)=(t-s)^{q}\mathcal{G}(t,s,x,v),

with
q\in\mathbb{N}
. For every
T>0
satisfying

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{G}\|_{T,s_{1},s_{2}}:=\underset{0\leq t\leq T}{\sup}\left(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\underset{0\leq s\leq t}{\sup}\,\underset{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\sup}\,\big\{(1+|m|)^{s_{2}}(1+|\xi|)^{s_{1}}|(\mathcal{F}_{x,v}\mathcal{G})(t,s,m,\xi)|\big\}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}<+\infty,

for
s_{1}>1+2q
and
s_{2}>d/2+2q
,

\displaystyle\Big\|\nabla^{q}_{x}\mathrm{K}_{G_{[q]}}^{\mathrm{free}}[H]\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}+\Big\|\nabla^{q}_{x}\mathrm{K}_{G_{[q]}}^{\mathrm{fric}}[H]\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\mathcal{G}\|_{T,s_{1},s_{2}}\|H\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}.

Proof.

We only perform the proof in the case of
\mathrm{K}_{G_{[q]}}^{\mathrm{fric}}
(the proof is similar for
\mathrm{K}_{G_{[q]}}^{\mathrm{free}}
). Writing for all
t\geq 0

\displaystyle H(t,x):=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\widehat{H}_{k}(t)e^{ik\cdot x}\ \ \text{in}\ \mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}),

we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{K}_{G_{[q]}}^{\mathrm{fric}}[H](t,x)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\widehat{H}_{k}(s)e^{ik\cdot x}(ik)\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{-ik\cdot(e^{t-s}-1)v}G_{[q]}(t,s,x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\widehat{H}_{k}(s)e^{ik\cdot x}(ik)\cdot(\mathcal{F}_{v}G_{[q]})(t,s,x,k(e^{t-s}-1))\,\mathrm{d}s.

We now expand
G_{[q]}
in Fourier series along the
x
variable so that

\displaystyle\mathrm{K}_{G_{[q]}}^{\mathrm{fric}}[H](t,x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}e^{i(k+\ell)\cdot x}\int_{0}^{t}\widehat{H}_{k}(s)(ik)\cdot(\mathcal{F}_{x,v}G_{[q]})(t,s,\ell,k(e^{t-s}-1))\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}e^{i\ell^{\prime}\cdot x}\left\{\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\int_{0}^{t}\widehat{H}_{k}(s)(ik)\cdot(\mathcal{F}_{x,v}G_{[q]})(t,s,\ell^{\prime}-k,k(e^{t-s}-1))\,\mathrm{d}s\right\},

and then

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}^{q}\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{fric}}_{(t-s)^{q}\mathcal{G}}[H](t,x)=\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}e^{i\ell\cdot x}\left\{(i\ell)^{q}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{q}\widehat{H}_{k}(s)(ik)\cdot(\mathcal{F}_{x,v}\mathcal{G})(t,s,\ell-k,k(e^{t-s}-1))\,\mathrm{d}s\right\}.

By the Parseval equality and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality (in frequency and time), we get

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}^{q}\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{fric}}_{(t-s)\mathcal{G}}[F](t)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\ell|^{2q}\left|\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{q}\widehat{H}_{k}(s)(ik)\cdot(\mathcal{F}_{x,v}\mathcal{G})(t,s,\ell-k,k(e^{t-s}-1))\,\mathrm{d}s\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\ell|^{2q}\left(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{2q}|\widehat{H}_{k}(s)|^{2}|k\cdot(\mathcal{F}_{x,v}\mathcal{G})(t,s,\ell-k,k(e^{t-s}-1))|\,\mathrm{d}s\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\left(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\int_{0}^{t}|k\cdot(\mathcal{F}_{x,v}\mathcal{G})(t,s,\ell-k,k(e^{t-s}-1))|\mathrm{d}s\right).

Integrating in time yields

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}^{q}\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{fric}}_{(t-s)\mathcal{G}}[F]\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\times\mathbb{T}^{d}))}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\ell|^{2q}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}(t-s)^{2q}|\widehat{H}_{k}(s)|^{2}|k\cdot(\mathcal{F}_{x,v}\mathcal{G})(t,s,\ell-k,k(e^{t-s}-1))|\,\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\quad\times\underset{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}{\sup}\underset{t\in(0,T)}{\sup}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\int_{0}^{t}|k\cdot(\mathcal{F}_{x,v}\mathcal{G})(t,s,\ell-k,k(e^{t-s}-1))|\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{I})\times(\mathrm{II}).

A first step is to note that

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\int_{0}^{t}

\displaystyle|k\cdot(\mathcal{F}_{x,v}\mathcal{G})(t,s,\ell-k,k(e^{t-s}-1))|\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}s\in(0,t)\\
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\end{subarray}}{\sup}(1+|\xi|)^{\alpha}|\mathcal{F}_{x,v}\mathcal{G}(t,s,\ell-k,\xi))|\int_{0}^{t}\frac{|k|}{(1+|k|(e^{t-s}-1))^{\alpha}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}s\in(0,t)\\
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\end{subarray}}{\sup}(1+|\xi|)^{\alpha}|\mathcal{F}_{x,v}\mathcal{G}(t,s,\ell-k,\xi))|\int_{0}^{t}\frac{|k|}{(1+|k|(t-s))^{\alpha}}\mathrm{d}s,

and that the change of variable
\tau=|k|(t-s)
in the last integral yields

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\frac{|k|}{(1+|k|(t-s))^{\alpha}}\mathrm{d}s\leq\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}\tau}{(1+\tau)^{\alpha}}<+\infty,

provided that
\alpha>1
. With this observation, we can treat the term
(\mathrm{II})
and obtain by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality in
k

\displaystyle(\mathrm{II})\leq\underset{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}{\sup}\underset{t\in(0,T)}{\sup}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}s\in(0,t)\\
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\end{subarray}}{\sup}(1+|\xi|)^{s_{1}}|\mathcal{F}_{x,v}\mathcal{G}(t,s,\ell-k,\xi))|\leq\|\mathcal{G}\|_{T,s_{1},s_{2}},

for
s_{1}>1
and
s_{2}>d/2
. For the term
(\mathrm{I})
, we use Fubini-Tonelli theorem and get

\displaystyle(\mathrm{I})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\ell|^{2q}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}(t-s)^{2q}|\widehat{H}_{k}(s)|^{2}|k||(\mathcal{F}_{x,v}\mathcal{G})(t,s,\ell-k,k(e^{t-s}-1))|\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\widehat{H}_{k}(s)|^{2}\int_{s}^{T}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}(t-s)^{2q}|\ell|^{2q}|k||(\mathcal{F}_{x,v}\mathcal{G})(t,s,\ell-k,k(e^{t-s}-1))|\,\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\|H\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}^{2}\underset{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}{\sup}\underset{0\leq s\leq T}{\sup}\int_{s}^{T}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}(t-s)^{2q}|\ell|^{2q}|k||(\mathcal{F}_{x,v}\mathcal{G})(t,s,\ell-k,k(e^{t-s}-1))|\,\mathrm{d}t.

Note that the last expression can be taken into account for
k\in\dot{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
only (indeed, the term corresponding to
k=0
vanishes in
(\mathrm{I})
). We then have

\displaystyle\underset{k\in\dot{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}{\sup}\underset{0\leq s\leq T}{\sup}\int_{s}^{T}(t-s)^{2q}|k|\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\ell|^{2q}|(\mathcal{F}_{x,v}\mathcal{G})(t,s,\ell-k,k(e^{t-s}-1))|\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\underset{k\in\dot{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}{\sup}\underset{0\leq s\leq T}{\sup}\int_{s}^{T}\frac{(t-s)^{2q}|k|}{(1+|k|(e^{t-s}-1))^{\alpha_{1}}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\ell|^{2q}(1+|k|(e^{t-s}-1))^{\alpha_{1}})|(\mathcal{F}_{x,v}\mathcal{G})(t,s,\ell-k,k(e^{t-s}-1))|\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\underset{k\in\dot{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}{\sup}\underset{0\leq s\leq T}{\sup}\int_{s}^{T}\frac{(t-s)^{2q}|k|}{(1+|k|(e^{t-s}-1))^{\alpha_{1}}}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\underset{0\leq s\leq T}{\sup}\underset{s\leq t\leq T}{\sup}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\ell|^{2q}\,\underset{\xi}{\sup}\,(1+|\xi|^{\alpha_{1}})|(\mathcal{F}_{x,v}\mathcal{G})(t,s,\ell-k,\xi)|

\displaystyle\leq\underset{k\in\dot{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}{\sup}\,\underset{0\leq s\leq T}{\sup}\int_{s}^{T}\frac{(t-s)^{2q}|k|}{(1+|k|(t-s))^{\alpha_{1}}}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\underset{0\leq s\leq T}{\sup}\underset{s\leq t\leq T}{\sup}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\ell+k|^{2q}\,\underset{\xi}{\sup}\,(1+|\xi|^{\alpha_{1}})|(\mathcal{F}_{x,v}\mathcal{G})(t,s,\ell,\xi)|,

therefore

\displaystyle\underset{k\in\dot{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}{\sup}

\displaystyle\underset{0\leq s\leq T}{\sup}\int_{s}^{T}(t-s)^{2q}|k|\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\ell|^{2q}|(\mathcal{F}_{x,v}\mathcal{G})(t,s,\ell-k,k(e^{t-s}-1))|\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\underset{k\in\dot{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}{\sup}\,\underset{0\leq s\leq T}{\sup}\int_{s}^{T}\frac{(t-s)^{2q}|k|}{(1+|k|(t-s))^{\alpha_{1}}}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\underset{0\leq s\leq T}{\sup}\underset{s\leq t\leq T}{\sup}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\ell|^{2q}\,\underset{\xi}{\sup}\,(1+|\xi|^{\alpha_{1}})|(\mathcal{F}_{x,v}\mathcal{G})(t,s,\ell,\xi)|

\displaystyle\quad+\underset{k\in\dot{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}{\sup}\,\underset{0\leq s\leq T}{\sup}\int_{s}^{T}\frac{(t-s)^{2q}|k|^{1+2q}}{(1+|k|(t-s))^{\alpha_{1}}}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\underset{0\leq s\leq T}{\sup}\underset{s\leq t\leq T}{\sup}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\,\underset{\xi}{\sup}\,(1+|\xi|^{\alpha_{1}})|(\mathcal{F}_{x,v}\mathcal{G})(t,s,\ell,\xi)|

\displaystyle=\mathrm{S}_{1}+\mathrm{S}_{2}.

Let us treat these two terms separately.

\bullet
For
\mathrm{S}_{1}
, we have by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality

\displaystyle\mathrm{S}_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\underset{k\in\dot{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}{\sup}\underset{0\leq s\leq T}{\sup}\int_{s}^{T}\frac{(t-s)^{2q}|k|}{(1+|k|(t-s))^{\alpha_{1}}}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\underset{0\leq s\leq T}{\sup}\underset{s\leq t\leq T}{\sup}\left(\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\underset{\xi}{\sup}\,\left\{(1+|\ell|^{2q+\alpha_{2}})(1+|\xi|^{\alpha_{1}})|(\mathcal{F}_{x,v}\mathcal{G})(t,s,\ell,\xi)|\right\}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

if
\alpha_{2}>d/2
. For the integral term, we write for
\alpha_{1}>3
.

\displaystyle\underset{k\in\dot{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}{\sup}\underset{0\leq s\leq T}{\sup}\int_{s}^{T}\frac{(t-s)^{2q}|k|}{(1+|k|(t-s))^{\alpha_{1}}}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\underset{k\in\dot{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}{\sup}\frac{1}{|k|^{2q}}\underset{0\leq s\leq T}{\sup}\int_{0}^{|k|(T-s)}\frac{\tau^{2q}}{(1+\tau)^{\alpha_{1}}}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\leq\underset{k\in\dot{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}{\sup}\frac{1}{|k|^{2q}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{\tau^{2q}}{(1+\tau)^{\alpha_{1}}}\,\mathrm{d}\tau,

which is a finite constant independent of
k
and
T
(since
\alpha_{1}>1+2q
) therefore

\displaystyle\mathrm{S}_{1}\lesssim\underset{0\leq t\leq T}{\sup}\left(\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\underset{0\leq s\leq t}{\sup}\ \underset{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\sup}\,\left\{(1+|\ell|^{2q+\alpha_{2}})(1+|\xi|^{\alpha_{1}})|(\mathcal{F}_{x,v}\mathcal{G})(t,s,\ell,\xi)|\right\}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

\bullet
For
\mathrm{S}_{2}
, we have for
\alpha_{2}>d/2

\displaystyle\mathrm{S}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\underset{k\in\dot{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}{\sup}\underset{0\leq s\leq T}{\sup}\int_{s}^{T}\frac{(t-s)^{2q}|k|^{1+2q}}{(1+|k|(t-s))^{\alpha_{1}}}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\underset{0\leq s\leq T}{\sup}\underset{s\leq t\leq T}{\sup}\left(\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\underset{\xi}{\sup}\,\left\{(1+|m|^{\alpha_{2}})(1+|\xi|^{\alpha_{1}})|(\mathcal{F}_{x,v}\mathcal{G})(t,s,\ell,\xi)|\right\}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

The integral term now reads for
\alpha_{1}>3

\displaystyle\underset{k\in\dot{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}{\sup}\underset{0\leq s\leq T}{\sup}\int_{s}^{T}\frac{(t-s)^{2q}|k|^{1+2q}}{(1+|k|(t-s))^{\alpha_{1}}}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\underset{k\in\dot{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}{\sup}\underset{0\leq s\leq T}{\sup}\int_{0}^{|k|(T-s)}\frac{\tau^{2q}}{(1+\tau)^{\alpha_{1}}}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\leq\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{\tau^{2q}}{(1+\tau)^{\alpha_{1}}}\,\mathrm{d}\tau,

therefore

\displaystyle\mathrm{S}_{1}\lesssim\underset{0\leq t\leq T}{\sup}\left(\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\underset{0\leq s\leq t}{\sup}\ \underset{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\sup}\,\left\{(1+|\ell|^{\alpha_{2}})(1+|\xi|^{\alpha_{1}})|(\mathcal{F}_{x,v}\mathcal{G})(t,s,\ell,\xi)|\right\}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

We have thus proven that

\displaystyle(\mathrm{I})\lesssim\|H\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}^{2}\left(\|\mathcal{G}\|_{T,s_{1},s_{2}}+\|\mathcal{G}\|_{T,s_{1},s_{2}+2q}\right),

for
s_{1}>1+2q
and
s_{2}>d/2+2q
.All in all, we get

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}^{q}\mathrm{K}_{G_{[q]}}^{\mathrm{fric}}[H]\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}\leq C\|\mathcal{G}\|_{T,s_{1},s_{2}}\|H\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))},

which ends the proof.
∎

We then deduce the two following propositions. The first one is a direct consequence of Proposition
4.3
with
q=0
, and states the continuity of

\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}
and
\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{fric}}
on
\mathrm{L}^{2}_{T}\mathrm{L}^{2}_{x}
.

Proposition 4.4
.

There exists
C>0
such that for every
T>0
, if
p>1+d
and
\sigma>d/2
then for all
H\in\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))

\displaystyle\Big\|\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}[H]\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}+\Big\|\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{fric}}[H]\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}

\displaystyle\leq C\underset{0\leq s,t\leq T}{\sup}\|G(t,s)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{p}_{\sigma}}\|H\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}.

Proof.

By Proposition
4.3
with
q=0
, we have

\displaystyle\Big\|\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}[H]\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}+\Big\|\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{fric}}[H]\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}

\displaystyle\leq C\|G\|_{T,s_{1},s_{2}}\|H\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}.

for any
s_{1}>1
and
s_{2}>d/2
. Now appealing to
[
HKR16
, Remark 3]
, one can prove that for all
p>1+d
and
\sigma>d/2
, there exist
s_{2}>d/2
and
s_{1}>1
such that

\displaystyle\|G\|_{T,s_{1},s_{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\underset{0\leq s,t\leq T}{\sup}\|G(t,s)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{p}_{\sigma}},

hence the result.
∎

When the kernel
G
vanishes along the diagonal in time
\{t=s\}
, Proposition
4.3
with
q=1
leads to the following additional regularizing effect of the operators
\mathrm{K}_{G}
(as already observed in
[
HKRon
]
). Loosely speaking, the operators
\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}
and
\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{fric}}
are bounded from
\mathrm{L}^{2}_{T}\mathrm{L}^{2}_{x}
to
\mathrm{L}^{2}_{T}\dot{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{x}
in this case.

Proposition 4.5
.

There exists
C>0
such that if the kernel
G
satisfies

\displaystyle G(t,t,x,v)=0,

the following holds. Let
T>0
. If
p>7+d
and
\sigma>d/2
then for all
H\in\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))

\displaystyle\Big\|\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}[H]\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}+\Big\|\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{fric}}[H]\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}\leq C(1+T)\underset{0\leq s,t\leq T}{\sup}\|\partial_{s}G(t,s)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{p}_{\sigma}}\|H\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}.

Proof.

Since
G(t,t,x,v)=0
, the Taylor’s formula shows that

\displaystyle G(t,s,x,v)=(t-s)\widetilde{G}(t,s,x,v),\ \ \widetilde{G}(t,s,x,v):=-\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{s}G(t,t+\tau(s-t),x,v)\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

By Proposition
4.3
with
q=1
, we get for
s_{1}>3
and
s_{2}>d/2+2

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}[H]\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}+\|\nabla_{x}\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{fric}}[H]\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\widetilde{G}\|_{T,s_{1},s_{2}}\|H\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}.

To conclude, we observe that

\displaystyle\big\{(1+|m|)^{s_{2}}(1+|\xi|)^{s_{1}}

\displaystyle|(\mathcal{F}_{x,v}\widetilde{G})(t,s,m,\xi)|\big\}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq\left((1+|m|)^{s_{2}}(1+|\xi|)^{s_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}|\mathcal{F}_{x,v}(\partial_{s}G)(t,t+\tau(s-t),m,\xi)|\,\mathrm{d}\tau\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq\int_{0}^{1}\big\{(1+|m|)^{s_{2}}(1+|\xi|)^{s_{1}}|\mathcal{F}_{x,v}(\partial_{s}G)(t,t+\tau(s-t),m,\xi)|\big\}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\tau,

therefore

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}[H]\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}+\|\nabla_{x}\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{fric}}[H]\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}\leq C\|\partial_{s}G\|_{T,s_{1},s_{2}}\|H\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}.

Now appealing to
[
HKR16
, Remark 3]
, one can prove that for all
p>2\ell+s+1+d
and
\sigma>d/2
(with
\ell,s\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
), there exist
s_{2}>\ell+d/2
and
s_{1}>s+1
such that

\displaystyle\|G\|_{T,s_{1},s_{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\underset{0\leq s,t\leq T}{\sup}\|G(t,s)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{p}_{\sigma}}.

Since
G(t,t,x,v)=0
, we also have

\displaystyle\|G\|_{T,s_{1},s_{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T\underset{0\leq s,t\leq T}{\sup}\|\partial_{s}G(t,s)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{p}_{\sigma}}.

By Proposition
4.4
, and taking
\ell=s=2
, we end up with the desired conclusion.
∎

Remark 4.6
.

A variant of Proposition
4.5
holds in the following form: there exists
C>0
such that for
p>7+d
and
\sigma>d/2
, and

\displaystyle G(t,t,x,v)=0,

we have for all
H\in\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathrm{H}}^{-1}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))

\displaystyle\Big\|\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}[H]\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}+\Big\|\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{fric}}[H]\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}

\displaystyle\leq C(1+T)\underset{0\leq s,t\leq T}{\sup}\|\partial_{s}G(t,s)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{p}_{\sigma}}\|H\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\dot{\mathrm{H}}^{-1}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}.

We don’t detail the proof, which follows the same lines as the one of Proposition
4.5
.

We finally investigate the smoothing properties of the difference operator
\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}-\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{fric}}
. A somewhat surprising result is the fact that this operator gains one additional derivative. This is the content of the following proposition.

Proposition 4.7
.

There exists
C>0
such that for every
T>0
, if
p>8+d
and
\sigma>1+d/2
then for all
H\in\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))
,

\displaystyle\Big\|\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}[H]-\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{fric}}[H]\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}

\displaystyle\leq C\varphi(T)\underset{0\leq s,t\leq T}{\sup}\|G(t,s)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{p}_{\sigma}}\|H\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))},

where
\varphi:\mathbb{R}^{+}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
is a continuous nondecreasing function.

Proof.

Following the computations performed in the proof of Proposition
4.5
, we have

\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}[F](t,x)-\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{fric}}[F](t,x)=\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}e^{i\ell\cdot x}\Bigg\{\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\int_{0}^{t}\widehat{F}_{k}(s)(ik)\cdot\left[(\mathcal{F}_{x,v}G)(t,s,\ell-k,k(t-s))\right.\\
\left.-(\mathcal{F}_{x,v}G)(t,s,\ell-k,k(e^{t-s}-1))\right]\,\mathrm{d}s\Bigg\},

therefore, if we set

\displaystyle\Theta(t,s,\ell,k):=(\mathcal{F}_{x,v}G)(t,s,\ell-k,k(t-s))-(\mathcal{F}_{x,v}G)(t,s,\ell-k,k(e^{t-s}-1)),

we get

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}\left(\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}[H]-\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{fric}}[H]\right)(t)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\ell|^{2}\left(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\int_{0}^{t}|\widehat{H}_{k}(s)||k||\Theta(t,s,\ell,k)|\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{2}.

We also have, by setting
\mathcal{G}^{t,s}_{\ell-k}=(\mathcal{F}_{x,v}G)(t,s,\ell-k,\bullet)

\displaystyle|\Theta(t,s,\ell,k)|

\displaystyle=|\mathcal{G}^{t,s}_{\ell-k}(k(e^{t-s}-1))-\mathcal{G}^{t,s}_{\ell-k}(k(t-s))|

\displaystyle=|\mathcal{G}^{t,s}_{\ell-k}(k(t-s)+k(t-s)^{2}\varphi(t-s))-\mathcal{G}^{t,s}_{\ell-k}(k(t-s))|

\displaystyle\leq\underset{\theta\in[0,1]}{\sup}|\nabla_{\xi}\mathcal{G}^{t,s}_{\ell-k}\left(\xi_{\theta}^{t,s}(k(t-s))\right)||k|(t-s)^{2}\varphi(t-s),

where
\varphi(z)=\sum_{i\geq 0}\frac{z^{i}}{(i+2)!}
and
\xi_{\theta}^{t,s}(z)=z+\theta z(t-s)\varphi(t-s)
. By continuity, there exists
\theta^{\star}\in[0,1]
(which may depend on all the other variables) such that

\displaystyle|\Theta(t,s,\ell,k)|\leq|\nabla_{\xi}\mathcal{G}^{t,s}_{\ell-k}\left(\xi_{\theta^{\star}}^{t,s}(k(t-s))\right)||k|(t-s)^{2}\varphi(t-s).

This yields

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}

\displaystyle\left(\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}[H]-\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{fric}}[H]\right)(t)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\ell|^{2}\left(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\int_{0}^{t}|\widehat{H}_{k}(s)||k|^{2}(t-s)^{2}\varphi(t-s)|\nabla_{\xi}\mathcal{G}^{t,s}_{\ell-k}\left(\xi_{\theta^{\star}}^{t,s}(k(t-s))\right)|\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\ell|^{2}\left(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\int_{0}^{t}|\widehat{H}_{k}(s)|^{2}|k|^{2}(t-s)^{3}\varphi(t-s)^{2}|\nabla_{\xi}\mathcal{G}^{t,s}_{\ell-k}\left((\xi_{\theta^{\star}}^{t,s}(k(t-s))\right)|\,\mathrm{d}s\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\left(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\int_{0}^{t}|k|^{2}(t-s)|\nabla_{\xi}\mathcal{G}^{t,s}_{\ell-k}\left(\xi_{\theta^{\star}}^{t,s}(k(t-s))\right)|\,\mathrm{d}s\right),

thanks to the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.
As in the proof of Proposition
4.5
, we obtain by integrating in time that

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}

\displaystyle\left(\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}[H]-\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{fric}}[H]\right)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}((0,T)\times\mathbb{T}^{d})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\ell|^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\widehat{H}_{k}(s)|^{2}|k|^{2}(t-s)^{3}\varphi(t-s)^{2}|\nabla_{\xi}\mathcal{G}^{t,s}_{\ell-k}\left(\xi_{\theta^{\star}}^{t,s}(k(t-s))\right)|\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\times\underset{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}{\sup}\underset{t\in(0,T)}{\sup}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\int_{0}^{t}|k|^{2}(t-s)|\nabla_{\xi}\mathcal{G}^{t,s}_{\ell-k}\left(\xi_{\theta^{\star}}^{t,s}(k(t-s))\right)|\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{A})\times(\mathrm{B}).

First, we note that for all
\theta\in[0,1]
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
and
0\leq s\leq t

\displaystyle|\xi_{\theta}^{t,s}(k(t-s))|

\displaystyle=|k(t-s)+\theta k(t-s)^{2}\varphi(t-s)|

\displaystyle=|k|(t-s)\left[1+\theta(t-s)\varphi(t-s)\right]

\displaystyle\geq|k|(t-s).

For
(\mathrm{B})
, we thus have

\displaystyle(\mathrm{B})

\displaystyle\leq\underset{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}{\sup}\underset{t\in(0,T)}{\sup}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\int_{0}^{t}(1+|\xi_{\theta^{\star}}^{t,s}(k(t-s))|)^{\beta_{1}}|\nabla_{\xi}\mathcal{G}^{t,s}_{\ell-k}\left(\xi_{\theta^{\star}}^{t,s}(k(t-s))\right)|\frac{|k|^{2}(t-s)}{(1+|\xi_{\theta^{\star}}^{t,s}(k(t-s))|)^{\beta_{1}}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\underset{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}{\sup}\underset{t\in(0,T)}{\sup}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\underset{s,\xi}{\sup}\big\{(1+|\xi|)^{\beta_{1}}|\nabla_{\xi}\mathcal{G}^{t,s}_{\ell-k}(\xi)|\big\}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{|k|^{2}(t-s)}{(1+|k|(t-s))^{\beta_{1}}}\mathrm{d}s.

Since

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\frac{|k|^{2}(t-s)}{(1+|k|(t-s))^{\beta_{1}}}\mathrm{d}s=\int_{0}^{|k|t}\frac{\tau}{(1+\tau)^{\beta_{1}}}\mathrm{d}\tau\leq\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{\tau}{(1+\tau)^{\beta_{1}}}\mathrm{d}\tau<+\infty,

if
\beta_{1}>2
, we get

\displaystyle(\mathrm{B})\lesssim\underset{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}{\sup}\underset{t\in(0,T)}{\sup}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\underset{s,\xi}{\sup}\big\{(1+|\xi|)^{\beta_{1}}|\nabla_{\xi}\mathcal{G}^{t,s}_{\ell-k}(\xi)|\big\}.

By choosing
\beta_{2}>d/2
and by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, this yields

\displaystyle(\mathrm{B})\lesssim\underset{t\in(0,T)}{\sup}\left(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\underset{s,\xi}{\sup}\big\{(1+|k|)^{\beta_{2}}(1+|\xi|)^{\beta_{1}}|\nabla_{\xi}\mathcal{G}^{t,s}_{k}(\xi)|\big\}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Let us estimate the other term
(\mathrm{A})
. By the Fubini-Tonelli theorem, we have

\displaystyle(\mathrm{A})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\ell|^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\widehat{H}_{k}(s)|^{2}|k|^{2}(t-s)^{3}\varphi(t-s)^{2}|\nabla_{\xi}\mathcal{G}^{t,s}_{\ell-k}\left(\xi_{\theta^{\star}}^{t,s}(k(t-s))\right)|\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\widehat{H}_{k}(s)|^{2}\int_{s}^{T}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\ell|^{2}|k|^{2}(t-s)^{3}\varphi(t-s)^{2}|\nabla_{\xi}\mathcal{G}^{t,s}_{\ell-k}\left(\xi_{\theta^{\star}}^{t,s}(k(t-s))\right)|\,\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\|H\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}^{2}\underset{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}{\sup}\underset{0\leq s\leq T}{\sup}\int_{s}^{T}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\ell|^{2}|k|^{2}(t-s)^{3}\varphi(t-s)^{2}|\nabla_{\xi}\mathcal{G}^{t,s}_{\ell-k}\left(\xi_{\theta^{\star}}^{t,s}(k(t-s))\right)|\,\mathrm{d}t.

As in the proof of Proposition
4.3
, we have

\displaystyle\underset{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}{\sup}

\displaystyle\underset{0\leq s\leq T}{\sup}\int_{s}^{T}|k|^{2}(t-s)^{3}\varphi(t-s)^{2}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\ell|^{2}|\nabla_{\xi}\mathcal{G}^{t,s}_{\ell-k}\left(\xi_{\theta^{\star}}^{t,s}(k(t-s))\right)|\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\underset{k\in\dot{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}{\sup}\,\underset{0\leq s\leq T}{\sup}\int_{s}^{T}\frac{|k|^{2}(t-s)^{3}\varphi(t-s)^{2}}{(1+|k|(t-s))^{\alpha_{1}}}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\underset{0\leq s\leq T}{\sup}\underset{s\leq t\leq T}{\sup}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\ell|^{2}\,\underset{\xi}{\sup}\,(1+|\xi|^{\alpha_{1}})|\nabla_{\xi}\mathcal{G}^{t,s}_{\ell}(\xi)|

\displaystyle\quad+\underset{k\in\dot{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}{\sup}\,\underset{0\leq s\leq T}{\sup}\int_{s}^{T}\frac{|k|^{4}(t-s)^{3}\varphi(t-s)^{2}}{(1+|k|(t-s))^{\alpha_{1}}}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\underset{0\leq s\leq T}{\sup}\underset{s\leq t\leq T}{\sup}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\,\underset{\xi}{\sup}\,(1+|\xi|^{\alpha_{1}})|\nabla_{\xi}\mathcal{G}^{t,s}_{\ell}(\xi)|

\displaystyle=\mathrm{T}_{1}+\mathrm{T}_{2}.

We treat these two terms in a separate way.

\bullet
In
\mathrm{T}_{1}
, the integral term can be bounded via
