\displaystyle\underset{k\in\dot{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}{\sup}\underset{0\leq s\leq T}{\sup}\int_{s}^{T}\frac{|k|^{2}(t-s)^{3}\varphi(t-s)^{2}}{(1+|k|(t-s))^{\alpha_{1}}}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\underset{k\in\dot{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}{\sup}\frac{1}{|k|^{2}}\underset{0\leq s\leq T}{\sup}\varphi(T-s)^{2}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{\tau^{3}}{(1+\tau)^{\alpha_{1}}}\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

\displaystyle\leq\varphi(T)^{2}\underset{k\in\dot{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}{\sup}\frac{1}{|k|^{2}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{\tau^{3}}{(1+\tau)^{\alpha_{1}}}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim\varphi(T)^{2},

provided that
\alpha_{1}>4
. This implies that for any
\alpha_{2}>d/2

\displaystyle\mathrm{T}_{1}\lesssim\varphi(T)^{2}\underset{0\leq s\leq T}{\sup}\underset{s\leq t\leq T}{\sup}\left(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\underset{\xi}{\sup}\,\left\{(1+|m|^{2+\alpha_{2}})(1+|\xi|^{\alpha_{1}})|\nabla_{\xi}\mathcal{G}^{t,s}_{m}(\xi)|\right\}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

\bullet
In
\mathrm{T}_{2}
, the integral term can be bounded in a similar way via

\displaystyle\underset{k\in\dot{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}{\sup}\,\underset{0\leq s\leq T}{\sup}\int_{s}^{T}\frac{|k|^{4}(t-s)^{3}\varphi(t-s)^{2}}{(1+|k|(t-s))^{\alpha_{1}}}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\underset{0\leq s\leq T}{\sup}\varphi(T-s)^{2}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{\tau^{3}}{(1+\tau)^{\alpha_{1}}}\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

\displaystyle\leq\varphi(T)^{2}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{\tau^{3}}{(1+\tau)^{\alpha_{1}}}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim\varphi(T)^{2},

provided that
\alpha_{1}>4
. This implies that for any
\alpha_{2}>d/2

\displaystyle\mathrm{T}_{2}\lesssim\varphi(T)\underset{0\leq s\leq T}{\sup}\underset{s\leq t\leq T}{\sup}\left(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\underset{\xi}{\sup}\,\left\{(1+|m|^{\alpha_{2}})(1+|\xi|^{\alpha_{1}})|\nabla_{\xi}\mathcal{G}^{t,s}_{m}(\xi)|\right\}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

All in all, we get for
\alpha_{1}>4
and
\alpha_{2}>2+d/2

\displaystyle(\mathrm{A})

\displaystyle\lesssim\varphi(T)^{2}\|H\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\underset{0\leq s\leq T}{\sup}\underset{s\leq t\leq T}{\sup}\left(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\underset{\xi}{\sup}\,\left\{(1+|m|^{\alpha_{2}})(1+|\xi|^{\alpha_{1}})|\nabla_{\xi}\mathcal{G}^{t,s}_{m}(\xi)|\right\}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varphi(T)^{2}\|H\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\underset{0\leq t\leq T}{\sup}\left(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\underset{0\leq s\leq t}{\sup}\underset{\xi}{\sup}\,\left\{(1+|m|^{\alpha_{2}})(1+|\xi|^{\alpha_{1}})|\nabla_{\xi}\mathcal{G}^{t,s}_{m}(\xi)|\right\}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

We have thus proven that for
s_{1}>4
and
s_{2}>2+d/2

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{x}\left(\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}[H]-\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{fric}}[H]\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}\lesssim\varphi(T)\left\|v\otimes G\right\|_{T,s_{1},s_{2}}\|H\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))},

where we have used the semi-norm
\|\cdot\|_{T,s_{1},s_{2}}
from Proposition
4.3
. We can now conclude as in the proof of Proposition
4.5
. We observe that for all
p>2\ell+s+1+d
and
\sigma>1+d/2
(with
\ell,s\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
), there exist
s_{1}>s+1
and
s_{2}>\ell+d/2
such that

\displaystyle\|v\otimes G\|_{T,s_{1},s_{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\underset{0\leq s,t\leq T}{\sup}\|G(t,s)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{p}_{\sigma}}.

By taking
\ell=2
ans
s=3
, and by finally using Proposition
4.3
, we reach the desired conclusion.

∎

5.
Analysis of the kinetic moments

Following the bootstrap procedure initiated in Section
2.2
, we want to control
\left\|\varrho_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})}
uniformly in
\varepsilon
and for
T<T_{\varepsilon}
. In view of the transport equation bearing on
\varrho_{\varepsilon}
(see Lemma
2.2
), we will relate the kinetic moments
\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}}
and
j_{f_{\varepsilon}}
to the fluid density
\varrho_{\varepsilon}
itself.

In this section, to ease readability, we drop out the subscripts
\varepsilon
when we refer to the solution. Recall that
\Lambda
will always stand for a nonnegative continuous function which is independent of
\varepsilon
, nondecreasing with respect to each of its argument, that may depend implicitly on the initial data and that may change from line to line.

For all
T\in[0,T_{\varepsilon})
small enough, the goal of this section is thus to prove the following result.

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
T\in(0,\min\left(T_{\varepsilon}(R),\overline{T}(R)\right)
. For all
|I|\leq m
, we have for any
t\in(0,T)
,

\displaystyle\partial_{x}^{I}\rho_{f}(t,x)

\displaystyle=p^{\prime}(\varrho(t,x))\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla_{x}[\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}^{I}\varrho](s,x-(t-s)v)\cdot\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s+R^{I}[\rho_{f}](t,x),

\displaystyle\partial_{x}^{I}j_{f}(t,x)

\displaystyle=p^{\prime}(\varrho(t,x))\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}v\nabla_{x}[\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}^{I}\varrho](s,x-(t-s)v)\cdot\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s+R^{I}[j_{f}](t,x),

where the remainders
R^{I}[\rho_{f}]
and
R^{I}[j_{f}]
satisfy

\displaystyle\left\|R^{I}[\rho_{f}]\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T,\mathrm{H}^{1}_{x})}\leq\Lambda(T,R),\ \ \left\|R^{I}[j_{f}]\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T,\mathrm{H}^{1}_{x})}\leq\Lambda(T,R).

We recall the definition of the time
T_{\varepsilon}(R)
from our bootstrap procedure settled in Section
2.2
, as well as the definition of the time
\overline{T}(R)
from Lemma
3.1
in Section
3
. Note that it is independent of
\varepsilon
. In the rest of this section, we will always implicitly consider times
T>0
such that

\displaystyle T<\min\left(T_{\varepsilon}(R),\overline{T}(R)\right).

From Proposition
5.1
, we can immediately infer the following corollary.

Corollary 5.2
.

For
m>2+d
,
\sigma>1+d/2
and
|I|\leq m
, we have

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}^{I}\rho_{f}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda(T,R),

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}^{I}j_{f}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda(T,R).

Proof.

By Proposition
5.1
, we can write

\displaystyle\partial_{x}^{I}\rho_{f}=p^{\prime}(\varrho)\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}[\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}^{I}\varrho]+R^{I}[\rho_{f}],

with
G(t,x,v)=\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v)
and
\left\|R^{I}[\rho_{f}]\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T,\mathrm{H}^{1}_{x})}\leq\Lambda(T,R)
. Since the kernel
G
satisfies for
p>1+d

\displaystyle\underset{0\leq t\leq T}{\sup}\|G\|_{\mathcal{H}^{p}_{\sigma}}\leq\|f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{m-1}_{\sigma})}\leq\Lambda(T,R),

we can use the estimate from Proposition
4.1
to get

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}^{I}\rho_{f}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|p^{\prime}(\varrho)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{\infty})}\Lambda(T,R)\|\partial_{x}^{I}\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}+\|R^{I}[\rho_{f}]\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathrm{C}(\|\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-2})})\Lambda(T,R)+\Lambda(T,R)

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda(T,R),

by Sobolev embedding, Proposition
A.3
and Lemma
2.20
. The same argument applies for
\|\partial_{x}^{I}j_{f}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}
.
∎

Our strategy to prove Proposition
5.1
goes as follows:

-

first, we take derivatives in the Vlasov equation to obtain a system of coupled kinetic equations satisfied by the augmented unknown
(\partial^{I}_{x}\partial^{J}_{v}f_{\varepsilon})_{|I|+|J|=m-1,m}
;

-

next, we study the average in velocity of
\mathcal{F}
by relying on Duhamel formula and the Lagrangian point of view of Section
3
. We isolate the leading terms and prove estimates for the remainders, using crucially the techniques developed in Sections
3
and
4
.

5.1.
The integro-differential system for derivatives of moments

5.1.1.
Applying derivatives

We start with the following algebraic lemma, where we apply
\partial_{x}^{I}\partial_{v}^{J}
to the Vlasov equation.
Let us recall the notation
\widehat{\alpha}^{k}
and
\overline{\alpha}^{k}
for shifted indices (see Definition
2.8
).

Lemma 5.3
.

For any
I=(i_{1},\cdots,i_{d}),J=(j_{1},\cdots,,j_{d})\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
such that
|I|+|J|\in\{m-1,m\}
and for any smooth function
f(t,x,v)
, we have

\displaystyle\left[\partial_{x}^{I}\partial_{v}^{J},\mathcal{T}_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\right]f=\partial_{x}^{I}\partial_{v}^{J}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\cdot\nabla_{v}f+\mathcal{M}^{I,J}\mathcal{F}+\mathcal{R}^{I,J}_{1}+\mathcal{R}^{I,J}_{0},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}

\displaystyle:=\left(\partial_{x}^{I}\partial_{v}^{J}f\right)_{\begin{subarray}{c}I,J\in\mathbb{N}^{d},\\
|I|+|J|\in\{m-1,m\}\end{subarray}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{I,J}\mathcal{F}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{p=1}^{d}\mathbf{1}_{j_{p}\neq 0}\left(\partial_{x}^{\widehat{I}^{p}}\partial_{v}^{\overline{J}^{p}}f-\partial_{x}^{I}\partial_{v}^{J}f\right)+\mathbf{1}_{|I|>2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<\alpha<I\\
|\alpha|\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\binom{I}{\alpha}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial_{x}^{I-\alpha}\partial_{v}^{J}f

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbf{1}_{|I|=1,2}\partial_{x}^{I}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\cdot\partial_{v}^{J}\nabla_{v}f,

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{I,J}_{1}

\displaystyle:=\mathbf{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}|I|>2\\
|J|\neq 0\end{subarray}}\partial_{x}^{I}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{J}f+\mathbf{1}_{|I|>1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<\alpha<I\\
|\alpha|=m-1\end{subarray}}\binom{I}{\alpha}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial_{x}^{I-\alpha}\partial_{v}^{J}f,

where

(5.1)

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{R}^{I,J}_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{0}_{r})}\leq\Lambda(T,R),

and where
\mathcal{R}^{I,J}_{0}
is a remainder satisfying,

(5.2)

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{R}^{I,J}_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r})}\leq\Lambda(T,R).

Proof.

Using Lemma
2.9
, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{x}^{I}\partial_{v}^{J}(\mathcal{T}_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}f)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{T}_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}(\partial_{x}^{I}\partial_{v}^{J}f)+\sum_{p=1}^{d}\mathbf{1}_{j_{p}\neq 0}\left(\partial_{x}^{\widehat{I}^{p}}\partial_{v}^{\overline{J}^{p}}f-\partial_{x}^{I}\partial_{v}^{J}f\right)+\left[\partial_{x}^{I}\partial_{v}^{J},E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\cdot\nabla_{v}\right]f.

Since the force
E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}(t,x)
does not depend on
v
, we expand the commutator as

\displaystyle\left[\partial_{x}^{I}\partial_{v}^{J},E(t,x)\cdot\nabla_{v}\right]f=\mathbf{1}_{I\neq 0}\partial_{x}^{I}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\cdot\partial_{v}^{J}\nabla_{v}f+\mathbf{1}_{|I|>1}\sum_{0<\alpha<I}\binom{I}{\alpha}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\cdot\partial_{x}^{I-\alpha}\partial_{v}^{J}\nabla_{v}f.

Note that if
J\neq 0
then
|I|\leq m-1
, and if
|I|=1,2
then
J\neq 0
. The terms that cannot be considered as remainders in the last sum are those such that
|I|-|\alpha|+|J|+1\in\{m-1,m\}
, that is for
|\alpha|\in\{1,2\}
. We thus have

\displaystyle\left[\partial_{x}^{I}\partial_{v}^{J},E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\cdot\nabla_{v}\right]f

\displaystyle=\mathbf{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}|I|\neq 0\\
J=0\end{subarray}}\partial_{x}^{I}\partial_{v}^{J}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\cdot\nabla_{v}f+\mathbf{1}_{|I|=1,2}\partial_{x}^{I}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\cdot\partial_{v}^{J}\nabla_{v}f

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbf{1}_{|I|>2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<\alpha<I\\
|\alpha|\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\binom{I}{\alpha}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial_{x}^{I-\alpha}\partial_{v}^{J}f

\displaystyle\quad+\mathcal{R}^{I,J}_{1}+\mathcal{R}^{I,J}_{0},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{I,J}_{1}

\displaystyle:=\mathbf{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}|I|>2\\
|J|\neq 0\end{subarray}}\partial_{x}^{I}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\cdot\partial_{v}^{J}\nabla_{v}f+\mathbf{1}_{|I|>1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<\alpha<I\\
|\alpha|=m-1\end{subarray}}\binom{I}{\alpha}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial_{x}^{I-\alpha}\partial_{v}^{J}f,

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{I,J}_{0}

\displaystyle:=\mathbf{1}_{|I|>1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<\alpha<I\\
3\leq|\alpha|\leq m-2\end{subarray}}\binom{I}{\alpha}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial_{x}^{I-\alpha}\partial_{v}^{J}f.

Let us estimate the remainder
\mathcal{R}^{I,J}_{0}
in
\mathcal{H}_{r}^{1}
: setting
\chi(v)=(1+|v|^{2})^{r/2}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{R}^{I,J}_{0}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{r}^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<\alpha<I\\
3\leq|\alpha|\leq m-2\end{subarray}}\|\chi\partial_{x}^{\alpha}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial_{x}^{I-\alpha}\partial_{v}^{J}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<\alpha<I\\
3\leq|\alpha|\leq m-2\end{subarray}}\sum_{k=1}^{d}\Big(\|\chi\partial_{x}^{\widehat{\alpha}^{k}}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial_{x}^{I-\alpha}\partial_{v}^{J}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}_{x,v}}+\|\chi\partial_{x}^{\alpha}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\cdot\partial_{x_{k}}\nabla_{v}\partial_{x}^{I-\alpha}\partial_{v}^{J}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\|\chi\partial_{x}^{\alpha}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\cdot\partial_{v_{k}}\nabla_{v}\partial_{x}^{I-\alpha}\partial_{v}^{J}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}_{x,v}}\Big).

\bullet
If
\frac{m+1}{2}\leq|\alpha|\leq m-2
, then for all
k

\displaystyle\|\chi\partial_{x}^{\alpha}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial_{x}^{I-\alpha}\partial_{v}^{J}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|\chi\partial_{x}^{\widehat{\alpha}^{k}}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial_{x}^{I-\alpha}\partial_{v}^{J}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}^{2}+\|\partial_{x}^{\widehat{\alpha}^{k}}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\chi(v)^{2}\|\nabla_{v}\partial_{x}^{I-\alpha}\partial_{v}^{J}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\lesssim\|E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m-1}}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\chi(v)^{2}\|\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{J}f\|_{\mathrm{H}^{\sigma}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}v,

provided that
\sigma>|I-\alpha|+d/2
. Since
|J|+1+|I|-|\alpha|+d/2\leq m+1-|\alpha|+d/2\leq\frac{m+1+d}{2}
and since
m>4+d
, we can find such a
\sigma
so that

\displaystyle\|\chi\partial_{x}^{\alpha}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial_{x}^{I-\alpha}\partial_{v}^{J}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}_{x,v}}+\|\chi\partial_{x}^{\widehat{\alpha}^{k}}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial_{x}^{I-\alpha}\partial_{v}^{J}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}_{x,v}}\lesssim\|E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m-1}}\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}_{r}^{m-1}}.

Likewise, we have for all
k

\displaystyle\|\chi\partial_{x}^{\alpha}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\cdot\partial_{x_{k}}\nabla_{v}\partial_{x}^{I-\alpha}\partial_{v}^{J}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}\leq\|E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m-1}}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\chi(v)^{2}\|\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{J}f\|_{\mathrm{H}^{\sigma}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}v,

provided that
\sigma>1+|I-\alpha|+d/2
. Since
|J|+1+1+|I|-|\alpha|+d/2\leq m+2-|\alpha|+d/2\leq\frac{m+3+d}{2}
and since
m>6+d
, there exists such a
\sigma
so that

\displaystyle\|\chi\partial_{x}^{\alpha}E\cdot\partial_{x_{k}}\nabla_{v}\partial_{x}^{I-\alpha}\partial_{v}^{J}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}_{x,v}}\lesssim\|E\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m-1}}\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}_{r}^{m-1}}.

The same procedure can be applied for the terms
\|\chi\partial_{x}^{\alpha}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\cdot\partial_{v_{k}}\nabla_{v}\partial_{x}^{I-\alpha}\partial_{v}^{J}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}_{x,v}}
.

\bullet
If
3\leq|\alpha|<\frac{m+1}{2}
, then

\displaystyle\|\chi\partial_{x}^{\alpha}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial_{x}^{I-\alpha}\partial_{v}^{J}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle+\|\chi\partial_{x}^{\widehat{\alpha}^{k}}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial_{x}^{I-\alpha}\partial_{v}^{J}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}+\|\partial_{x}^{\widehat{\alpha}^{k}}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}\right)\|\chi\nabla_{v}\partial_{x}^{I-\alpha}\partial_{v}^{J}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\|_{\mathrm{H}^{\sigma}}\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}_{r}^{m-1}},

provided that
\sigma>1+|\alpha|+d/2
. Since
1+|\alpha|+d/2\leq\frac{m+3+d}{2}
and
m>5+d
, we can find such a
\sigma
so that

\displaystyle\|\chi\partial_{x}^{\widehat{\alpha}^{k}}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial_{x}^{I-\alpha}\partial_{v}^{J}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}_{x,v}}\lesssim\|E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m-1}}\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}_{r}^{m-1}}.

Likewise, we have for all
k

\displaystyle\|\chi\partial_{x}^{\alpha}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\cdot\partial_{x_{k}}\nabla_{v}\partial_{x}^{I-\alpha}\partial_{v}^{J}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}\leq\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}\|\chi\partial_{x}\nabla_{v}\partial_{x}^{I-\alpha}\partial_{v}^{J}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}_{x,v}}\lesssim\|E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\|_{\mathrm{H}^{\sigma}}\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}_{r}^{m-1}},

provided that
\sigma>|I-\alpha|+d/2
. Since
|\alpha|+d/2\leq\frac{m+2+d}{2}
and
m>4+d
, there exists such a
\sigma
so that

\displaystyle\|\chi\partial_{x}^{\alpha}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial_{x}\partial_{x}^{I-\alpha}\partial_{v}^{J}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}_{x,v}}\lesssim\|E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m-1}}\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}_{r}^{m-1}}.

The same procedure can be applied for the terms
\|\chi\partial_{x}^{\alpha}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\cdot\partial_{v_{k}}\nabla_{v}\partial_{x}^{I-\alpha}\partial_{v}^{J}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}_{x,v}}
.

All in all, we have proven that for all
t\in[0,T]

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{R}^{I,J}_{0}(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}_{r}^{1}}\lesssim\|E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}(t)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m-1}}\|f(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}_{r}^{m-1}}\leq\|f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}_{r}^{m-1})}\|E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}(t)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m-1}}\leq R\|E(t)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m-1}}.

By the estimate (
2.2
) from Lemma
2.7
and by Lemma
2.20
, we finally have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{R}^{I,J}_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r})}\leq\Lambda(T,R).

With the same exact arguments, we easily obtain the fact that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{R}^{I,J}_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{0}_{r})}\leq\Lambda(T,R),

and this concludes the proof.

∎

Remark 5.4
.

We will actually obtain an improved
\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{1}_{r})
estimate for the term
\mathcal{R}^{I,J}_{1}
(or, more preciely, related terms) in the end of the current section.

We can see
\mathcal{M}^{I,J}\mathcal{F}
appearing in Lemma
5.3
as a linear combination of
\mathcal{F}^{K,L}=\partial_{x}^{K}\partial_{v}^{L}f
. More precisely, we can write for all
(I,J)
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{I,J}\mathcal{F}

\displaystyle=\sum_{K,L}\mathcal{M}_{(I,J),(K,L)}\mathcal{F}^{K,L},

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{(I,J),(K,L)}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{p=1}^{d}\mathbf{1}_{j_{p}\neq 0}(\mathbf{1}_{(K,L)=(\widehat{I}^{p},\overline{J}^{p})}-\mathbf{1}_{(K,L)=(I,J)})+\mathbf{1}_{|I|=1,2}\sum_{p=1}^{d}\mathbf{1}_{(K,L)=(0,\widehat{J}^{p})}\partial_{x}^{I}(E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho})_{p}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{p=1}^{d}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<\alpha<I\\
|\alpha|\in\{1,2\}\end{subarray}}\binom{I}{\alpha}\mathbf{1}_{(K,L)=(I-\alpha,\widehat{J}^{p})}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}(E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho})_{p}.

Let us observe that the coefficient involved in the operator
\mathcal{M}
involve only
0
,
1
or
2
derivatives of the force field
E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}(t,x)
, but nothing coming from
f
.

We finally introduce the following additional notations which will allow us to reformulate Lemma
5.3
in a compact way.

Definition 5.5
.

We consider the following quantities:

(1)

\mathcal{R}_{0}
and
\mathcal{R}_{1}
are the vectors defined by

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{0}=\left(\mathcal{R}^{I,J}_{0}\right)_{\begin{subarray}{c}I,J\in\mathbb{N}^{d},\\
|I|+|J|\in\{m-1,m\}\end{subarray}},\ \ \mathcal{R}_{1}=\left(\mathcal{R}^{I,J}_{0}\right)_{\begin{subarray}{c}I,J\in\mathbb{N}^{d},\\
|I|+|J|\in\{m-1,m\}\end{subarray}};

(2)

\mathcal{M}
is the linear map defined by

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}=\left(\mathcal{M}_{(I,J),(K,L)}\right)_{\begin{subarray}{c}(I,J),(K,L)\\
|I|+|J|,|K|+|L|\in\{m-1,m\}\end{subarray}};

(3)

\mathcal{L}
is the vector defined by

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}=\left(\partial_{x}^{I}\partial_{v}^{J}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\cdot\nabla_{v}f\right)_{\begin{subarray}{c}I,J\in\mathbb{N}^{d},\\
|I|+|J|\in\{m-1,m\}\end{subarray}}.

5.1.2.
The semi-Lagrangian approach

If
f
satisfies the Vlasov equation (in a strong sense), we have
\partial_{x}^{I}\partial_{v}^{J}(\mathcal{T}_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}f)=0
for any
I,J
. We can use Lemma
5.3
to obtain the following coupled system of equations satisfied by the family
\mathcal{F}=(\partial_{x}^{I}\partial_{v}^{J}f)_{I,J}
:

(5.3)

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\mathcal{F}+\mathcal{M}\mathcal{F}+\mathcal{L}=-\mathcal{R}_{0}-\mathcal{R}_{1}.

For any function
g(t,x,v)
, we set

\displaystyle\widetilde{g}(t,x,v)=g(t,\mathrm{X}^{t;0}(x,v),\mathrm{V}^{t;0}(x,v)),

where

s\mapsto\mathrm{Z}^{s;t}(x,v)=\left(\mathrm{X}^{s;t}(x,v),\mathrm{V}^{s;t}(x,v)\right)

is the solution to

(5.4)

\left\{\begin{aligned} \frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}\mathrm{X}^{s;t}(x,v)&=\mathrm{V}^{s;t}(x,v),\ \ \mathrm{X}^{t;t}(x,v)=x\in\mathbb{T}^{d},\\[5.69054pt]
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}\mathrm{V}^{s;t}(x,v)&=-\mathrm{V}^{s;t}(x,v)+E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}(s,\mathrm{X}^{s;t}(x,v)),\ \ \mathrm{V}^{t;t}(x,v)=v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.\end{aligned}\right.

After the composition by
(t,x,v)\mapsto(t,\mathrm{X}^{t;0}(x,v),\mathrm{V}^{t;0}(x,v))
, we thus obtain by the method of characteristics

(5.5)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}+\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}+\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}=d\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}-\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{0}-\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{1}.

To deal with the coupling matrix
\mathcal{M}
, we introduce the following object.

Definition 5.6
.

For all
(x,v)
and
s,t\geq 0
, we define the resolvant operator
\mathfrak{N}^{s,t}(x,v)
as the solution
s\mapsto\mathfrak{N}^{s,t}(x,v)
of

(5.6)

\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{s}\mathfrak{N}^{s;t}+\left[\mathcal{M}\circ\mathrm{Z}^{s;0}-d\mathrm{Id}\right]\mathfrak{N}^{s;t}&=0,\\
\mathfrak{N}^{t;t}&=\mathrm{Id}.\end{aligned}\right.

The resolvant is well-defined thanks to the Cauchy-Lipschitz theorem. We also have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{N}^{s;t}(x,v)=e^{d(s-t)}\mathrm{N}^{s;t}(x,v),

where

(5.7)

\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{s}\mathrm{N}^{s;t}+\mathcal{M}\circ\mathrm{Z}^{s;0}\mathrm{N}^{s;t}&=0,\\
\mathrm{N}^{t;t}&=\mathrm{Id}.\end{aligned}\right.

For the upcoming analysis, we need the following bounds on the resolvant.

Lemma 5.7
.

For all
0\leq k<m-3-d/2
, we have

\displaystyle\underset{0\leq s,t\leq T}{\sup}\|\mathrm{N}^{s;t}\|_{\mathrm{W}^{k,\infty}_{x,v}}+\underset{0\leq s,t\leq T}{\sup}\|\partial_{s}\mathrm{N}^{s;t}\|_{\mathrm{W}^{k,\infty}_{x,v}}+\underset{0\leq s,t\leq T}{\sup}\|\partial_{t}\mathrm{N}^{s;t}\|_{\mathrm{W}^{k,\infty}_{x,v}}\leq\Lambda(T,R).

Proof.

We have

\mathrm{N}^{s;t}=\mathrm{Id}-\int_{t}^{s}[\mathcal{M}\circ\mathrm{Z}^{\tau,0}]\mathrm{N}^{\tau;t}\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

By the definition of coefficients of the matrix
\mathcal{M}
, we also have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{M}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{\infty})}\lesssim\underset{|\alpha|\leq 2}{\sup}\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}E\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{\infty})}\leq\Lambda(T,R),

thanks to the estimate (
2.2
) from Lemma
2.7
and by Sobolev embedding (with
m-1>2+d/2
). Grönwall’s lemma leads to the conclusion.
∎

We first obtain the following decomposition for the kinetic moments of the vector
\mathcal{F}
.

Lemma 5.8
.

We have

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{F}(t,x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v=\mathcal{I}_{in}^{0}(t,x)+\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}_{0}}^{0}(t,x)+\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}_{1}}^{0}(t,x)\\
-\int_{0}^{t}e^{d(t-s)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathrm{N}^{t;s}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,v))\mathcal{L}(s,\mathrm{Z}^{s;t}(x,v))\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s,

and

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}v\otimes\mathcal{F}(t,x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v=\mathcal{I}_{in}^{1}(t,x)+\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}_{0}}^{1}(t,x)+\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}_{1}}^{1}(t,x)\\
-\int_{0}^{t}e^{d(t-s)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}v\otimes\mathrm{N}^{t;s}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,v))\mathcal{L}(s,\mathrm{Z}^{s;t}(x,v))\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s,

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{in}^{0}(t,x)

\displaystyle:=e^{dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathrm{N}^{t;0}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,v))\mathcal{F}_{\mid t=0}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,v))\,\mathrm{d}v,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}_{j}}^{0}(t,x)

\displaystyle:=-\int_{0}^{t}e^{d(t-s)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathrm{N}^{t;s}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,v))\mathcal{R}_{j}(s,\mathrm{Z}^{s;t}(x,v))\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s,\ \ j=0,1,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{in}^{1}(t,x)

\displaystyle:=e^{dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}v\otimes\mathrm{N}^{t;0}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,v))\mathcal{F}_{\mid t=0}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,v))\,\mathrm{d}v,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}_{j}}^{1}(t,x)

\displaystyle:=-\int_{0}^{t}e^{d(t-s)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}v\otimes\mathrm{N}^{t;s}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,v))\mathcal{R}_{j}(s,\mathrm{Z}^{s;t}(x,v))\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s;\ \ j=0,1.

Proof.

We only explain the case of the moment of order
0
, the other being similar. Starting from the equation (
5.5
) and using the resolvant operators
\mathfrak{N}
and
\mathrm{N}
defined in (
5.6
) and (
5.7
), we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}(t)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{N}^{t,0}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\mid t=0}-\int_{0}^{t}\mathfrak{N}^{t,s}\left[\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}(s)+\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{0}(s)+\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{1}(s)\right]\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=e^{dt}\mathrm{N}^{t;0}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\mid t=0}-\int_{0}^{t}e^{d(t-s)}\mathrm{N}^{t;s}\left[\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}(s)+\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{0}(s)+\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{1}(s)\right]\,\mathrm{d}s.

After a composition by the map
(t,x,v)\mapsto(t,\mathrm{X}^{0;t}(x,v),\mathrm{V}^{0;t}(x,v))
, we obtain

\mathcal{F}(t)=e^{dt}\left[\mathrm{N}^{t;0}\circ\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}\right]\mathcal{F}_{\mid t=0}\circ\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}-\int_{0}^{t}e^{d(t-s)}\left[\mathrm{N}^{t;s}\circ\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}\right]\left(\mathcal{R}_{0}(s,\mathrm{Z}^{s;t})+\mathcal{R}_{1}(s,\mathrm{Z}^{s;t})\right)\,\mathrm{d}s\\
-\int_{0}^{t}e^{d(t-s)}\left[\mathrm{N}^{t;s}\circ\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}\right]\mathcal{L}(s,\mathrm{Z}^{s;t})\,\mathrm{d}s.

We reach the desired conclusion by integrating in velocity.

∎

5.2.
First remainders

Let us show straightaway that some of the previous terms can be considered as remainders.

Lemma 5.9
.

We have

(5.8)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{I}_{in}^{0}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})}\|+\|\mathcal{I}_{in}^{1}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda(T,R)\|f^{\mathrm{in}}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m+1}_{r}},

(5.9)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}_{0}}^{0}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})}+\|\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}_{0}}^{1}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda(T,R),

(5.10)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}_{1}}^{0}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}+\|\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}_{1}}^{1}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda(T,R).

Proof.

We shall first estimate the term
\mathcal{I}_{in}^{0}
. We have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{I}_{in}^{0}(t,x)|\lesssim e^{dT}\underset{0\leq t\leq T}{\sup}\|\mathrm{N}^{t;s}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{x,v}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\mathcal{F}_{0}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,v))|\,\mathrm{d}v\lesssim\Lambda(T,R)\sum_{I,J}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\partial_{x}^{I}\partial_{v}^{J}f^{\mathrm{in}}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,v))|\,\mathrm{d}v,

thanks to Lemma
5.7
with
k=0
. Using the generalized Minkowski inequality and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we get

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{I}_{in}^{0}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda(T,R)\sum_{I,J}\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\partial_{x}^{I}\partial_{v}^{J}f^{\mathrm{in}}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(\cdot,v))\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}v\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda(T,R)\sum_{I,J}\left\|\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}(1+|v|^{2})^{r}|\partial_{x}^{I}\partial_{v}^{J}f^{\mathrm{in}}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,v))|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}v\right)^{1/2}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T)},

since
2r>d
. We then perform the change of variable
(x,v)\mapsto\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,v)
which yields

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}(1+|v|^{2})^{r}|\partial_{x}^{I}\partial_{v}^{J}f^{\mathrm{in}}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,v))|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}v\lesssim\Lambda(T,R)\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}(1+|\mathrm{V}^{t;0}(x,v)|^{2})^{r}|\partial_{x}^{I}\partial_{v}^{J}f^{\mathrm{in}}(x,v)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}v.

Since

\displaystyle\mathrm{V}^{t;0}(x,v)=e^{-t}v+\int_{0}^{t}e^{\tau-t}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}(\tau,\mathrm{X}^{\tau;t}(x,v))\,\mathrm{d}\tau,

we have by Sobolev embedding

\displaystyle|\mathrm{V}^{t;0}(x,v)|^{2}\leq|v|^{2}+\left|\int_{0}^{t}\|E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}(\tau)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\right|^{2}\lesssim|v|^{2}+T\|E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-1})}^{2}.

By the estimate (
2.2
) from Lemma
2.7
, we get

\displaystyle|\mathrm{V}^{t;0}(x,v)|^{2}\leq|v|^{2}\Lambda(T,R),

and then

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}(1+|\mathrm{V}^{t;0}(x,v)|^{2})^{r}|\partial_{x}^{I}\partial_{v}^{J}f^{\mathrm{in}}(x,v)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda(T,R)\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}(1+|v|^{2})^{r}|\partial_{x}^{I}\partial_{v}^{J}f^{\mathrm{in}}(x,v)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda(T,R)\|f^{\mathrm{in}}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r}}^{2}.

This implies

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{I}_{in}^{0}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}\leq\Lambda(T,R)\|f^{\mathrm{in}}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r}}.

Likewise, using
2r>d+1
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{I}_{in}^{1}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}\lesssim\Lambda(T,R)\|f^{\mathrm{in}}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r}}.

We also have

\displaystyle\left[\mathcal{I}_{in}^{0}\right]_{(I,J)}(t,x)=e^{dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sum_{(K,L)}\mathrm{N}^{t;0}_{(I,J),(K,L)}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,v))\left[\mathcal{F}_{\mid t=0}\right]_{(K,L)}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,v))\,\mathrm{d}v,

and

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}\left[\mathcal{I}_{in}^{0}\right]_{(I,J)}(t,x)

\displaystyle=e^{dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sum_{(K,L)}\nabla_{x}\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,v)\nabla_{x}\mathrm{N}^{t;0}_{(I,J),(K,L)}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,v))\left[\mathcal{F}_{\mid t=0}\right]_{(K,L)}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,v))\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\quad+e^{dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sum_{(K,L)}\mathrm{N}^{t;0}_{(I,J),(K,L)}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,v))\nabla_{x}\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,v)\nabla_{x}\left[\mathcal{F}_{\mid t=0}\right]_{(K,L)}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,v))\,\mathrm{d}v.

The same procedure as before, using Lemma
5.7
with
k=1
and the pointwise bounds (
3.12
)-(
3.13
) from Remark
3.2
, gives

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}\mathcal{I}_{in}^{0}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}+\|\nabla_{x}\mathcal{I}_{in}^{1}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}\lesssim\Lambda(T,R)\|f^{\mathrm{in}}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m+1}_{r}}.

We now estimate the terms
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}_{0}}^{0}
and
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}_{0}}^{1}
. We use the same arguments as before, with an additional Cauchy-Schwarz inequality in time leading to

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}_{0}}^{0}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda(T,R)\sum_{I,J}\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|\mathcal{R}_{0}^{I,J}(s,\mathrm{Z}^{s;t}(\cdot,v)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}v\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T)}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda(T,R)\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\left\|\mathcal{R}_{0}(s)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{0}_{r}}\,\mathrm{d}s\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T)}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda(T,R)T\left\|\mathcal{R}_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{0}_{r})}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda(T,R),

thanks to (
5.2
) in Lemma
5.3
. We obtain the same result for
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}_{0}}^{1}
. Using again (
5.2
) for the first order derivative, we also have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}_{0}}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})}+\|\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}_{0}}^{1}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})}\lesssim\Lambda(T,R).

For the estimate in
\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})
of the last term
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}_{1}}^{0}
, we end up with the conclusion thanks to the inequality (
5.1
) in Lemma
5.3
, combined with the same arguments as before.
∎

Note that the previous lemma does not give any control on
\|\nabla_{x}\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}_{1}}^{0}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}+\|\nabla_{x}\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}_{1}}^{1}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}
. The treatment of these terms requires additional arguments that we will develop in the next subsections. For now, we merely state the result and postpone the proof to the end of Subsection
5.4
.

Lemma 5.10
.

We have

(5.11)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}_{1}}^{0}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})}+\|\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}_{1}}^{1}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})}\leq\Lambda(T,R).

5.3.
The leading terms and the conclusion

In this section, we focus on the following two terms:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}^{0}(t,x):=-\int_{0}^{t}e^{d(t-s)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathrm{N}^{t;s}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,v))\mathcal{L}(s,\mathrm{Z}^{s;t}(x,v))\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s,

and

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}^{1}(t,x):=-\int_{0}^{t}e^{d(t-s)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}v\otimes\mathrm{N}^{t;s}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,v))\mathcal{L}(s,\mathrm{Z}^{s;t}(x,v))\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s.

The goal is to prove that
\mathfrak{L}^{0}
and
\mathfrak{L}^{1}
can be decomposed as a sum of a leading term and a remainder in
\mathrm{L}^{2}_{T}\mathrm{H}^{1}
. This will imply the result stated in Proposition
5.1
. Since the treament of
\mathfrak{L}^{1}
is similar, we focus on
\mathfrak{L}^{0}
.

First, we have the following decomposition, which introduces several remainder terms that we shall estimate later on. Recall the definition of the straightening diffeomorphism
\psi_{s,t}(x,v)
from Lemma
3.1
.

Lemma 5.11
.

For
|I|\leq m
, we have

[\mathfrak{L}^{0}]_{(I,0)}(t,x)=-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\partial_{x}^{I}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}(s,x-(t-s)v)\cdot\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s\\
+\mathscr{R}_{I}^{\mathrm{Diff}}(t,x)+\mathscr{R}_{I,1}^{\mathrm{Duha}}(t,x)+\mathscr{R}_{I,2}^{\mathrm{Duha}}(t,x),

with

\displaystyle\mathscr{R}_{I}^{\mathrm{Diff}}(t,x)

\displaystyle:=-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left[\partial_{x}^{I}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}(s,x+(1-e^{t-s})v)-\partial_{x}^{I}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}(s,x-(t-s)v)\right]\cdot\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle\mathscr{R}_{I,1}^{\mathrm{Duha}}(t,x)

\displaystyle:=-\sum_{K}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\partial_{x}^{K}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}(s,x+(1-e^{t-s})v)\cdot\mathrm{H}^{K,I}(s,t,x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle\mathscr{R}_{I,2}^{\mathrm{Duha}}(t,x)

\displaystyle:=-\sum_{K}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\partial_{x}^{K}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}(s,x+(1-e^{t-s}v))\cdot\mathfrak{H}^{K,I}(t,s,x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s,

where
\mathrm{H}^{K,I}
and
\mathfrak{H}^{K,I}
are vector fields defined by

(5.12)

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{K,I}(t,s,x,v):=\mathrm{N}^{t;s}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,\psi_{s,t}(x,v)))_{(I,0),(K,0)}\mathrm{J}^{s,t}(x,v)\nabla_{v}f(t,x,\psi_{s,t}(x,v))-\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v),

and

(5.13)

\mathfrak{H}^{K,I}(t,s,x,v):=\int_{s}^{t}e^{d(t-\tau)}\mathrm{N}^{t;s}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,\psi_{s,t}(x,v)))_{(I,0),(K,0)}\\
\big(\nabla_{x}f(\tau,\mathrm{Z}^{\tau;t}(x,\psi_{s,t}(x,v)))-\nabla_{v}f(\tau,\mathrm{Z}^{\tau;t}(x,\psi_{s,t}(x,v)))\big)\,\mathrm{J}^{s,t}(x,v)\,\mathrm{d}\tau,

with

\displaystyle\mathrm{J}^{s,t}(x,w)=|\mathrm{det}\left(\mathrm{D}_{w}\psi_{s,t}(x,w)\right)|.

Proof.

Let
T\in(0,\min\left(T_{\varepsilon}(R),\overline{T}(R)\right)
.
We have for
|I|\leq m

(5.14)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&[\mathfrak{L}^{0}]_{(I,0)}(t,x)\\
&\qquad=-\sum_{(K,L)}\int_{0}^{t}e^{d(t-s)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathrm{N}^{t;s}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,v))_{(I,0),(K,L)}\partial^{K}_{x}\partial^{L}_{v}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}(s,\mathrm{X}^{s;t}(x,v))\cdot\nabla_{v}f(s,\mathrm{Z}^{s;t}(x,v))\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s\\
&\qquad=-\sum_{K}\int_{0}^{t}e^{d(t-s)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathrm{N}^{t;s}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,v))_{(I,0),(K,0)}\partial^{K}_{x}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}(s,\mathrm{X}^{s;t}(x,v))\cdot\nabla_{v}f(s,\mathrm{Z}^{s;t}(x,v))\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s,\end{split}

since
E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}
does not depend on the
v
variable.
By Lemma
2.9
, we know that

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}(\nabla_{v}f)=\nabla_{v}f-\nabla_{x}f.

Invoking Duhamel formula, we get

\displaystyle\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v)=e^{d(t-s)}\nabla_{v}f(s,\mathrm{Z}^{s;t}(x,v))+\int_{s}^{t}e^{d(t-\tau)}\left(\nabla_{v}f(\tau,\mathrm{Z}^{\tau;t}(x,v))-\nabla_{x}f(\tau,\mathrm{Z}^{\tau;t}(x,v))\right)\,\mathrm{d}\tau,

and therefore

\displaystyle e^{d(t-s)}\nabla_{v}f(s,\mathrm{Z}^{s;t}(x,v))=\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v)+\int_{s}^{t}e^{d(t-\tau)}\left(\nabla_{x}f(\tau,\mathrm{Z}^{\tau;t}(x,v))-\nabla_{v}f(\tau,\mathrm{Z}^{\tau;t}(x,v))\right)\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

Inserting this expression in (
5.14
) yields

(5.15)

\displaystyle\begin{split}[\mathfrak{L}^{0}]_{(I,0)}(t,x)&=\mathscr{L}_{I}^{1}(t,x)+\mathscr{L}_{I}^{2}(t,x),\\
\end{split}

where

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{I}^{1}(t,x)

\displaystyle:=-\sum_{K}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathrm{N}^{t;s}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,v))_{(I,0),(K,0)}\partial^{K}_{x}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}(s,\mathrm{X}^{s;t}(x,v))\cdot\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{I}^{2}(t,x)

\displaystyle:=-\sum_{K}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{s}^{t}e^{d(t-\tau)}\mathrm{N}^{t;s}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,v))_{(I,0),(K,0)}\partial^{K}_{x}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}(s,\mathrm{X}^{s;t}(x,v))

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad{\cdot}\left(\nabla_{x}f(\tau,\mathrm{Z}^{\tau;t}(x,v))-\nabla_{v}f(\tau,\mathrm{Z}^{\tau;t}(x,v))\right)\,\mathrm{d}\tau\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s.

Let us transform these two expressions in order to make the terms
\mathscr{R}_{I}^{1}(t,x)
,
\mathscr{R}_{I}^{2}(t,x)
and
\mathscr{R}_{I}^{3}(t,x)
appear.

\bullet
We first focus on
\mathscr{L}_{I}^{1}
, which will produce the leading term in the result. Using the change of variable
v=\psi_{s,t}(x,w)
coming from Lemma
3.1
, we have (since
t\leq\overline{T}(R)
)

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{I}^{1}(t,x)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{K}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathrm{N}^{t;s}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,\psi_{s,t}(x,v)))_{(I,0),(K,0)}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\partial_{x}^{K}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}(s,x+(1-e^{t-s})v)\cdot\nabla_{v}f(t,x,\psi_{s,t}(x,v))\mathrm{J}^{s,t}(x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s,

where
\mathrm{J}^{s,t}(x,w)=|\mathrm{det}\left(\mathrm{D}_{w}\psi_{s,t}(x,w)\right)|
. We obtain

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{I}^{1}(t,x)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{K}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\partial_{x}^{K}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}(s,x+(1-e^{t-s})v)\cdot\nabla_{v}f(t,x,\psi_{t,t}(x,v))

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\mathrm{N}^{t;t}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,\psi_{t,t}(x,v)))_{(I,0),(K,0)}\mathrm{J}^{t,t}(x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{K}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\partial_{x}^{K}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}(s,x+(1-e^{t-s})v)\cdot\mathrm{H}^{K,I}(t,s,x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s,

with

\mathrm{H}^{K,I}(t,s,x,v):=\mathrm{N}^{t;s}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,\psi_{s,t}(x,v)))_{(I,0),(K,0)}\mathrm{J}^{s,t}(x,v)\nabla_{v}f(t,x,\psi_{s,t}(x,v))\\[5.69054pt]
\quad-\mathrm{N}^{t;t}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,\psi_{t,t}(x,v)))_{(I,0),(K,0)}\mathrm{J}^{t,t}(x,v)\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v).

Now observe that since
\mathrm{N}^{t;t}=\mathrm{Id}
and
\psi_{t,t}=\mathrm{Id}
, we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{v}f(t,x,\psi_{t,t}(x,v))\mathrm{N}^{t;t}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,\psi_{t,t}(x,v)))_{(I,0),(K,0)}\mathrm{J}^{t,t}(x,v)=\mathbf{1}_{I=K}\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v),

therefore

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{I}^{1}(t,x)

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\partial_{x}^{I}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}(s,x+(1-e^{t-s})v)\cdot\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{K}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\partial_{x}^{K}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}(s,x+(1-e^{t-s})v)\cdot\mathrm{H}^{K,I}(s,t,x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\partial_{x}^{I}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}(s,x-(t-s)v)\cdot\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s+\mathscr{R}_{I}^{\mathrm{Diff}}(t,x)+\mathscr{R}_{I,1}^{\mathrm{Duha}}(t,x).

\bullet
To deal with the term
\mathscr{L}_{I}^{2}
, we apply again the change of variable
v=\psi_{s,t}(x,w)
from Lemma
3.1
and get (since
T\leq\overline{T}(R)
)

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{I}^{2}(t,x)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{K}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\partial_{x}^{K}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}(s,x+(1-e^{t-s}v))\cdot\mathfrak{H}^{K,I}(t,s,x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s,

where

\mathfrak{H}^{K,I}(t,s,x,v):=\int_{s}^{t}e^{d(t-\tau)}\mathrm{N}^{t;s}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,\psi_{s,t}(x,v)))_{(I,0),(K,0)}\\
\big(\nabla_{x}f(\tau,\mathrm{Z}^{\tau;t}(x,\psi_{s,t}(x,v)))-\nabla_{v}f(\tau,\mathrm{Z}^{\tau;t}(x,\psi_{s,t}(x,v)))\big)\,\mathrm{J}^{s,t}(x,v)\,\mathrm{d}\tau,

which means that
\mathscr{L}_{I}^{2}(t,x)=\mathscr{R}_{I,2}^{\mathrm{Duha}}(t,x)
. Combining the previous decompositions eventually yields the conclusion.

∎

We now have the following lemma, which is the continuation of Lemma
5.11
, in which we express
[\mathfrak{L}^{0}]_{(I,0)}
as a sum of a leading term and a remainder which is controlled in
\mathrm{L}^{2}_{T}\mathrm{H}^{1}
. The proof, which is rather lengthy and technical, is based on the smoothing estimates of Section
4
. We refer to Subsection
5.4
where the proof is postponed.

Lemma 5.12
.

We have for all
|I|=m
,

\displaystyle[\mathfrak{L}^{0}]_{(I,0)}(t,x)=p^{\prime}(\varrho(t,x))\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla_{x}[\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}^{I}\varrho](s,x-(t-s)v)\cdot\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s+\mathscr{R}_{I}(t,x),

with

\displaystyle\|\mathscr{R}_{I}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T,\mathrm{H}^{1})}\leq\Lambda(T,R).

We can finally proceed with the proof of Proposition
5.1
.

Proof of Proposition
5.1
.

We only treat the case of
\partial_{x}^{I}\rho_{f}
, the one of
\partial_{x}^{I}j_{f}
being similar. First invoking Lemma
5.8
, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{x}^{I}\rho_{f}

\displaystyle=[\mathcal{I}_{in}^{0}]_{(I,0)}+[\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}_{0}}^{0}]_{(I,0)}+[\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}_{1}}^{0}]_{(I,0)}+[\mathfrak{L}^{0}]_{(I,0)}.

Thanks to Lemmas
5.9
–
5.10
–
5.12
, we infer

\displaystyle\partial_{x}^{I}\rho_{f}(t,x)

\displaystyle=p^{\prime}(\varrho(t,x))\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla_{x}[\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}^{I}\varrho](s,x-(t-s)v)\cdot\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+[\mathcal{I}_{0}^{0}]_{(I,0)}(t,x)+[\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}_{0}}^{0}]_{(I,0)}(t,x)+[\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}_{1}}^{0}]_{(I,0)}(t,x)+\mathscr{R}_{I}(t,x),

where the previous remainders are estimated as

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{I}_{in}^{0}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})}\|

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda(T,R)\|f^{\mathrm{in}}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m+1}_{r}},

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}_{0}}^{0}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})}+\|\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}_{1}}^{0}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda(T,R),

\displaystyle\|\mathscr{R}_{I}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T,\mathrm{H}^{1})}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda(T,R).

This concludes the proof.
∎

5.4.
Estimates of the last remainders

In this subsection, we mainly aim at giving a proof for Lemma
5.12
and Lemma
5.10
, that we have previously stated. We shall rely on the crucial smoothing estimates derived in Section
4
to treat the different remainders. Broadly speaking, there are three types of terms requiring a gain of regularity:

-

Type I
: terms that will be treated thanks to the continuity estimate of Proposition
4.1
, as in
[
HKR16
]
, and of Proposition
4.4
. It will be used for the terms
\nabla_{x}\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}_{1}}^{0}
and
\nabla_{x}\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}_{1}}^{1}
in the proof of Lemma
5.10
.

-

Type II
: terms involving a kernel vanishing on the diagonal in time. They will be treated by the regularization estimate of Proposition
4.5
. Such terms will appear in the proof of Lemma
5.12
, as well as for the remainders
\mathscr{R}_{I}^{\mathrm{Diff}},\mathscr{R}_{I,1}^{\mathrm{Duha}}
and
\mathscr{R}_{I,2}^{\mathrm{Duha}}
.

-

Type III
: terms involving the difference between the integral operators
\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{free}}
and
\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{fric}}
(see Section
4
. They will be handled thanks to the regularization estimate of Proposition
4.7
. They will also appear in the treatment of the different remainders.

Let us mention that the previous gains require to control a fixed number of derivatives of the kernels that are involved (see Section
4
).

Recall also the expression

\displaystyle E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}(t,x)=u(t,x)-p^{\prime}(\varrho)\nabla_{x}\left[\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\varrho\right](t,x),

as well as Definition
2.8
for shifted indices. In order to check that the assumptions of the smoothing estimates of Section
4
are satisfied, it is convenient to have the following result.

Lemma 5.13
.

For any
K\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
such that
|K|>0
, we have

\partial_{x}^{K}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}=-p^{\prime}(\varrho)\nabla_{x}[\partial_{x}^{K}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\varrho]+\partial_{x}^{K}u-\sum_{\ell=0}^{\lfloor\frac{|K|-1}{2}\rfloor}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1,{\cdots},d\\
\beta\in\mathbb{B}_{K}(i,\ell)\end{subarray}}\binom{K}{\widehat{\beta}^{i}}\nabla_{x}(\partial_{x}^{\overline{K}^{i}-\beta}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\varrho)\nabla_{x}(\partial_{x}^{\beta}p^{\prime}(\varrho))\cdot e_{i}\\
-\sum_{\ell=\lfloor\frac{|K|-1}{2}\rfloor+1}^{|K|-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1,{\cdots},d\\
\beta\in\mathbb{B}_{K}(i,\ell)\end{subarray}}\binom{K}{\widehat{\beta}^{i}}\nabla_{x}(\partial_{x}^{\beta}p^{\prime}(\varrho))\cdot e_{i}\nabla_{x}(\partial_{x}^{\overline{K}^{i}-\beta}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\varrho),

where

\displaystyle\mathbb{B}_{K}(i,\ell):=\left\{\beta\in\mathbb{N}^{d}\mid|\beta|=\ell,\ \ 0<\widehat{\beta}^{i}\leq K\right\},\ \ i=1,{\cdots},d,\ \ \ell=0,{\cdots},|K|-1.

Proof.

The proof directly follows from Leibniz formula, which provides

\displaystyle\partial_{x}^{K}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}=-p^{\prime}(\varrho)\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{K}\varrho+\partial_{x}^{K}u-\sum_{\ell=0}^{|K|-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1,{\cdots},d\\
\beta\in\mathbb{B}_{K}(i,\ell)\end{subarray}}\binom{K}{\widehat{\beta}^{i}}\nabla_{x}(\partial_{x}^{\beta}p^{\prime}(\varrho))\cdot e_{i}\nabla_{x}(\partial_{x}^{\overline{K}^{i}-\beta}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\varrho),

and from which we infer the conclusion.
∎

In the next lemma, we show how to obtain the leading term of Lemma
5.12
(up to some good remainder) from the integral term of Lemma
5.11
.

Lemma 5.14
.

We have

-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\partial_{x}^{I}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}(s,x-(t-s)v)\cdot\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s\\
=p^{\prime}(\varrho(t,x))\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla_{x}[\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}^{I}\varrho](s,x-(t-s)v)\cdot\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s+\widetilde{\mathscr{R}}_{I}(t,x),

where the remainder
\widetilde{\mathscr{R}}_{I}
satisfies

\displaystyle\left\|\widetilde{\mathscr{R}}_{I}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})}\leq\Lambda(T,R).

Proof.

Let us introduce the following vector fields

\displaystyle G_{1}(s,t,x,v)=[p^{\prime}(\varrho(s,x-(t-s)v)-p^{\prime}(\varrho(t,x))]\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v),

and

\displaystyle G_{3,i}^{\beta}(s,t,x,v)

\displaystyle:=\Big(\nabla_{x}(\partial_{x}^{\beta}p^{\prime}(\varrho))(s,x-(t-s)v)\cdot e_{i}\Big)\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v),

\displaystyle G_{4,i}^{K,\beta}(s,t,x,v)

\displaystyle:=\Big(\nabla_{x}(\partial_{x}^{\overline{K}^{i}-\beta}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\varrho)(s,x-(t-s)v)\cdot\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v)\Big)e_{i},

for

\displaystyle\beta\in\mathbb{B}_{K}(i,\ell)=\left\{\beta\in\mathbb{N}^{d}\mid|\beta|=\ell,\ \ 0<\widehat{\beta}^{i}\leq K\right\},\ \ i=1,{\cdots},d,\ \ \ell=0,{\cdots},|K|-1.

Thanks to Lemma
5.13
, we can write

-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\partial_{x}^{I}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}(s,x-(t-s)v)\cdot\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s\\
=p^{\prime}(\varrho(t,x))\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla_{x}[\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}^{I}\varrho](s,x-(t-s)v)\cdot\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s+\mathbf{S_{1}}(t,x)+\mathbf{S_{2}}(t,x)+\mathbf{S_{3}}(t,x)+\mathbf{S_{4}}(t,x),

where

\displaystyle\mathbf{S_{1}}(t,x)

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{K}_{G_{1}}^{\mathrm{free}}[\partial_{x}^{I}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\varrho],

\displaystyle\mathbf{S_{2}}(t,x)

\displaystyle:=-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\partial_{x}^{I}u(s,x-(t-s)v)\cdot\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle\mathbf{S_{3}}(t,x)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\ell=0}^{\lfloor\frac{|I|-1}{2}\rfloor}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1,{\cdots},d\\
\beta\in\mathbb{B}_{I}(i,\ell)\end{subarray}}\binom{I}{\widehat{\beta}^{i}}\mathrm{K}_{G_{3,i}^{\beta}}^{\mathrm{free}}\left[\partial_{x}^{\overline{I}^{i}-\beta}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\varrho\right],

\displaystyle\mathbf{S_{4}}(t,x)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\ell=\lfloor\frac{|I|-1}{2}\rfloor+1}^{|I|-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1,{\cdots},d\\
\beta\in\mathbb{B}_{I}(i,\ell)\end{subarray}}\binom{I}{\widehat{\beta}^{i}}\mathrm{K}_{G_{4,i}^{\overline{I}^{i},\beta}}^{\mathrm{free}}\Big[\partial_{x}^{\beta}p^{\prime}(\varrho)\Big].

The treatment of the term
\mathbf{S_{2}}
will follow from a straightforward estimate. The terms
\mathbf{S_{3}}
and
\mathbf{S_{4}}
are terms of
Type I
and we will use the continuity estimates provided by Proposition
4.1
. The term
\mathbf{S_{1}}
, which already contains
I
derivatives of
\varrho
, is a term of
Type II
(since
G_{1}(t,t,x,v)=0
) and we will rely on the regularization estimate of Proposition
4.5
.

\bullet

Estimate of
\mathbf{S_{1}}
: Since
G_{1}(t,t,x,v)=0
, we use Proposition
4.5
to get

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{S_{1}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+T)\underset{0\leq s,t\leq T}{\sup}\,\|\partial_{s}G_{1}(s,t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\ell}_{\sigma}}\|\partial_{x}^{I}\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+T)\underset{0\leq s,t\leq T}{\sup}\,\|\partial_{s}G_{1}(s,t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\ell}_{\sigma}}\|\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})},

for
\ell>7+d
and
\sigma>d/2
. A direct computation gives

\displaystyle\partial_{s}G_{1}(s,t,x,v)=p^{\prime\prime}(\varrho)\left[\partial_{s}\varrho+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\varrho\right](s,x-(t-s)v)\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v).

We thus have for all
0\leq s,t\leq T

\displaystyle\|\partial_{s}G_{1}(s,t,x,v)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\ell}_{\sigma}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{T}\sum_{|\mu|+|\nu|\leq\ell}\sum_{\gamma=0}^{\mu+\nu}\left(\|\partial_{x}^{\gamma}(p^{\prime\prime}(\varrho)\partial_{s}\varrho(s))\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}^{2}+\|\partial_{x}^{\gamma}(p^{\prime\prime}(\varrho)\nabla_{x}\varrho(s))\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\langle v\rangle^{2\sigma+2}|\partial^{\mu+\nu-\gamma}_{x,v}\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{T}\left(\left\|p^{\prime\prime}(\varrho)\partial_{s}\varrho(s)\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{k}}^{2}+\left\|p^{\prime\prime}(\varrho)\nabla_{x}\varrho(s)\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{k}}^{2}\right)\|f(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m-1}_{\sigma+1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{T}\left\|p^{\prime\prime}(\varrho(s))\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{k}}^{2}\left(\left\|\partial_{s}\varrho(s)\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{k}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla_{x}\varrho(s)\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{k}}^{2}\right)\|f(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m-1}_{\sigma+1}}^{2},

for
k>\frac{d}{2}+\ell>\frac{d}{2}+1+d
and
m-1>\ell>1+d
. Using the equation satisfied by
\varrho
, we get

\displaystyle\left\|p^{\prime\prime}(\varrho(s))\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{k}}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda\left(\left\|\varrho(s)\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{k}}\right)\left\|\varrho(s)\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{k}},

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{s}\varrho(s)\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{k}}+\left\|\nabla_{x}\varrho(s)\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{k}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|u\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{k}}\left\|\nabla_{x}\varrho\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{k}}+\left\|\frac{\varrho}{1-\rho_{f}}\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{k}}\left\|\mathrm{div}_{x}\left(j_{f}-\rho_{f}u+u\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{k}}+\left\|\varrho\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{k+1}},

thanks to the algebra property of
\mathrm{H}^{k}
. Taking
k+1<m-3
and using the bootstrap assumption combined with (the proof) of Lemma
2.20
, we obtain

\displaystyle\underset{0\leq s,t\leq T}{\sup}\,\|\partial_{s}G_{1}(s,t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\ell}_{\sigma}}\lesssim\Lambda(T,R).

\bullet

Estimate of
\mathbf{S_{2}}
:
Using the generalized Minkowski inequality followed by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have for
r>d/2

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{S_{2}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}(1+|v|^{2})^{r}|\partial_{x}^{I}u(s,x-(t-s)v)|^{2}|\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}v\right)^{1/2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T)}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}(1+|v|^{2})^{r}|\partial_{x}^{I}u(s,y)|^{2}|\nabla_{v}f(t,y+(t-s)v,v)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}v\right)^{1/2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T)}

\displaystyle\leq\underset{t\in[0,T]}{\sup}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(1+|v|^{2})^{r}\|\nabla_{v}f(t,\cdot,v)\|^{2}_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}\,\mathrm{d}v\right)^{1/2}\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\|\partial_{x}^{I}u(s)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{m-1}_{r})}\|u\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})},

by Sobolev embedding in the last line, since
m-1>1+d/2
. This yields

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{S_{2}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}\lesssim\Lambda(T,R).

Likewise, we have since
m-1>2+d/2

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{x}\mathbf{S_{2}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{m-1}_{r})}\|u\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})}+\|f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{m-1}_{r})}\|u\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m+1})}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda(T,R),

which gives the conclusion.

\bullet

Estimate of
\mathbf{S_{3}}
and
\mathbf{S_{4}}
: for all
0\leq|\beta|\leq\lfloor\frac{|I|-1}{2}\rfloor
and
i=1,\cdots,d
, we use Proposition
4.1
(after taking one derivative in space) to get

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{free}}_{G_{3,i}^{\beta}}\left[\partial_{x}^{\overline{I}^{i}-\beta}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\varrho\right]\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})}\lesssim\underset{0\leq s,t\leq T}{\sup}\|G_{3,i}^{\beta}(t,s)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\ell+1}_{\sigma}}\left\|\partial_{x}^{\overline{I}^{i}-\beta}\varrho\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})},

for
\ell>1+d
and
\sigma>d/2
therefore

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{free}}_{G_{3,i}^{\beta}}\left[\partial_{x}^{\overline{I}^{i}-\beta}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\varrho\right]\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})}\lesssim\underset{0\leq s,t\leq T}{\sup}\|G_{3,i}^{\beta}(t,s)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\ell+1}_{\sigma}}\left\|\varrho\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})}.

We have for all
0\leq s,t\leq T

\displaystyle\|G_{3,i}^{\beta}(t,s)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\ell+1}_{\sigma}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{T}\sum_{|\mu|+|\nu|\leq\ell+1}\sum_{\gamma=0}^{\mu+\nu}\|\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\nabla_{x}(\partial_{x}^{\beta}p^{\prime}(\varrho))(s)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\langle v\rangle^{2\sigma}|\partial^{\mu+\nu-\gamma}_{x,v}\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{T}\|p^{\prime}(\varrho)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{k})}^{2}\|f(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m-1}_{\sigma}}^{2},

provided that
m-1\geq\ell+2
and
k>\frac{d}{2}+|\gamma|+1+|\beta|
. Since
\ell>1+d
and
\frac{d}{2}+|\gamma|+1+|\beta|\leq\frac{d}{2}+\ell+2+\frac{m-1}{2}
, a condition such as
3d+9<m
ensures that

\displaystyle\|G_{3,i}^{\beta}(t,s)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\ell+1}_{\sigma}}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{T}\|p^{\prime}(\varrho)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-2})}\|f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{m-1}_{\sigma})}\leq\Lambda(T,R).

thanks to (
2.1
) from Lemma
2.7
, Sobolev embedding, and Lemma
2.20
. We thus obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{free}}_{G_{3,i}^{\beta}}\left[\partial_{x}^{\overline{I}^{i}-\beta}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\varrho\right]\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})}\leq\Lambda(T,R).

We proceed in the same way for
\mathbf{S_{4}}
: for all
\lfloor\frac{|I|-1}{2}\rfloor+1\leq|\beta|\leq|I|-1
and
i=1,\cdots,d
, we take one derivative in space and use Proposition
4.1
to write for
\ell>1+d
and
\sigma>d/2

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{free}}_{G_{4,i}^{\overline{I}^{i},\beta}}\left[\partial_{x}^{\beta}p^{\prime}(\varrho)\right]\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})}

\displaystyle\leq\underset{0\leq s,t\leq T}{\sup}\|G_{4,i}^{\overline{I}^{i},\beta}(t,s)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\ell+1}_{\sigma}}\left\|p^{\prime}(\varrho)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})}.

The kernel
G_{4,i}^{\overline{I}^{i},\beta}
is estimated as before: using Leibniz rule, we have for all
0\leq s,t\leq T

\displaystyle\|G_{4,i}^{\overline{I}^{i},\beta}(t,s)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\ell+1}_{\sigma}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{T}\|\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{k})}^{2}\|f(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m-1}_{\sigma}}^{2},

provided that
m-1>2+\ell
and
k>\frac{d}{2}+|\gamma|+1+|\overline{I}^{i}|-|\beta|
. Since
\ell>1+d
and

\frac{d}{2}+|\gamma|+1+|\overline{I}^{i}|-|\beta|\leq\frac{d+2\ell+m+1}{2},

a condition such as
3d+9<m
ensures that

\displaystyle\|G_{4,i}^{\overline{I}^{i},\beta}(t,s)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\ell+1}_{\sigma}}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{T}\|\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-2})}\|f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{m-1}_{\sigma})}.

We thus obtain by Sobolev embedding, the bound (
2.1
) in Lemma
2.7
and Lemma
2.20
.

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{free}}_{G_{4,i}^{\overline{I}^{i},\beta}}\left[\partial_{x}^{\beta}p^{\prime}(\varrho)\right]\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})}\leq\Lambda(T,R).

∎

The remaining task is to show that the remainder terms
\mathscr{R}_{I}^{\mathrm{Diff}},\mathscr{R}_{I,1}^{\mathrm{Duha}}
and
\mathscr{R}_{I,2}^{\mathrm{Duha}}
introduced in Lemma
5.11
are well-controlled in
\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})
. For the first one, we have the following lemma.

Lemma 5.15
.

We have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathscr{R}_{I}^{\mathrm{Diff}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})}\leq\Lambda(T,R).

Proof.

In view of Lemma
5.13
, let us write for
0\leq|\mu|\leq\lfloor\frac{|K|-1}{2}\rfloor
and
\lfloor\frac{|K|-1}{2}\rfloor+1\leq|\nu|\leq|K|-1

\displaystyle\big(\nabla_{x}[\partial_{x}^{\overline{K}^{i}-\mu}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\varrho]\nabla_{x}[\partial_{x}^{\mu}p^{\prime}(\varrho)]{\cdot}e_{i}+\nabla_{x}[\partial_{x}^{\mu}p^{\prime}(\varrho)]{\cdot}e_{i}\nabla_{x}[\partial_{x}^{\overline{K}^{i}-\nu}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\varrho]\big)(s,x-(t-s)v)\cdot\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v)

\displaystyle\qquad-\big(\nabla_{x}[\partial_{x}^{\overline{K}^{i}-\mu}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\varrho]\nabla_{x}[\partial_{x}^{\mu}p^{\prime}(\varrho)]{\cdot}e_{i}+\nabla_{x}[\partial_{x}^{\mu}p^{\prime}(\varrho)]{\cdot}e_{i}\nabla_{x}[\partial_{x}^{\overline{K}^{i}-\nu}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\varrho]\big)(s,x+(1-e^{t-s})v)\cdot\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v)

\displaystyle=\Big(\nabla_{x}[\partial_{x}^{\overline{K}^{i}-\mu}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\varrho](s,x-(t-s)v)-\nabla_{x}[\partial_{x}^{\overline{K}^{i}-\mu}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\varrho](s,x+(1-e^{t-s})v)\Big)\cdot G_{8,\mu,i}(s,t,x,v)

\displaystyle\quad+\nabla_{x}[\partial_{x}^{\overline{K}^{i}-\mu}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\varrho](s,x+(1-e^{t-s})v)\cdot G_{9,\mu,i}(s,t,x,v)

\displaystyle\quad+\nabla_{x}[\partial_{x}^{\nu}p^{\prime}(\varrho)](s,x-(t-s)v)\cdot G_{10,\nu,i}^{K}(s,t,x,v)

\displaystyle\quad+\Big(\nabla_{x}[\partial_{x}^{\nu}p^{\prime}(\varrho)](s,x-(t-s)v)-\nabla_{x}[\partial_{x}^{\nu}p^{\prime}(\varrho)](s,x+(1-e^{t-s})v)\Big)\cdot G_{11,\nu,i}^{K}(s,t,x,v).

where

\displaystyle G_{8,\mu,i}(s,t,x,v)

\displaystyle:=\Big(\nabla_{x}[\partial_{x}^{\mu}p^{\prime}(\varrho)](s,x-(t-s)v){\cdot}e_{i}\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v)\Big),

\displaystyle G_{9,\mu,i}(s,t,x,v)

\displaystyle:=\Big((\nabla_{x}[\partial_{x}^{\mu}p^{\prime}(\varrho)]{\cdot}e_{i}-\nabla_{x}[\partial_{x}^{\mu}p^{\prime}(\varrho)](s,x+(1-e^{t-s})v){\cdot}e_{i})\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v)\Big),

\displaystyle G_{10,\nu,i}^{K}(s,t,x,v)

\displaystyle:=\Big((\nabla_{x}[\partial_{x}^{\overline{K}^{i}-\nu}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\varrho](s,x-(t-s)v)-\nabla_{x}[\partial_{x}^{\overline{K}^{i}-\nu}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\varrho](s,x+(1-e^{t-s})v)){\cdot}\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v)e_{i}\Big),

\displaystyle G_{11,\nu,i}^{K}(s,t,x,v)

\displaystyle:=\Big(\nabla_{x}[\partial_{x}^{\overline{K}^{i}-\nu}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\varrho](s,x+(1-e^{t-s})v){\cdot}\nabla_{x}f(t,x,v)e_{i}\Big).

By Lemma
5.13
, we can thus rewrite

\displaystyle\mathscr{R}_{I}^{\mathrm{Diff}}=\mathbf{S_{5}}+\mathbf{S_{6}}+\mathbf{S_{7}}+\mathbf{S_{8}}+\mathbf{S_{9}}+\mathbf{S_{10}}+\mathbf{S_{11}},

where

\displaystyle\mathbf{S_{5}}(t,x)

\displaystyle:=-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left[\partial_{x}^{I}u(s,x+(1-e^{t-s})v)-\partial_{x}^{I}u(s,x-(t-s)v)\right]\cdot\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s,

and

\displaystyle\mathbf{S_{6}}

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{K}_{G_{6}}^{\mathrm{free}}[\partial_{x}^{I}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\varrho]-\mathrm{K}_{G_{6}}^{\mathrm{fric}}[\partial_{x}^{I}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\varrho],\ \ \ ÷G_{6}(t,s,x,v):=p^{\prime}(\varrho(s,x-(t-s)v))\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v),

\displaystyle\mathbf{S_{7}}

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{K}_{G_{7}}^{\mathrm{fric}}[\partial_{x}^{I}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\varrho],\ \ G_{7}(t,s,x,v):=\left[(p^{\prime}(\varrho)(s,x-(t-s)v)-p^{\prime}(\varrho)(s,x+(1-e^{t-s})v))\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v)\right],

\displaystyle\mathbf{S_{8}}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\ell=0}^{\lfloor\frac{|I|-1}{2}\rfloor}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1,{\cdots},d\\
\beta\in\mathbb{B}_{I}(i,\ell)\end{subarray}}\binom{I}{\widehat{\beta}^{i}}\left(\mathrm{K}_{G_{8,\beta,i}}^{\mathrm{free}}[\partial_{x}^{\overline{I}^{i}-\beta}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\varrho]-\mathrm{K}_{G_{8,\beta,i}}^{\mathrm{fric}}[\partial_{x}^{\overline{I}^{i}-\beta}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\varrho]\right),

\displaystyle\mathbf{S_{9}}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\ell=0}^{\lfloor\frac{|I|-1}{2}\rfloor}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1,{\cdots},d\\
\beta\in\mathbb{B}_{I}(i,\ell)\end{subarray}}\binom{I}{\widehat{\beta}^{i}}\mathrm{K}_{G_{9,\beta,i}}^{\mathrm{fric}}\left[\partial_{x}^{\overline{I}^{i}-\beta}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\varrho\right],

\displaystyle\mathbf{S_{10}}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\ell=\lfloor\frac{|I|-1}{2}\rfloor+1}^{|I|-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1,{\cdots},d\\
\beta\in\mathbb{B}_{I}(i,\ell)\end{subarray}}\binom{I}{\widehat{\beta}^{i}}\mathrm{K}_{G_{10,\beta,i}^{I}}^{\mathrm{free}}\left[\partial_{x}^{\beta}p^{\prime}(\varrho)\right],

\displaystyle\mathbf{S_{11}}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\ell=\lfloor\frac{|I|-1}{2}\rfloor+1}^{|I|-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1,{\cdots},d\\
\beta\in\mathbb{B}_{I}(i,\ell)\end{subarray}}\binom{I}{\widehat{\beta}^{i}}\left(\mathrm{K}_{G_{11,\beta,i}^{I}}^{\mathrm{free}}[\partial_{x}^{\beta}p^{\prime}(\varrho)]-\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{fric}}_{G_{11,\beta,i}^{I}}[\partial_{x}^{\beta}p^{\prime}(\varrho)]\right).

Let us explain how to estimate each of these terms. The term
\mathbf{S_{5}}
will be estimated by a direct proof. All the other terms actually require the use of the smoothing estimates coming either from Proposition
4.5
or Proposition
4.7
:

-

for
\mathbf{S_{6}},\mathbf{S_{8}}
and
\mathbf{S_{11}}
, we have the difference of the operators
\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{free}}
and
\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{fric}}
which appears: these terms are therefore of
Type III
and we will use Proposition
4.7
;

-

since the kernels
G_{7}
,
G_{9}
and
G_{10}
vanish on the diagonal
\{s=t\}
, the terms
\mathbf{S_{7}},\mathbf{S_{9}}
and
\mathbf{S_{10}}
are of
Type II
and we will appeal to Proposition
4.5
.

Let us now turn to the estimates.

\bullet

Estimate of
\mathbf{S_{5}}
: the argument is the same as for
\mathbf{S}_{2}
above. We obtain

\left\|\mathbf{S_{5}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}\lesssim\Lambda(T,R).

\bullet

Estimate of
\mathbf{S_{6}},\mathbf{S_{8}}
and
\mathbf{S_{11}}
: by Proposition
4.7
, we have for all
\ell>8+d
and
\sigma>1+d/2

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{S_{6}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}

\displaystyle\lesssim\underset{0\leq s,t\leq T}{\sup}\|G_{6}(t,s)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\ell}_{\sigma}}\|\partial_{x}^{I}\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda(T,R),

thanks to the bound (
2.1
) in Lemma
2.7
, Lemma
2.20
and provided that we can take
m-2\geq\frac{d}{2}+8+d
.

Likewise, for
\mathbf{S_{8}}
, we use Proposition
4.7
to get

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{S_{8}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})}\lesssim\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq s,t\leq T\\
0\leq|\beta|\leq\lfloor\frac{|I|-1}{2}\rfloor\\
i=1,\cdots,d\end{subarray}}{\sup}\,\|G_{8,\beta,i}(s,t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\ell}_{\sigma}}\|\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-1})},

for all
\ell>8+d
and
\sigma>1+d/2
. As in the treatment of
\mathbf{S_{3}}
above, we deduce

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{S_{8}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})}\lesssim C_{T}\|p^{\prime}(\varrho)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-2})}\|f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{m-1}_{\sigma})}\|\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-1})}\leq\Lambda(T,R),

since
m>3d+21
. We argue in the same way for
\mathbf{S_{11}}
(see the treatment of
\mathbf{S_{4}}
above) and obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{S_{11}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})}\leq\Lambda(T,R).

\bullet

Estimate of
\mathbf{S_{7}},\mathbf{S_{9}}
and
\mathbf{S_{10}}
: we proceed exactly as in the estimate of
\textbf{S}_{1}
above, since
G_{7}(t,t,x,v)=0
. Here, we have

\partial_{s}G_{7}(s,t,x,v)=\Big[p^{\prime\prime}(\varrho)\left[\partial_{s}\varrho+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\varrho\right](s,x-(t-s)v)\\
-p^{\prime\prime}(\varrho)\left[\partial_{s}\varrho+e^{t-s}v\cdot\nabla_{x}\varrho\right](s,x+(1-e^{t-s})v)\Big]\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v).

Using triangle inequality with Proposition
4.5
, we end up with

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{S_{7}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})}\leq\Lambda(T,R).

For
\mathbf{S_{9}}
, using
G_{9,\beta,i}(t,t,x,v)=0
for
0\leq|\beta|\leq\lfloor\frac{|I|-1}{2}\rfloor
, we also have by Proposition
4.5

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{fric}}_{G_{9,\beta,i}}\left[\partial_{x}^{\overline{I}^{i}-\beta}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\varrho\right]\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+T)\underset{0\leq s,t\leq T}{\sup}\,\|\partial_{s}G_{9,\beta,i}(s,t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\ell}_{\sigma}}\|\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-1})},

for all
\ell>7+d
and
\sigma>d/2
. We then proceed as in the estimate of
\mathbf{S}_{1}
to deal with the time derivative, combined with what we have done for the estimate of
\mathbf{S}_{3}
(since
m>3d+19
for instance) and get

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{S_{9}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})}\leq\Lambda(T,R)\|f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{m-1}_{\sigma})}\|\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-1})}\leq\Lambda(T,R).

Finally, we use the same exact arguments as before for
\mathbf{S_{10}}
(see the estimate of
\mathbf{S}_{4}
above for instance) to get

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{S_{10}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})}\leq\Lambda(T,R)\|\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-1})}\leq\Lambda(T,R).

∎

The second term
\mathscr{R}_{I,1}^{\mathrm{Duha}}
from Lemma
5.11
is estimated thanks to the following lemma.

Lemma 5.16
.

We have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathscr{R}_{I,1}^{\mathrm{Duha}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})}\leq\Lambda(T,R).

Proof.

Let us introduce the following vector fields

\displaystyle G_{14,i,\beta}^{K,I}(s,t,x,v)

\displaystyle:=\Big(\nabla_{x}(\partial_{x}^{\beta}p^{\prime}(\varrho))(s,x+(1-e^{t-s}v))\cdot e_{i}\Big)\mathrm{H}^{K,I}(s,t,x,v),

\displaystyle G_{15,i,\beta}^{K,I}(s,t,x,v)

\displaystyle:=\Big(\nabla_{x}(\partial_{x}^{\overline{K}^{i}-\beta}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\varrho)(s,x+(1-e^{t-s}v))\cdot\mathrm{H}^{K,I}(s,t,x,v)\Big)e_{i},

for

\displaystyle\beta\in\mathbb{B}_{K}(i,\ell)=\left\{\beta\in\mathbb{N}^{d}\mid|\beta|=\ell,\ \ 0<\widehat{\beta}^{i}\leq K\right\},\ \ i=1,{\cdots},d,\ \ \ell=0,{\cdots},|K|-1,

and where we recall the expression of the kernel
H
defined in (
5.12
) by

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{K,I}(t,s,x,v):=\mathrm{N}^{t;s}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,\psi_{s,t}(x,v)))_{(I,0),(K,0)}\mathrm{J}^{s,t}(x,v)\nabla_{v}f(t,x,\psi_{s,t}(x,v))-\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v).

By Lemma
5.13
, we now decompose
\mathscr{R}_{I,1}^{\mathrm{Duha}}
as

\displaystyle\mathscr{R}_{I,1}^{\mathrm{Duha}}=\mathbf{S_{12}}+\mathbf{S_{13}}+\mathbf{S_{14}}+\mathbf{S_{15}},

where

\displaystyle\mathbf{S_{12}}(t,x)

\displaystyle:=-\sum_{K}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\partial_{x}^{K}u(s,x+(1-e^{t-s})v)\cdot\mathrm{H}^{K,I}(t,s,x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s,

and

\displaystyle\mathbf{S_{13}}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{K}\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{fric}}_{G_{13}}\left[\partial_{x}^{K}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\varrho\right],\ \ \ \ G_{13}(t,s,x,v):=p^{\prime}(\varrho)(s,x+(1-e^{t-s})v)\mathrm{H}^{K,I}(t,s,x,v),

\displaystyle\mathbf{S_{14}}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{K}\sum_{\ell=0}^{\lfloor\frac{|K|-1}{2}\rfloor}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1,{\cdots},d\\
\beta\in\mathbb{B}_{K}(i,\ell)\end{subarray}}\binom{I}{\widehat{\beta}^{i}}\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{free}}_{G_{14,i,\beta}^{K,I}}\left[\partial_{x}^{\overline{K}^{i}-\beta}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\varrho\right],

\displaystyle\mathbf{S_{15}}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{K}\sum_{\ell=\lfloor\frac{|K|-1}{2}\rfloor+1}^{|K|-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1,{\cdots},d\\
\beta\in\mathbb{B}_{K}(i,\ell)\end{subarray}}\binom{K}{\widehat{\beta}^{i}}\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{free}}_{G_{15,i,\beta}^{K,I}}\left[\partial_{x}^{\beta}p^{\prime}(\varrho)\right].

Let us estimate each of theses terms. Note that
\mathrm{H}^{K,I}(t,t,x,v)=0
so that the kernels appearing in
\mathbf{S_{13}},\mathbf{S_{14}}
and
\mathbf{S_{15}}
vanish in the diagonal in time: these terms are therefore of
Type II
and we will use the regularization property from Proposition
4.5
to handle them.

\bullet

Estimate of
\mathbf{S_{12}}
: We proceed as in the estimate for
\mathbf{S}_{2}
above and first get for
k>1+\frac{d}{2}

\displaystyle\left\|J_{8}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})}\lesssim\sum_{K}\left\|\mathrm{H}^{K,I}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{k}_{r})}\|u\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m+1})}.

Now observe that for
s,t
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{H}^{K,I}(s,t)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}_{r}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|J^{s,t}\right\|_{\mathrm{W}^{k,\infty}_{x,v}}\left\|\mathrm{N}^{s;t}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(\cdot,\psi_{s,t}))\nabla_{v}f(t,\cdot,\psi_{s,t})\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}_{r}}+\left\|f(t)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k+1}_{r}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|J^{s,t}\right\|_{\mathrm{W}^{k,\infty}_{x,v}}\left(1+\left\|\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}\right\|_{\mathrm{W}^{k,\infty}_{x,v}}\right)\left(1+\left\|\psi_{s,t}\right\|_{\dot{\mathrm{W}}^{k,\infty}_{x,v}}\right)\left\|\mathrm{N}^{t;s}\right\|_{\mathrm{W}^{k,\infty}_{x,v}}\sum_{|\gamma|\leq k}\left\|\partial_{x,v}^{\gamma}(\nabla_{v}f)(t,\cdot,\psi_{s,t})\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{0}_{r}}

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|f(t)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k+1}_{r}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda(T,R)\sum_{|\gamma|\leq k}\left\|\partial_{x,v}^{\gamma}(\nabla_{v}f)(t,\cdot,\psi_{s,t})\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{0}_{r}}+\left\|f(t)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k+1}_{r}},

thanks to the estimate (
3.5
) of Lemma
3.1
, Remark
3.2
and Lemma
5.7
, and since
m>4+d
. To handle the last sum, we write

\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\partial_{x,v}^{\gamma}\nabla_{v}f(t,x,\psi_{s,t}(x,v))|^{2}(1+|v|^{2})^{r}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}v\\
\leq\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\partial_{x,v}^{\gamma}\nabla_{v}f(t,x,\psi_{s,t}(x,v))|^{2}(1+|v-\psi_{s,t}(x,v)|^{2}+|\psi_{s,t}(x,v)|^{2})^{r}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}v,

and use the change of variable
v\mapsto w=\psi_{s,t}(x,v)
from Lemma
3.1
, combined with the bounds (
3.4
) and (
3.3
) to get (choosing
k
such that
k+1\leq m-1
)

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{H}^{K,I}(s,t)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}_{r}}\leq\Lambda(T,R),

Taking a supremum in time we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{S_{12}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})}\leq\Lambda(T,R).

which yields the result.

\bullet

Estimate of
\mathbf{S_{13}},\mathbf{S_{14}}
and
\mathbf{S_{15}}
: Since
\mathrm{H}^{K,I}(t,t,x,v)=0
, we observe that
G_{13}(t,t,x,v)=0
. By Proposition
4.5
, we therefore have for
\ell>7+d
and
\sigma>d/2

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{S_{13}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+T)\sum_{K}\underset{0\leq s,t\leq T}{\sup}\,\|\partial_{s}\left[p^{\prime}(\varrho(s,x+(1-e^{t-s})v))\mathrm{H}^{K,I}(s,t)\right]\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\ell}_{\sigma}}\|\partial_{x}^{K}\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{T}\sum_{K}\underset{0\leq s,t\leq T}{\sup}\,\|p^{\prime\prime}(\varrho)\partial_{s}\varrho(s,x+(1-e^{t-s})v))\mathrm{H}^{K,I}(s,t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\ell}_{\sigma}}\|\partial_{x}^{K}\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{T}\sum_{K}\underset{0\leq s,t\leq T}{\sup}\,\|p^{\prime\prime}(\varrho)\nabla_{x}\varrho(s,x+(1-e^{t-s})v))\mathrm{H}^{K,I}(s,t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\ell}_{\sigma+1}}\|\partial_{x}^{K}\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{T}\sum_{K}\underset{0\leq s,t\leq T}{\sup}\,\|p^{\prime}(\varrho(s,x+(1-e^{t-s})v))\partial_{s}\mathrm{H}^{K,I}(s,t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\ell}_{\sigma}}\|\partial_{x}^{K}\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}.

The two first terms can be handled by similar arguments to the ones used for
\mathbf{S}_{1}
and
\mathbf{S_{12}}
, a fixed number of derivatives being involved. For the last one, we proceed as for the other terms, combined with the arguments used for
\mathbf{S}_{12}
, and write for all
t,s

\displaystyle\|p^{\prime}(\varrho(s,x+(1-e^{t-s})v))\partial_{s}\mathrm{H}^{K,I}(s,t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\ell}_{\sigma}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{T}\|p^{\prime}(\varrho)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-2})}\|\partial_{s}\mathrm{H}^{K,I}(s,t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\ell}_{\sigma}}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda(T,R)\Big(\|J^{s,t}\|_{\mathrm{W}^{\ell,\infty}_{x,v}}\|\nabla_{v}f(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\ell}_{\sigma}}+\|\partial_{s}J^{s,t}\|_{\mathrm{W}^{\ell,\infty}_{x,v}}\|\nabla_{v}f(t,\cdot,\psi_{s,t})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\ell}_{\sigma}}+\|\nabla_{v}f(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\ell+1}_{\sigma}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda(T,R),

since
m>3d/2+11
. This yields

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{S_{13}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})}\leq\Lambda(T,R).

Next, for
\mathbf{S_{14}}
, we use the fact that
G_{14,i,\beta}^{K,I}(t,t,x,v)=0
for
0\leq|\beta|\leq\lfloor\frac{|K|-1}{2}\rfloor
and
i=1,\cdots,d
so that by Proposition
4.5
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{K}_{G_{14,i,\beta}^{K,I}}\left[\partial_{x}^{\overline{K}^{i}-\beta}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\varrho\right]\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+T)\underset{0\leq t,s\leq T}{\sup}\,\left\|\partial_{s}G_{14,i,\beta}^{K,I}(s,t)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\ell}_{\sigma}}\left\|\varrho\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})},

for
\ell>7+d
and
\sigma>d/2
. Next, we have for all
t,s

\left\|\partial_{s}G_{14,i,\beta}^{K,I}(s,t)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\ell}_{\sigma}}\lesssim\left\|\partial_{s}[\nabla_{x}(\partial_{x}^{\beta}p^{\prime}(\varrho))(s,x+(1-e^{t-s})v)]\mathrm{H}^{K,I}(s,t)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\ell}_{\sigma}}\\
+\left\|\nabla_{x}(\partial_{x}^{\beta}p^{\prime}(\varrho))(s,x+(1-e^{t-s})v)\partial_{s}\mathrm{H}^{K,I}(s,t)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\ell}_{\sigma}}.

The first term can be handled by arguments similar to the ones used for
\mathbf{S_{9}}
,
\mathbf{S}_{1}
and for
\mathbf{S_{12}}
. The second can be adressed by the same procedure where one relies on the arguments used for
\mathbf{S_{13}}
. Likewise, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{S_{14}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})}\leq\Lambda(T,R).

∎

We eventually estimate the third term
\mathscr{R}_{I,2}^{\mathrm{Duha}}
from Lemma
5.11
.

Lemma 5.17
.

We have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathscr{R}_{I,2}^{\mathrm{Duha}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})}\leq\Lambda(T,R).

Proof.

We proceed as before by introducing the vector fields

\displaystyle G_{18,i,\beta}^{K,I}(t,s,x,v)

\displaystyle:=\left(\nabla_{x}(\partial_{x}^{\beta}p^{\prime}(\varrho))(s,x+(1-e^{t-s}v))\cdot e_{i}\right)\mathfrak{H}^{K,I}(s,t,x,v),

\displaystyle G_{19,i,\beta}^{K,I}(t,s,x,v)

\displaystyle:=\Big(\nabla_{x}(\partial_{x}^{\overline{K}^{i}-\beta}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\varrho)(s,x+(1-e^{t-s}v))\cdot\mathfrak{H}^{K,I}(s,t,x,v)\Big)e_{i},

where

\displaystyle\beta\in\mathbb{B}_{K}(i,\ell)=\left\{\beta\in\mathbb{N}^{d}\mid|\beta|=\ell,\ \ 0<\widehat{\beta}^{i}\leq K\right\},\ \ i=1,{\cdots},d,\ \ \ell=0,{\cdots},|K|-1.

Let us also recall the expression of the kernel
\mathfrak{H}
defined in (
5.13
) by

\mathfrak{H}^{K,I}(t,s,x,v):=\int_{s}^{t}e^{d(t-\tau)}\mathrm{N}^{t;s}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,\psi_{s,t}(x,v)))_{(I,0),(K,0)}\\
