\big(\nabla_{x}f(\tau,\mathrm{Z}^{\tau;t}(x,\psi_{s,t}(x,v)))-\nabla_{v}f(\tau,\mathrm{Z}^{\tau;t}(x,\psi_{s,t}(x,v)))\big)\,\mathrm{J}^{s,t}(x,v)\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

Thanks to Lemma
5.13
, we can write

\displaystyle\mathscr{R}_{I,2}^{\mathrm{Duha}}=\mathbf{S}_{16}+\mathbf{S}_{17}+\mathbf{S}_{18}+\mathbf{S}_{19},

where

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{16}(t,x)

\displaystyle:=-\sum_{K}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\partial_{x}^{K}u(s,x+(1-e^{t-s}v))\cdot\mathfrak{H}^{K,I}(t,s,x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s,

and

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{17}

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{fric}}[\partial_{x}^{K}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\varrho],\ \ \ G_{17}(t,s,x,v):=p^{\prime}(\varrho(s,x+(1-e^{t-s})v))\mathfrak{H}^{K,I}(t,s,x,v),

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{18}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{K}\sum_{\ell=0}^{\lfloor\frac{|K|-1}{2}\rfloor}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1,{\cdots},d\\
\beta\in\mathbb{B}_{K}(i,\ell)\end{subarray}}\binom{K}{\widehat{\beta}^{i}}\mathrm{K}_{G_{18,i,\beta}^{K,I}}\left[\partial_{x}^{\overline{K}^{i}-\beta}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\varrho\right],

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}_{19}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{K}\sum_{\ell=\lfloor\frac{|K|-1}{2}\rfloor+1}^{|K|-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1,{\cdots},d\\
\beta\in\mathbb{B}_{K}(i,\ell)\end{subarray}}\binom{K}{\widehat{\beta}^{i}}\mathrm{K}_{G_{19,i,\beta}^{K,I}}\left[\partial_{x}^{\beta}p^{\prime}(\varrho)\right].

Let us estimate these terms, as we have done previously. Let us observe that
\mathfrak{H}^{K,I}(t,t,x,v)=0
therefore the terms
\mathbf{S}_{17},\mathbf{S}_{18}
and
\mathbf{S}_{19}
are of
Type II
and we can rely on Proposition
4.5
.

\bullet

Estimate of
\mathbf{S}_{16}
: We mainly proceed as for
\mathbf{S}_{12}
, the kernel being changed from
\mathrm{H}
to
\mathfrak{H}
. Hence, we only have to give an estimate for
\left\|\mathfrak{H}^{K,I}(s,t)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}_{r}}
(for
k
and
r
large enough).
As in the estimate of
\mathbf{S}_{12}
, we use
\mathrm{W}^{k,\infty}_{x,v}
bounds on
\psi_{s,t},\mathrm{Z}^{s;t}
and
\mathrm{N}^{s;t}
from Lemmas
3.1
, Remark
3.2
and Lemma
5.7
to write

\displaystyle\left\|\mathfrak{H}^{K,I}(s,t)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}_{r}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda(T,R)\left\|J^{s,t}\right\|_{\mathrm{W}^{k,\infty}_{x,v}}^{2}\left(1+\left\|\mathrm{N}^{s;t}\right\|_{\mathrm{W}^{k,\infty}_{x,v}}^{2}\right)\left\|\int_{s}^{t}\left[\nabla_{x}f(\tau,\mathrm{Z}^{\tau;t}(\cdot,\psi_{s,t}))-\nabla_{v}f(\tau,\mathrm{Z}^{\tau;t}(\cdot,\psi_{s,t}))\right]\,\mathrm{d}\tau\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}_{r}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda(T,R)\sum_{|\gamma|\leq k}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\langle v\rangle^{2r}\left|\int_{s}^{t}\left[\partial_{x,v}^{\gamma}(\nabla_{x}f)(\tau,\mathrm{Z}^{\tau;t}(\cdot,\psi_{s,t}))-\partial_{x,v}^{\gamma}(\nabla_{v}f)(\tau,\mathrm{Z}^{\tau;t}(\cdot,\psi_{s,t}))\right]\mathrm{d}\tau\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}v.

By the the generalized Minkowski inequality, the last expression is bounded by

\displaystyle\sum_{|\gamma|\leq k}\left(\int_{s}^{t}\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\langle v\rangle^{2r}|\partial_{x,v}^{\gamma}(\nabla_{x}f)(\tau,\mathrm{Z}^{\tau;t}(\cdot,\psi_{s,t}))-\partial_{x,v}^{\gamma}(\nabla_{v}f)(\tau,\mathrm{Z}^{\tau;t}(\cdot,\psi_{s,t}))|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}v\right)^{1/2}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda(T,R)\sum_{|\gamma|\leq k}\left(\int_{s}^{t}\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\langle v\rangle^{2r}|\partial_{x,v}^{\gamma}(\nabla_{x}f)(\tau,x,v)-\partial_{x,v}^{\gamma}(\nabla_{v}f)(\tau,x,v)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}v\right)^{1/2}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\right)^{2},

where we have performed the change of variable
v\mapsto w=\psi_{s,t}(x,v)
from Lemma
3.1
followed by
(x,w)\mapsto\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,w)
. Here, we have used the bounds on the Jacobian from (
3.3
), as well as the one on
|v-\psi_{s,t}(x,v)|
via (
3.4
). It follows that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathfrak{H}^{K,I}(s,t)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}_{r}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda(T,R)|t-s|^{2}\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq T}\,\Big\{\|\nabla_{x}f(\tau)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}_{r}}^{2}+\|\nabla_{v}f(\tau)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}_{r}}^{2}\Big\},

and therefore

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{S_{16}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})}\leq\Lambda(T,R).

\bullet

Estimate of
\mathbf{S}_{17},\mathbf{S}_{18}
and
\mathbf{S}_{19}
: We mainly proceed as for
\mathbf{S}_{13},\mathbf{S}_{14}
and
\mathbf{S}_{15}
, using Proposition
4.5
. As before, the kernel has just been changed from
\mathrm{H}
to
\mathfrak{H}
. Hence, we only have to provide an estimate for
\|\partial_{s}\mathfrak{H}^{K,I}(s,t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}_{r}}
(for
k
and
r
large enough).
We have

\displaystyle\partial_{s}\mathfrak{H}^{K,I}(s,t,x,v)

\displaystyle=\partial_{s}J^{s,t}\mathrm{N}^{t;s}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,\psi_{s,t}(x,v)))_{(I,0),(K,0)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\int_{s}^{t}e^{d(t-\tau)}\big[\nabla_{x}f(\tau,\mathrm{Z}^{\tau;t}(x,\psi_{s,t}(x,v)))-\nabla_{v}f(\tau,\mathrm{Z}^{\tau;t}(x,\psi_{s,t}(x,v)))\big]\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\quad+J^{s,t}\partial_{s}\left\{\mathrm{N}^{t;s}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,\psi_{s,t}(x,v)))_{(I,0),(K,0)}\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\int_{s}^{t}e^{d(t-\tau)}\big[\nabla_{x}f(\tau,\mathrm{Z}^{\tau;t}(x,\psi_{s,t}(x,v)))-\nabla_{v}f(\tau,\mathrm{Z}^{\tau;t}(x,\psi_{s,t}(x,v)))\big]\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\quad+J^{s,t}\mathrm{N}^{t;s}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,\psi_{s,t}(x,v)))_{(I,0),(K,0)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\int_{s}^{t}e^{d(t-\tau)}\partial_{s}\big[\nabla_{x}f(\tau,\mathrm{Z}^{\tau;t}(x,\psi_{s,t}(x,v)))-\nabla_{v}f(\tau,\mathrm{Z}^{\tau;t}(x,\psi_{s,t}(x,v)))\big]\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\quad-e^{d(t-s)}\mathrm{J}^{s,t}\mathrm{N}^{t;s}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,\psi_{s,t}(x,v)))_{(I,0),(K,0)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\big[\nabla_{x}f(s,\mathrm{Z}^{s;t}(x,\psi_{s,t}(x,v)))-\nabla_{v}f(s,\mathrm{Z}^{s;t}(x,\psi_{s,t}(x,v)))\big].

Using the same
\mathrm{W}^{k,\infty}_{x,v}
bounds on
J^{s,t}
,
\mathrm{N}^{s;t}
and
\dot{\mathrm{W}}^{k,\infty}_{x,v}
bounds on
\psi_{s,t}
as before, as well as on their time derivatives, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\partial_{s}\mathfrak{H}^{K,I}(s,t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}_{r}}\leq\Lambda(T,R).

This eventually yields

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{S_{17}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})}+\left\|\mathbf{S_{18}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})}+\left\|\mathbf{S_{19}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})}\leq\Lambda(T,R).

This ends the proof.
∎

We end this section by eventually giving a proof of Lemma
5.10
. Let us mention that it only requires the use of the continuity estimate coming from Proposition
4.4
and not the ones of Proposition
4.5
-
4.7
.

Proof of Lemma
5.10
.

In view of the estimate (
5.10
) of Lemma
5.9
, we only have to prove that

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}_{1}}^{0}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}+\|\nabla_{x}\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}_{1}}^{1}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}\leq\Lambda(T,R).

We only write the proof for
\nabla_{x}\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}_{1}}^{0}
. Let us recall that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}_{1}}^{0}(t,x)=-\int_{0}^{t}e^{d(t-s)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathrm{N}^{t;s}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,v))\mathcal{R}_{1}(s,\mathrm{Z}^{s;t}(x,v))\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}=\left(\mathcal{R}^{K,L}_{1}\right)_{|K|+|L|\in\{m-1,m\}},

with

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{K,L}_{1}=\mathbf{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}|K|>2\\
L\neq 0\end{subarray}}\partial_{x}^{K}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{L}f+\mathbf{1}_{|K|>1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<\alpha<K\\
|\alpha|=m-1\end{subarray}}\binom{L}{\alpha}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial_{x}^{L-\alpha}\partial_{v}^{K}f.

We have for all
(I,J)

\displaystyle\left[\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}_{1}}^{0}\right]_{(I,J)}(t,x)=-\int_{0}^{t}e^{d(t-s)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sum_{(K,L)}\mathrm{N}^{t;s}_{(I,J),(K,L)}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,v))\left[\mathcal{R}_{1}\right]_{(K,L)}(s,\mathrm{Z}^{s;t}(x,v))\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s,

therefore

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}\left[\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}_{1}}^{0}\right]_{(I,J)}(t,x)

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{t}e^{d(t-s)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sum_{(K,L)}\nabla_{x}\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,v)\nabla_{x}\mathrm{N}^{t;s}_{(I,J),(K,L)}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,v))\left[\mathcal{R}_{1}\right]_{(K,L)}(s,\mathrm{Z}^{s;t}(x,v))\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{0}^{t}e^{d(t-s)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sum_{(K,L)}\mathrm{N}^{t;s}_{(I,J),(K,L)}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,v))\nabla_{x}\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,v)\nabla_{x}\left[\mathcal{R}_{1}\right]_{(K,L)}(s,\mathrm{Z}^{s;t}(x,v))\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s.

\bullet
For the first term, there is no derivative on
\mathcal{R}_{1}
. We can proceed exactly as for
\mathcal{R}_{0}
in Lemma
5.9
, relying on the estimate (
5.10
).

\bullet
The second term is more involved, since
\nabla_{x}\left[\mathcal{R}_{1}\right]_{(K,L)}
contains several types of terms:

-

some are of the form

\mathbf{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}|K|>2\\
L\neq 0\end{subarray}}\nabla_{x}[\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{L}f]\partial_{x}^{K}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}.

They involve at most
m
derivatives of
\varrho
and at most
2+(m-3)=m-1
derivatives of
f
. We can bound this term in
\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{0}_{r})
, following the argument used for
\mathcal{R}_{0}
in the proof of Lemma
5.3
. Its contribution to
\nabla_{x}\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}_{1}}^{0}
is handled as in the proof of Lemma
5.9
(for the term
\nabla_{x}\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}_{0}}^{0}
);

-

some are of the form

\nabla_{x}[\nabla_{v}\partial_{x}^{L-\alpha}\partial_{v}^{K}f]\partial_{x}^{\alpha}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho},

with
|K|>1
and
|\alpha|=m-1
. They involve at most
m
derivatives of
\varrho
and at most
3
derivatives of
f
. We can also bound this term in
\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{0}_{r})
, following the argument used for
\mathcal{R}_{0}
in the proof of Lemma
5.3
. As before, we can follow the proof of Lemma
5.9
to control its contribution to
\nabla_{x}\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R}_{1}}^{0}
;

-

some are of the form

\mathbf{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}|K|>2\\
L\neq 0\end{subarray}}\nabla_{x}[\partial_{x}^{K}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}]\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{L}f,

and of the form

\nabla_{x}[\partial_{x}^{\alpha}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}]\nabla_{v}\partial_{x}^{L-\alpha}\partial_{v}^{K}f,

with
|K|>1
and
|\alpha|=m-1
. These terms involve at most
m+1
derivatives of
\varrho
and cannot be directly treated as the previous ones. We need to rely on the smoothing estimates from Section
4
(by making some terms of
Type I
appear).

We then focus on the two last types of terms, and we have to deal with

\mathbb{W}_{1}^{K,L}(t,x):=-\int_{0}^{t}e^{d(t-s)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathrm{N}^{t;s}_{(I,J),(K,L)}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,v))\\
\nabla_{x}\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,v)\mathbf{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}|K|>2\\
L\neq 0\end{subarray}}\nabla_{x}[\partial_{x}^{K}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}]\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{L}f(s,\mathrm{Z}^{s;t}(x,v))\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s,

and

\mathbb{W}_{2}^{K,L}(t,x):=-\int_{0}^{t}e^{d(t-s)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathrm{N}^{t;s}_{(I,J),(K,L)}(\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,v))\\
\nabla_{x}\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}(x,v)\mathbf{1}_{|K|>1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<\alpha<K\\
|\alpha|=m-1\end{subarray}}\binom{L}{\alpha}\nabla_{x}[\partial_{x}^{\alpha}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}]\nabla_{v}\partial_{x}^{L-\alpha}\partial_{v}^{K}f(s,\mathrm{Z}^{s;t}(x,v))\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s,

for
|K|+|L|\leq m
. Let us turn to the estimates of these terms in
\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})
.

\bullet

Estimate of
\mathbb{W}_{1}^{K,L}
when
\frac{m-2}{2}\leq|L|<m-2
: since
|K|+|L|\leq m
, this implies that
|K|\leq 1+m/2
. As in the proof of Lemma
5.9
, we have by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{W}_{1}^{K,L}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}\leq\Lambda(T,R)\left\|\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{K}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{L}f\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{0}_{r})},

and, by setting
\chi(v)=(1+|v|^{2})^{r/2}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\chi\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{K}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{L}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}_{x,v}}\lesssim\|\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{K}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{x}}\|\chi\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{L}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}_{x,v}}\lesssim\|E\|_{\mathrm{H}^{k}}\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m-1}_{r}},

with
k>\frac{d}{2}+1+|K|
. Since
d+6\leq m
, we can choose
k
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{W}_{1}^{K,L}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}\leq\Lambda(T,R)\|E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-1})}\|f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{m-1}_{r})}\leq\Lambda(T,R),

and conclude as in the proof of Lemma
5.3
. We obtain in that case

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{W}_{1}^{K,L}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}\leq\Lambda(T,R).

\bullet

Estimate of
\mathbb{W}_{1}^{K,L}
when
1\leq|L|\leq\frac{m-2}{2}-1
: in that case, we only have
|K|\leq m-1
. We shall rely on the smoothing estimate of Proposition
4.4
(to treat terms of
Type I
) and to recover the loss of the extra derivative on
\varrho
. To do so, we first write

\displaystyle|\mathbb{W}_{1}^{K,L}(t,x)|

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda(T,R)\left\|\nabla_{x}\mathrm{Z}^{0;t}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{x,v}}\underset{0\leq s\leq T}{\sup}\left\|\mathrm{N}^{t;s}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{x,v}}\mathbf{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}|K|>2\\
L\neq 0\end{subarray}}\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla_{x}[\partial_{x}^{K}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}]\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{L}f(s,\mathrm{Z}^{s;t}(x,v))\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s\right|,

and we perform the change of variable
v\mapsto\psi_{s,t}(x,w)
from Lemma
3.1
in the last integral (since
t\leq\overline{T}(R)
) to get

\displaystyle|\mathbb{W}_{1}^{K,L}(t,x)|

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda(T,R)\mathbf{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}|K|>2\\
L\neq 0\end{subarray}}\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla_{x}[\partial_{x}^{K}E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}](s,x+(1-e^{t-s})w)\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{L}f(s,\mathrm{Z}^{s;t}(x,\psi_{s,t}(x,w)))\,\mathrm{d}w\,\mathrm{d}s\right|,

thanks to the bounds (
3.3
) of Lemma
3.1
, Remark
3.2
and Lemma
5.7
.

Relying on the decomposition of Lemma
5.13
, we can make the operator
\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{fric}}
appear, hence dealing with terms of
Type I
, and use Proposition
4.4
(combined with the bounds on
\mathrm{Z}^{s;t}
and
\psi_{s,t}
) to estimate the last integral in
\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})
. The proof follows the same lines as the ones of Lemma
5.14
. Note that Proposition
4.4
only requires an estimate of
\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{L}f
in
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}_{r}^{s})
with
s>1+d
. Since
m>2d+2
, we obtain in that case

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{W}_{1}^{K,L}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}\leq\Lambda(T,R).

\bullet

Estimate of
\mathbb{W}_{2}^{K,L}
: to treat this term, we observe that it only involves at most
2
derivatives of
f
and the gradient of
m-1
derivatives of the force field
E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}
. Hence, we can exactly perform the same as previously for
\mathbb{W}_{1}^{K,L}
. We likewise obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{W}_{2}^{K,L}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}\leq\Lambda(T,R).

This concludes the proof of Lemma
5.10
.
∎

6.
Analysis of the fluid density

We pursue our goal which is to obtain an uniform control on
\|\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})}
. In the previous section, we have related the kinetic moments
\rho_{f}
and
j_{f}
to the fluid density
\varrho
, up to some well controlled remainders. In this section, we build on these relations to further analyze
\varrho
.

We start by taking derivatives in the transport equation satisfied by
\varrho
. By using the key Proposition
5.1
(which was precisely the main outcome of Section
5
), we obtain a factorization of the equation on the derivatives between

-

a purely hyperbolic part, which is the transport operator
\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla_{x}
;

-

an integro-differential operator part.

This is where the crucial Penrose condition (
P
) steps in and allows to justify that this last operator is actually elliptic in space-time and therefore can provide
\mathrm{L}^{2}_{T}\mathrm{L}^{2}_{x}
estimates without loss. This relies on a semiclassical pseudodifferential analysis, in the spirit of
[
HKR16
]
.

In this section, we will use the notation
M_{\mathrm{in}}
, which stands for a positive constant depending only on the initial data.

6.1.
Equation on the derivatives of the fluid density

For
T\in[0,\min\left(T_{\varepsilon}(R),\overline{T}(R)\right)
, the aim of this section is to prove the following proposition.

Proposition 6.1
.

Setting
h=\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\varrho
for
|\alpha|\leq m
, one has

(6.1)

\displaystyle\left(\mathrm{Id}-\frac{\varrho}{1-\rho_{f}}\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}\circ\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\right)\Big[\partial_{t}h+u\cdot\nabla_{x}h\Big]=\mathcal{R},\ \ t\in(0,T),

with
G(t,x,v)=p^{\prime}(\varrho(t,x))\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v)
and

\|\mathcal{R}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}\leq\Lambda(T,R,\|h(0)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}).

We start with a commutation result when one takes derivatives in the equation on
\varrho
.

Lemma 6.2
.

For all
|\alpha|\leq m
,
\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\varrho
satisfies the equation

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\varrho)+u\cdot\nabla_{x}(\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\varrho)+\frac{\varrho}{1-\rho_{f}}\mathrm{div}_{x}\left[j_{\partial_{x}^{\alpha}f}-\rho_{\partial_{x}^{\alpha}f}u\right]=R^{\alpha},

with

\displaystyle\|R^{\alpha}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}\leq\Lambda(T,R).

Proof.

Recall that by Lemma
2.2
, the transport equation on
\varrho
reads as

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\varrho+u\cdot\nabla_{x}\varrho+\frac{\varrho}{1-\rho_{f}}\mathrm{div}_{x}\left[j_{f}-\rho_{f}u\right]=S,\ \ S=-\frac{\varrho}{1-\rho_{f}}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,u.

We get, for all
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\varrho)+u\cdot\nabla_{x}(\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\varrho)+[\partial_{x}^{\alpha},u\cdot\nabla_{x}]\varrho+\frac{\varrho}{1-\rho_{f}}\mathrm{div}_{x}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\left(j_{f}-\rho_{f}u\right)+\left[\partial_{x}^{\alpha},\frac{\varrho\mathrm{div}_{x}}{1-\rho_{f}}\right](j_{f}-\rho_{f}u)=\partial_{x}^{\alpha}S,

and therefore
\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\varrho
satisfies the equation

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\varrho)+u\cdot\nabla_{x}(\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\varrho)+\frac{\varrho}{1-\rho_{f}}\mathrm{div}_{x}\left[j_{\partial_{x}^{\alpha}f}-\rho_{\partial_{x}^{\alpha}f}u\right]=R^{\alpha},

where
R^{\alpha}
is a remainder defined by

\displaystyle R^{\alpha}

\displaystyle:=\partial_{x}^{\alpha}S-[\partial_{x}^{\alpha},u\cdot\nabla_{x}]\varrho-\left[\partial_{x}^{\alpha},\frac{\varrho\mathrm{div}_{x}}{1-\rho_{f}}\right](j_{f}-\rho_{f}u)+\frac{\varrho}{1-\rho_{f}}\mathrm{div}_{x}\left([\partial_{x}^{\alpha},u]\rho_{f}\right)

\displaystyle:=\partial_{x}^{\alpha}S+\mathcal{C}_{1}+\mathcal{C}_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{3}.

Let us estimate each of these terms in
\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})
, for all
|\alpha|\leq m
.

\bullet

Estimate of
\partial_{x}^{\alpha}S
: we use the tame estimate from Proposition
A.2
to write

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}S\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\frac{\varrho}{1-\rho_{f}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}\left\|\mathrm{div}_{x}\,u\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}+\left\|\frac{\varrho}{1-\rho_{f}}\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}\left\|\mathrm{div}_{x}\,u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}=\mathfrak{S}_{1}+\mathfrak{S}_{2}.

Since
m-2>d/2
, we have by Lemma
2.20
and (
2.5
)

\displaystyle\left\|\mathfrak{S}_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\frac{1}{1-\rho_{f}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{\infty})}\left\|\varrho\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-2})}\left\|u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m+1})}\leq\Lambda\left(T,R\right).

For
\mathfrak{S}_{2}
, we combine the tame estimate from Proposition
A.2
with Lemma
A.5
which provides

\displaystyle\left\|\mathfrak{S}_{2}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-2})}\left\|u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})}+\|\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})}\left\|\frac{1}{1-\rho_{f}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{\infty})}\left\|u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-2})}\left(\left\|\rho_{f}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{\infty})}\right)\left\|\rho_{f}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})}\left\|u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda(T,R)+\Lambda(T,R)\left\|\rho_{f}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})},

since
m-2>d/2
and again thanks to Lemma
2.20
and (
2.5
). We obtain

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}S\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}\leq\Lambda(T,R),

by using Corollary
5.2
.

\bullet

Estimate of
\mathcal{C}_{1}
: we have
-\mathcal{C}_{1}=[\partial_{x}^{\alpha},u\cdot](\nabla_{x}\varrho)
therefore the commutator estimate from Proposition
A.1
yields

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{C}_{1}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}\lesssim\|\nabla_{x}u\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}\|\nabla_{x}\varrho\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m-1}}+\|u\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}\|\nabla_{x}\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}\lesssim\|u\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}\|\varrho\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}},

since
m>1+d/2
. We then obtain by Corollary
5.2

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{C}_{1}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}\lesssim\|u\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})}\|\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})}\leq\Lambda(T,R).

\bullet

Estimate of
\mathcal{C}_{2}
: we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{2}=\left[\partial_{x}^{\alpha},\frac{\varrho}{1-\rho_{f}}\right](\mathrm{div}_{x}(j_{f}-\rho_{f}u)).

Applying the commutator estimate from Proposition
A.1
, we get

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{C}_{2}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\nabla_{x}\frac{\varrho}{1-\rho_{f}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}\|j_{f}-\rho_{f}u\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}+\left\|\frac{\varrho}{1-\rho_{f}}\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}\|\mathrm{div}_{x}(j_{f}-\rho_{f}u)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}=\mathcal{C}_{2,1}+\mathcal{C}_{2,2}.

For
\mathcal{C}_{2,1}
, we infer from Sobolev embedding (since
m-2>1+d/2
)

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{x}\frac{\varrho}{1-\rho_{f}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{1}{1-\rho_{f}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}\left\|\nabla_{x}\varrho\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}+\left\|\frac{\varrho}{(1-\rho_{f})^{2}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}\left\|\nabla_{x}\rho_{f}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\frac{1}{1-\rho_{f}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}\left\|\varrho\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m-2}}+\left\|\frac{1}{(1-\rho_{f})}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\left\|\rho\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m-2}}\left\|\nabla_{x}\rho_{f}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda(T,R),

thanks to Lemma
2.20
and Corollary
5.2
, therefore the tame estimate coming from Proposition
A.2

entails

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{C}_{2,1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda(T,R)\Big(\|j_{f}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})}+\|f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{m-1}_{r})}\|u\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\|u\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})}\|\rho_{f}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})}\Big),

since
m-1>d/2
. Invoking Lemma
2.1
and Corollary
5.2
, we get

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{C}_{2,1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T)}\leq\Lambda(T,R).

For
\mathcal{C}_{2,2}
, we observe that since
m-1>1+d/2

\displaystyle\|\mathrm{div}_{x}(j_{f}-\rho_{f}u)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}\lesssim\|j_{f}\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m-1}}+\|\rho_{f}\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m-1}}\|u\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m-1}},

therefore we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{C}_{2,2}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T)}\leq\Lambda(T,R),

by using what we have done for
\mathfrak{S}_{2}
above.

\bullet

Estimate of
\mathcal{C}_{3}
: we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{3}=\frac{\varrho}{1-\rho_{f}}\Big([\partial_{x}^{\alpha},\mathrm{div}_{x}\,u\cdot](\rho_{f})+[\partial_{x}^{\alpha},u\cdot](\nabla_{x}\rho_{f})\Big)=\frac{\varrho}{1-\rho_{f}}\left(\mathcal{C}_{3,1}+\mathcal{C}_{3,2}\right).

Applying Proposition
A.1
, we get for
m+1>2+d/2

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{C}_{3,1}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla_{x}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,u\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}\|\rho_{f}\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m-1}}+\|\mathrm{div}_{x}\,u\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}\|\rho_{f}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}\lesssim\|u\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m+1}}\|\rho_{f}\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m-1}},

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{C}_{3,2}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla_{x}u\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}\|\nabla_{x}\rho_{f}\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m-1}}+\|u\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}\|\nabla_{x}\rho_{f}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}\lesssim\|u\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}\|\rho_{f}\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}+\|u\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}\|\rho_{f}\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m-1}}.

Taking the
\mathrm{L}^{2}
-norm in time and using Lemma
2.1
, we have for
\mathcal{C}_{3,1}

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{C}_{3,1}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m+1})}\|\rho_{f}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-1})}\leq\Lambda(T,R),

while for
\mathcal{C}_{3,2}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{C}_{3,2}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}\left(0,T,\mathrm{H}^{m}\right)}\|\rho_{f}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})}+\|u\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m+1})}\|\rho_{f}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-1})}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda(T,R),

thanks to Corollary
5.2
.
∎

Let us now transform the equation on the derivatives of
\varrho
obtained in Lemma
6.2
. To ease readibility let us momentaneously set

(6.2)

\displaystyle\mathrm{K}_{1,G}^{\mathrm{free}}[F](t,x,):=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}v[\nabla_{x}F](s,x-(t-s)v)\cdot G(t,s,x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s.

Lemma 6.3
.

For
h=\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\varrho
with
|\alpha|\leq m
, one has

\displaystyle\partial_{t}h+u\cdot\nabla_{x}h+\frac{\varrho}{1-\rho_{f}}\mathrm{div}_{x}\Big[\mathrm{K}_{1,G}^{\mathrm{free}}(\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}h)-\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}(\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}h)u\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R},

with

G(t,x,v):=p^{\prime}(\varrho(t,x))\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v),

and where the remainder
\mathcal{R}
satisfies

\|\mathcal{R}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}\leq\Lambda(T,R).

Proof.

By Lemma
6.2
, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{t}h+u\cdot\nabla_{x}h+\frac{\varrho}{1-\rho_{f}}\mathrm{div}_{x}\left[j_{\partial_{x}^{\alpha}f}-\rho_{\partial_{x}^{\alpha}f}u\right]

\displaystyle=R^{\alpha},

with

\displaystyle\|R^{\alpha}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda(T,R).

Thanks to Proposition
5.1
, we can write

\displaystyle\rho_{\partial_{x}^{\alpha}f}=\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}(\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\varrho)+\mathrm{R}^{\alpha}[\rho_{f}],\ \ j_{\partial_{x}^{\alpha}f}=\mathrm{K}_{1,G}^{\mathrm{free}}(\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\varrho)+\mathrm{R}^{\alpha}[j_{f}],

with
G(t,x,v):=p^{\prime}(\varrho(t,x))\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v)
and where

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{R}^{\alpha}[\rho_{f}]\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T,\mathrm{H}^{1})}\leq\Lambda(T,R),\ \ \left\|\mathrm{R}^{\alpha}[j_{f}]\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T,\mathrm{H}^{1})}\leq\Lambda(T,R).

We obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{t}h+u\cdot\nabla_{x}h+\frac{\varrho}{1-\rho_{f}}\mathrm{div}_{x}\Big[\mathrm{K}_{1,G}^{\mathrm{free}}(\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}h)-\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}(\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}h)u\Big]

\displaystyle=R^{\alpha}-\mathrm{div}_{x}\Big[\mathrm{R}^{\alpha}[j_{f}]-\mathrm{R}^{\alpha}[\rho_{f}]u\Big].

Thanks to the aforementioned estimates in
\mathrm{L}^{2}_{T}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{x}
, we obtain the desired estimate on the remainder.
∎

Remark 6.4
.

In the sequel, we shall rely on the following estimate: for all
\ell>0
and
\sigma>0
such that
\ell<m-d/2-2
, we have

(6.3)

\displaystyle\underset{0\leq t\leq T}{\sup}\|G(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\ell}_{\sigma}}\leq\Lambda(T,R).

Indeed, we have

\displaystyle\|p^{\prime}(\varrho(t))\nabla_{v}f(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\ell}_{\sigma}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{|\mu|+|\nu|\leq\ell}\sum_{\gamma=0}^{\mu+\nu}\|\partial_{x}^{\gamma}(p^{\prime}(\varrho(t)))\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\langle v\rangle^{2\sigma}|\partial^{\mu+\nu-\gamma}_{x,v}\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|p^{\prime}(\varrho(t))\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{k}}^{2}\|f(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m-1}_{\sigma}}^{2},

if
m-1\geq\ell
and
k>\frac{d}{2}+\ell
. Invoking Lemma
2.20
by choosing also
k\leq m-2
, we obtain the estimate (
6.3
).

Our goal is now to understand the term

\mathrm{div}_{x}\Big[\mathrm{K}_{1,G}^{\mathrm{free}}(\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}h)-\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}(\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}h)u\Big].

We will show that up to good remainders, it is related to the transport part
\partial_{t}h+u\cdot\nabla_{x}h
appearing in the equation of Lemma
6.3
. This is the object of the following Lemmas
6.5
–
6.6
which are crucial commutation results.

Lemma 6.5
.

For all
|\alpha|\leq m
and
h=\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\varrho
, there holds

\mathrm{div}_{x}(\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}[\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}h]u)=\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}[\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}(u\cdot\nabla_{x}h)]+\mathcal{R},

with

\|\mathcal{R}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}\leq\Lambda(T,R).

Proof.

First, let us prove that for all smooth function
\mathfrak{h}(t,x)
, we have

(6.4)

\displaystyle\mathrm{div}_{x}(\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}[\mathfrak{h}]u)=\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}[(u\cdot\nabla_{x}\mathfrak{h})]+\mathcal{R},

with

(6.5)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{R}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}\leq\Lambda(T,R,\|\mathfrak{h}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}).

We have

\displaystyle\mathrm{div}_{x}(u\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}[\mathfrak{h}])

\displaystyle=u\cdot\nabla_{x}\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}[\mathfrak{h}]+(\mathrm{div}_{x}u)\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}[\mathfrak{h}]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}[(u\cdot\nabla_{x}\mathfrak{h})]+(\mathrm{div}_{x}u)\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}[\mathfrak{h}]+[u\cdot\nabla_{x},\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}][\mathfrak{h}].

Using the notation
\partial_{i}=\partial_{x_{i}}
, we have

\displaystyle[u\cdot\nabla_{x},\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}][\mathfrak{h}](t,x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{i}(t,x)\partial_{i}(\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}[\mathfrak{h}])(t,x)-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}[(u_{i}\partial_{i}\mathfrak{h})](t,x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{i}(t,x)\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla_{x}(\partial_{i}\mathfrak{h})(s,x-(t-s)v)\cdot G(t,x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=1}^{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{i}(t,x)\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla_{x}\mathfrak{h}(s,x-(t-s)v)\cdot\partial_{i}G(t,x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\partial_{i}\mathfrak{h}(s,x-(t-s)v)\nabla_{x}u_{i}(s,x-(t-s)v)\cdot G(t,x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}u_{i}(s,x-(t-s)v)\nabla_{x}(\partial_{i}\mathfrak{h})(s,x-(t-s)v)\cdot G(t,x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{free}}_{(u\cdot\nabla_{x})G}[\mathfrak{h}](t,x)-\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{free}}_{(G\cdot\nabla_{x})\widetilde{u}}[\mathfrak{h}](t,x)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=1}^{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla_{x}(\partial_{i}\mathfrak{h})(s,x-(t-s)v)\cdot\Big((u_{i}(t,x)-\widetilde{u}_{i}(s,t,x,v))G(t,x,v)\Big)\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s,

where we have set
\widetilde{u}(s,t,x,v)=u(s,x-(t-s)v)
. We thus get

\displaystyle\mathrm{div}_{x}(u\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}[\mathfrak{h}])=\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}[(u\cdot\nabla_{x}\mathfrak{h})]+(\mathrm{div}_{x}u)\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}[\mathfrak{h}]+\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{free}}_{(u\cdot\nabla_{x})G}[\mathfrak{h}]-\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{free}}_{(G\cdot\nabla_{x})\widetilde{u}}[\mathfrak{h}]+\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{free}}_{(u_{i}-\widetilde{u}_{i})G}[\partial_{i}\mathfrak{h}],

which gives a decomposition as (
6.4
). In the sequel, we shall constantly use the estimate (
6.3
) of Remark
6.4
, that is, for all
0<p<m-d/2-2
and
\sigma>0

(6.6)

\displaystyle\underset{0\leq t\leq T}{\sup}\|G(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{p}_{\sigma}}\leq\Lambda(T,R).

Let us estimate the different terms of the previous decomposition in order to prove (
6.5
). For the first one, we use the smoothing estimate of Proposition
4.1
to directly get for
\ell>1+d
and
\sigma>d/2

\displaystyle\left\|(\mathrm{div}_{x}u)\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}[\mathfrak{h}]\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda(T,R)\underset{0\leq t\leq T}{\sup}\|G(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\ell}_{\sigma}}\left\|\mathfrak{h}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}\leq\Lambda(T,R)\left\|\mathfrak{h}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})},

since
m>3+3d/2
. For the second and third ones, Proposition
4.1
yields

\left\|\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{free}}_{(u\cdot\nabla_{x})G}[\mathfrak{h}]\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}+\left\|\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{free}}_{(G\cdot\nabla_{x})\widetilde{u}}[\mathfrak{h}]\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}\\
\lesssim\left(\underset{0\leq s,t\leq T}{\sup}\|(u\cdot\nabla_{x})G(t,s)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{p}_{\sigma}}.+\underset{0\leq s,t\leq T}{\sup}\|(G\cdot\nabla_{x})\widetilde{u}(t,s)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{p}_{\sigma}}\right)\left\|\mathfrak{h}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})},

if
\sigma>d/2
and
p>1+d
. With the same arguments as in Remark
6.4
to estimate the terms inside the parentheses, we get (since
m>4+3d/2
)

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{free}}_{(u\cdot\nabla_{x})G}[\mathfrak{h}]\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}+\left\|\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{free}}_{(G\cdot\nabla_{x})\widetilde{u}}[\mathfrak{h}]\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}\lesssim\Lambda(T,R)\left\|\mathfrak{h}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}.

For the last term, we observe that the kernel
(u_{i}(t)-\widetilde{u}_{i}(t,s,x,v))G(t,x,v)
vanishes at
s=t
, therefore Remark
4.6
implies

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{free}}_{(u_{i}-\widetilde{u}_{i})G}[\partial_{i}\mathfrak{h}]\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda(T)\underset{0\leq s,t\leq T}{\sup}\|\partial_{s}(u_{i}-\widetilde{u}_{i})G(t,s)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{p}_{\sigma}}\left\|\mathfrak{h}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}

\displaystyle=\Lambda(T)\underset{0\leq s,t\leq T}{\sup}\|\partial_{s}\widetilde{u}_{i}(t,s)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{k}}\underset{0\leq t\leq T}{\sup}\|G(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{p}_{\sigma}}\left\|\mathfrak{h}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})},

for
p>7+d/2
,
k>p+d/2
and
\sigma>d/2
. Using the equation on
u
, we obtain (since
m>9+d
)

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{free}}_{(u_{i}-\widetilde{u}_{i})G}[\partial_{i}\mathfrak{h}]\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}\leq\Lambda(T,R)\left\|\mathfrak{h}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2})}.

All in all, this yields the claimed estimate (
6.5
).

Now applying (
6.4
) and (
6.5
) with
\mathfrak{h}=\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}h
, we get

\mathrm{div}_{x}(u\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}[\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}h])=\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}[(u\cdot\nabla_{x}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}h)]+\mathcal{R},

with

\|\mathcal{R}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}\leq\Lambda(T,R,\|\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}h\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))})\leq\Lambda(T,R,\|h\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}).

Finally observe that

\displaystyle\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}[(u\cdot\nabla_{x}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}h)]=\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}[\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}(u\cdot\nabla_{x}h)]+\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}\big[[u\cdot\nabla_{x},\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}]h\big].

Relying once again on Proposition
4.1
, we thus have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}\big[[u\cdot\nabla_{x},\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}]h\big]\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}\leq\Lambda(T,R)\left\|[u\cdot\nabla_{x},\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}]h\big]\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}.

Invoking (a variant of the proof of)
[
BGS07
, Theorem C.14]
about the commutator between a differential operator of order
1
and a regularizing operator, we get

\displaystyle\left\|[u\cdot\nabla_{x},\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}]h\big]\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\lesssim\left\|u\right\|_{\mathrm{W}^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\left\|h\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d})},

where this estimate is independent of
\varepsilon
. We obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}\big[[u\cdot\nabla_{x},\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}]h\big]\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}\leq\Lambda(T,R),

which concludes the proof.

∎

Lemma 6.6
.

For all
|\alpha|\leq m
and
h=\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\varrho
, and with
G(s,t,x,v)=p^{\prime}(\varrho(t,x))\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v)
, there holds

\mathrm{div}_{x}\mathrm{K}_{1,G}^{\mathrm{free}}[\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}h]=-\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}[\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\partial_{s}h]+\mathcal{R},

with

\|\mathcal{R}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}\leq\Lambda(T,R,\|h(0)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}).

Proof.

Recall the definition (
6.2
) for
\mathrm{K}_{1,G}^{\mathrm{free}}
. We first write

\mathrm{div}_{x}\mathrm{K}_{1,G}^{\mathrm{free}}[\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}h]=p^{\prime}(\varrho(t,x))\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla_{x}\mathrm{div}_{x}\left[v\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}h](s,x-(t-s)v)\right]\cdot\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s+\mathcal{R},

with

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(t,x)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla_{x}[\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}h](s,x-(t-s)v)\cdot\big[(v\cdot\nabla_{x})(p^{\prime}(\varrho(t,x))\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v))\big]\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{free}}_{(v\cdot\nabla_{x})(p^{\prime}(\varrho)\nabla_{v}f)}[\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}h].

Thanks to Proposition
4.1
, we thus have for
p>1+d
and
\sigma>d/2

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{R}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}h\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}\underset{0\leq s,t\leq T}{\sup}\|(v\cdot\nabla_{x}p^{\prime}(\varrho))\nabla_{v}f(t,s)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{p}_{\sigma}}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda(T,R).

Now observe the identity

\partial_{s}\Big[\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}h(s,x-(t-s)v)\Big]=(\partial_{s}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}h)(s,x-(t-s)v)+\mathrm{div}_{x}(v\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}h)(s,x-(t-s)v).

Since

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla_{x}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}h(t,x)\cdot\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v=0,

we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\partial_{s}\Big[\nabla_{x}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}h(s,x-(t-s)v)\Big]\cdot\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla_{x}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}h(0,x-tv)\cdot\nabla_{v}f(t,x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v.

Using the generalized Minkowski inequality and the Sobolev embedding,
the last term is estimated in
\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T,\mathrm{L}^{2})
by

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}h(0)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\underset{x\in\mathbb{T}^{d}}{\sup}|\nabla_{v}f(\cdot,x,v)|\,\mathrm{d}v\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T)}\leq\Lambda(T,R)\|h(0)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}.

We deduce that we can write

\mathrm{div}_{x}\mathrm{K}_{1,G}^{\mathrm{free}}[\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}h]=-\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}[\partial_{s}\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}h]+\mathcal{R},

with

\|\mathcal{R}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}\leq\Lambda(T,R,\|h(0)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}).

∎

Combining the results of Lemmas
6.3
–
6.5
–
6.6
leads to the proof of Proposition
6.1
.

6.2.
Propagation of the Penrose condition for short times

We now show how to propagate the Penrose stability condition (
P
) for short times. This will allow to study the operator

\mathrm{Id}-\frac{\varrho}{1-\rho_{f}}\mathrm{K}_{G}^{\mathrm{free}}\circ\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}

in the next sections, the outset being its ellipticity.

First, we shall need several estimates on the time derivatives of the solutions. We have the following basic lemma.

Lemma 6.7
.

Let
s\geq 0
and
\sigma\geq 0
. For all
T\in(0,T_{\varepsilon})
, the following holds.

•

If
s>d
and
s+1>d/2
, we have

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s}_{\sigma})}\lesssim\|f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s+1}_{\sigma+1})}+\|f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{s+1}_{\sigma})}\left(\|u\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{s})}+\Lambda\left(\|\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{s+1})}\right)\right).

•

If
s>d/2
and
\sigma>1+d/2
, we have

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\rho_{f}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{\infty})}\lesssim\|\partial_{t}\rho_{f}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{s})}\lesssim\|f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{1+s}_{\sigma})}.

•

If
s>d/2
and
\sigma>1+d/2
, we have

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{\infty})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{s})}\|\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1+s})}

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|\frac{1}{1-\rho_{f}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{\infty})}\|\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{s})}\Big(\|f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{1+s}_{\sigma})}+\|u\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1+s})}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\|f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{1+s}_{\sigma})}\|u\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1+s})}\Big).

Proof.

\bullet
Using the Vlasov equation satisfied by
f
, we get

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}f\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s}_{\sigma}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s}_{\sigma}}+\|v\cdot\nabla_{x}f\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s}_{\sigma}}+\|v\cdot\nabla_{v}f\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s}_{\sigma}}+\|E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\cdot\nabla_{v}f\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s}_{\sigma}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s+1}_{\sigma+1}}+\|E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\cdot\nabla_{v}f\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s}_{\sigma}}.

Next, combining the estimate (
A.2
) of Lemma
A.6
and the estimate (
2.2
) of Lemma
2.7
, we have for
s>d
such that
s>3+d/2

\displaystyle\|E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\cdot\nabla_{v}f\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s}_{\sigma}}\lesssim\|E_{\textnormal{reg},\varepsilon}^{u,\varrho}\|_{\mathrm{H}^{s}}\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s+1}_{\sigma}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}^{s+1}_{\sigma}}\left(\|u(t)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{s}}+\Lambda(\|\varrho(t)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{s+1}})\right),

hence providing the first estimate.

\bullet
To estimate
\partial_{t}\rho_{f}
, we use the fact that
\partial_{t}\rho_{f}=-\mathrm{div}_{x}j_{f}
so that we have by Sobolev embedding

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\rho_{f}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{\infty})}\lesssim\|\partial_{t}\rho_{f}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{s})}\lesssim\|j_{f}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1+s})}\lesssim\|f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{1+s}_{\sigma})},

thanks to Lemma
2.1
. This gives the second estimate.

\bullet
To estimate
\partial_{t}\varrho
, we use the equation

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\varrho=-u\cdot\nabla_{x}\varrho-\frac{1}{1-\rho_{f}}\,\mathrm{div}_{x}(j_{f}-\rho_{f}u+u)\varrho,

from which we infer

\|\partial_{t}\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{\infty})}\lesssim\|u\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{s})}\|\nabla_{x}\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{s})}\\
+\left\|\frac{1}{1-\rho_{f}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{\infty})}\|\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{s})}\|j_{f}-\rho_{f}u+u\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1+s})}.

We conclude by using Lemma
2.1
.
∎

The propagation of the Penrose condition (
P
) for short times is obtained in the following lemma.

Lemma 6.8
.

There exists
\widetilde{T}_{0}(R)>0
independent of
\varepsilon
such that the following holds: if
(f^{\mathrm{in}},\varrho^{\mathrm{in}})
satisfies the
c
-Penrose stability condition
\eqref{cond:Penrose}_{c}
for some
c>0
then
(f(t),\varrho(t))
satisfies the
\frac{c}{2}
-Penrose stability condition
\eqref{cond:Penrose}_{c/2}
for all
t\in\left[0,\min\left(\widetilde{T}_{0}(R),T^{\varepsilon}\right)\right]
.

Proof.

Let
T<T^{\varepsilon}
and recall the Definition
1.3
of the Penrose function
\mathscr{P}
. We start writing for all
t\in[0,T]

\displaystyle 1-\mathscr{P}_{f(t),\varrho(t)}(x,\gamma,\tau,k)

\displaystyle=1-\frac{p^{\prime}(\varrho(t,x))\rho(t,x)}{1-\rho_{f}(t,x)}\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-(\gamma+i\tau)s}\frac{ik}{1+|k|^{2}}\cdot\left(\mathcal{F}_{v}\nabla_{v}f\right)(t,x,ks)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=1-\mathscr{P}_{f^{\mathrm{in}},\varrho^{\mathrm{in}}}(x,\gamma,\tau,k)+\mathbf{A}_{0}(t,x,\gamma,\tau,k)+\mathbf{B}_{0}(t,x,\gamma,\tau,k),

where

\mathbf{A}_{0}(t,x,\gamma,\tau,k)\\
:=-\frac{p^{\prime}(\varrho^{\mathrm{in}}(x))\varrho^{\mathrm{in}}(x)}{1-\rho_{f^{\mathrm{in}}}(x)}\left(\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-(\gamma+i\tau)s}\frac{ik}{1+|k|^{2}}\cdot\left[\left(\mathcal{F}_{v}\nabla_{v}f\right)(t,x,ks)-\left(\mathcal{F}_{v}\nabla_{v}f^{\mathrm{in}}\right)(x,ks)\right]\,\mathrm{d}s\right),

and

\displaystyle\mathbf{B}_{0}(t,x,\gamma,\tau,k)

\displaystyle:=\left(\frac{p^{\prime}(\varrho^{\mathrm{in}}(x))\varrho^{\mathrm{in}}(x)}{1-\rho_{f^{\mathrm{in}}}(x)}-\frac{p^{\prime}(\varrho(t,x))\varrho(t,x)}{1-\rho_{f}(t,x)}\right)\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-(\gamma+i\tau)s}\frac{ik}{1+|k|^{2}}\cdot\left(\mathcal{F}_{v}\nabla_{v}f\right)(t,x,ks)\,\mathrm{d}s.

\bullet

Estimate of
\mathbf{A}_{0}
: using Taylor’s formula, we have

\displaystyle|\mathbf{A}_{0}(t,x,\gamma,\tau,k)|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{p^{\prime}(\varrho^{\mathrm{in}})\varrho^{\mathrm{in}}}{1-\rho_{f^{\mathrm{in}}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}|k|\int_{0}^{T}|\left(\mathcal{F}_{v}\nabla_{v}\partial_{t}f\right)(\theta,x,ks)|\,\mathrm{d}\theta\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{p^{\prime}(\varrho^{\mathrm{in}})\varrho^{\mathrm{in}}}{1-\rho_{f^{\mathrm{in}}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\left|\left(\mathcal{F}_{v}\nabla_{v}\partial_{t}f\right)\left(\theta,x,\frac{k}{|k|}s\right)\right|\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}\theta

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\frac{p^{\prime}(\varrho^{\mathrm{in}})\varrho^{\mathrm{in}}}{1-\rho_{f^{\mathrm{in}}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}\int_{0}^{T}\underset{s\in\mathbb{R}^{+}}{\sup}(1+s^{2})\left|\left(\mathcal{F}_{v}\nabla_{v}\partial_{t}f\right)\left(\theta,x,\frac{k}{|k|}s\right)\right|\,\mathrm{d}\theta,

therefore we get for
\sigma>d/2

\displaystyle|\mathbf{A}_{0}(t,x,\gamma,\tau,k)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\frac{p^{\prime}(\varrho^{\mathrm{in}})\varrho^{\mathrm{in}}}{1-\rho_{f^{\mathrm{in}}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}\int_{0}^{T}\underset{s\in\mathbb{R}^{+}}{\sup}\sum_{|\beta|\leq 2}\left|\left(\mathcal{F}_{v}\partial_{v}^{\beta}\nabla_{v}\partial_{t}f\right)\left(\theta,x,\frac{k}{|k|}s\right)\right|\,\mathrm{d}\theta

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\frac{p^{\prime}(\varrho^{\mathrm{in}})\varrho^{\mathrm{in}}}{1-\rho_{f^{\mathrm{in}}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}\int_{0}^{T}\left(\sum_{|\beta|\leq 2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(1+|v|^{2})^{\sigma}\left|\partial_{v}^{\beta}\nabla_{v}\partial_{t}f\left(\theta,x,v\right)\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}v\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}\theta

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\frac{p^{\prime}(\varrho^{\mathrm{in}})\varrho^{\mathrm{in}}}{1-\rho_{f^{\mathrm{in}}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}T\|\partial_{t}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{3+s}_{\sigma})},

for all
s>d/2
, thanks to the Sobolev embedding. Invoking Lemma
6.7
(taking
3+s>d
and
3+s+1>d/2
), we have

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{3+s}_{\sigma})}\leq\Lambda\left(T,R\right),

thanks to Lemma
2.20
, choosing
s+6\leq m
.
Therefore there exists a universal constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle|\mathbf{A}_{0}(t,x,\gamma,\tau,k)|\leq C\left\|\frac{p^{\prime}(\varrho^{\mathrm{in}})\varrho^{\mathrm{in}}}{1-\rho_{f^{\mathrm{in}}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}T\Lambda\left(T,R\right).

\bullet

Estimate of
\mathbf{B}_{0}
: Likewise, we have for all
s>d/2
such that
3+s<m-1

\displaystyle|\mathbf{B}_{0}(t,x,\gamma,\tau,k)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{3+s}_{\sigma})}\left|\frac{p^{\prime}(\varrho(t,x))\rho(t,x)}{1-\varrho_{f}(t,x)}-\frac{p^{\prime}(\varrho^{\mathrm{in}}(x))\varrho^{\mathrm{in}}(x)}{1-\rho_{f^{\mathrm{in}}}(x)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq R\int_{0}^{T}\left|\partial_{t}\left\{\frac{p^{\prime}(\varrho(\theta,x))\varrho(\theta,x)}{1-\rho_{f}(\theta,x)}\right\}\right|\,\mathrm{d}\theta

\displaystyle\leq RT\left\|\partial_{t}\left\{\frac{p^{\prime}(\varrho)\varrho}{1-\rho_{f}}\right\}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{\infty})},

Now observe that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\left\{\frac{p^{\prime}(\varrho)\varrho}{1-\rho_{f}}\right\}=\frac{1}{1-\rho_{f}}\left(\varrho\partial_{t}\varrho\,p^{\prime\prime}(\varrho)+p^{\prime}(\varrho)\partial_{t}\varrho\right)+p^{\prime}(\varrho)\varrho\frac{\partial_{t}\rho_{f}}{(1-\rho_{f})^{2}},

therefore by the Sobolev embedding, we get for all
s>d/2

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{t}\left\{\frac{p^{\prime}(\varrho)\varrho}{1-\rho_{f}}\right\}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{\infty})}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{1}{1-\rho_{f}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{\infty})}\|\partial_{t}\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{\infty})}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\times\left(\left\|\varrho\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{s})}\left\|p^{\prime\prime}(\varrho)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{s})}+\left\|p^{\prime}(\varrho)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{s})}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|\frac{1}{1-\rho_{f}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{\infty})}^{2}\|p^{\prime}(\varrho)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{s})}\|\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{s})}\|\partial_{t}\rho_{f}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{L}^{\infty})}.

Using Lemma
6.7
with Proposition
2.20
, we obtain as before

\displaystyle|\mathbf{B}_{0}(t,x,\gamma,\tau,k)|\leq CT\Lambda\left(T,R\right).

All in all, we have for all
t\in[0,T]

\displaystyle|\mathbf{A}_{0}(t,x,\gamma,\tau,k)|+|\mathbf{B}_{0}(t,x,\gamma,\tau,k)|\leq C\left(\left\|\frac{p^{\prime}(\varrho^{\mathrm{in}})\varrho^{\mathrm{in}}}{1-\rho_{f^{\mathrm{in}}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}+1\right)T\Lambda\left(T,R\right).

Consequently, there exists
\eta_{0}>0
independent of
\varepsilon
(and depending on
c
) such that for
\widetilde{T}_{0}=\widetilde{T}_{0}(R)
satisfying

\displaystyle C\left(\left\|\frac{p^{\prime}(\varrho^{\mathrm{in}})\varrho^{\mathrm{in}}}{1-\rho_{f^{\mathrm{in}}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}+1\right)\widetilde{T}_{0}\Lambda\left(\widetilde{T}_{0},R\right)\leq\eta_{0},

the
\frac{c}{2}
-Penrose stability condition
\eqref{cond:Penrose}_{c/2}
holds true for
(f(t),\varrho(t))
whenever
t\in\left[0,\left(\widetilde{T}_{0}(R),T^{\varepsilon}\right)\right]
, provided that
(f^{\mathrm{in}},\varrho^{\mathrm{in}})
satisfies the
c
-Penrose stability condition
\eqref{cond:Penrose}_{c}
.

∎

6.3.
Extension of the solution

In this section, our goal is to construct a suitable extension in time of the solution
(f_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon},u_{\varepsilon})
on the whole line
\mathbb{R}
. This technical step is required in view of the subsequent pseudodifferential analysis (in time-space) of Sections
6.4
–
6.5
– the symbols being dependent on our solutions.
A main issue is to obtain an extension still satisfying the Penrose stability condition (for all times): we refer to the later Proposition
6.18
.

The parameter
R
being fixed, there exists by continuity a nonnegative time
\widehat{T}(R)\leq 1
(independent of
\varepsilon
) such that

\displaystyle\forall T\in[0,\widehat{T}(R)],\ \ T\Lambda(T,R)\leq T^{1/2}\Lambda(T,R)\leq T^{1/4}\Lambda(T,R)\leq 1.

Define
T^{\star}_{\varepsilon}
(depending on
R
) as

(6.7)

\displaystyle T^{\star}_{\varepsilon}:=\min\left(T_{\varepsilon}(R),\overline{T}(R),\widetilde{T}_{0}(R),\widehat{T}(R)\right).

In particular, the Penrose stability condition holds on
[0,T^{\star}_{\varepsilon}]
thanks to Lemma
6.8
.

Consider two nonnegative nonincreasing cutoffs
\chi,\underline{\chi}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
such that

\displaystyle\forall t\in\mathbb{R},\ \ \chi(t)=\left\{\begin{aligned} &1,\ \ &&t\leq 0,\\
&0,\ \ &&t\geq 1,\end{aligned}\right.,\ \ \ \ \underline{\chi}(t)=\left\{\begin{aligned} &1,\ \ &&t\leq 0,\\
&1/2,\ \ &&t\geq 1.\end{aligned}\right.

We set for
\delta>0
to be fixed later,
\chi_{\delta}(t):=\chi(t/\delta)
.

Given a solution
(f,\varrho,u)
to the system (

S
ε

), we consider its extension
(\widetilde{f},\widetilde{\varrho},\widetilde{u})
as follows. Given
(N_{u},N_{f},N_{\varrho})\in\mathbb{N}^{3}
to be determined later on (by the number of derivatives we will use), we define:

\bullet

Extension in time for
u
: we set

\displaystyle\widetilde{u}(t)=\left\{\begin{aligned} &u(t),\ \ &&t\in[0,T^{\star}_{\varepsilon}],\\[5.69054pt]
&\chi(t-T^{\star}_{\varepsilon})\sum_{k=0}^{N_{u}}\partial_{t}^{k}u(T^{\star}_{\varepsilon})\frac{(t-T^{\star}_{\varepsilon})^{k}}{k!},\ \ &&t\geq T^{\star}_{\varepsilon},\\
&\chi(-t)\sum_{k=0}^{N_{u}}\partial_{t}^{k}u(0)\frac{t^{k}}{k!},\ \ &&t\leq 0.\end{aligned}\right.

In particular, the extension is
0
after
t=T^{\star}_{\varepsilon}+1
and before
t=-1
.

\bullet

Extension in time for
f
: we set

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}(t)=\left\{\begin{aligned} &f(t),\ \ &&t\in[0,T^{\star}_{\varepsilon}],\\[5.69054pt]
&\chi_{\delta}(t-T^{\star}_{\varepsilon})f(T^{\star}_{\varepsilon})+\chi_{\delta}(t-T^{\star}_{\varepsilon})\sum_{k=1}^{N_{f}}\partial_{t}^{k}f(T^{\star}_{\varepsilon})\frac{(t-T^{\star}_{\varepsilon})^{k}}{k!},\ \ &&t\geq T^{\star}_{\varepsilon},\\
&\chi_{\delta}(-t)f^{\mathrm{in}}+\chi_{\delta}(-t)\sum_{k=1}^{N_{f}}\partial_{t}^{k}f(0)\frac{t^{k}}{k!},\ \ &&t\leq 0.\end{aligned}\right.

In particular, the extension is
0
after
t=T^{\star}_{\varepsilon}+\delta
and before
t=-\delta
.

\bullet

Extension in time for
\varrho
: we set

\displaystyle\widetilde{\varrho}(t)=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\varrho(t),\ \ &&t\in[0,T^{\star}_{\varepsilon}],\\[5.69054pt]
&\underline{\chi}(t-T^{\star}_{\varepsilon})\varrho(T^{\star}_{\varepsilon})+\chi_{\delta}(t-T^{\star}_{\varepsilon})\sum_{k=1}^{N_{\varrho}}\partial_{t}^{k}\varrho(T^{\star}_{\varepsilon})\frac{(t-T^{\star}_{\varepsilon})^{k}}{k!},\ \ &&t\geq T^{\star}_{\varepsilon},\\
&\underline{\chi}(-t)\varrho^{\mathrm{in}}+\chi_{\delta}(-t)\sum_{k=1}^{N_{\varrho}}\partial_{t}^{k}\varrho(0)\frac{t^{k}}{k!},\ \ &&t\leq 0.\end{aligned}\right.

In particular, the extension is constant in time equal to
\varrho(T^{\star}_{\varepsilon})/2
after
t=T^{\star}_{\varepsilon}+1
and equal to
\varrho^{\mathrm{in}}/2
before
t=-1

The bounds from above and below (
B

(
T
)

Θ

μ
,
θ

) on
\varrho
and
\rho_{f}
are still valid for
\widetilde{\varrho}
and
\rho_{\widetilde{f}}
, provided that we choose the parameter
\delta
small enough.

Remark 6.9
.

Since
T^{\star}_{\varepsilon}\leq 1
, we observe that the Penrose function
\mathscr{P}_{\widetilde{f}(t),\widetilde{\varrho}(t)}
has a compact support in time included in
[0,2]
.

Here ever after, we drop out the tilde notation and we shall always consider the extension of our solutions. Let us conclude this section by explaining how we will deal with such an extension:

•

Replacing the former solution (defined on
[0,T^{\star}_{\varepsilon}]
) by its extension
(f,\varrho,u)
on
\mathbb{R}
, we observe that
(f,\varrho,u)
satisfies (

S
ε

) with the addition of a new source term
S^{new}
in the r.h.s which has a support included in
\mathbb{R}{\setminus}[0,T^{\star}_{\varepsilon}]
.

•

The results of Section
5
and Subsection
6.1
remain true on
[0,T^{\star}_{\varepsilon})
.

We also refer to Proposition
6.18
below where we will prove that the extension
(f,\varrho,u)
satisfies a Penrose stability condition for all times (the proof requiring some technical estimates from the upcoming Section
6.4
).

6.4.
Bounds on the symbols

The aim of this section is twofold:

•

obtain some bounds in terms of the initial data for some symbol seminorms of the Penrose function introduced in (
1.3
) (depending on the extension
(f,\varrho)
);

•

propagate the Penrose stability condition (
P
) on the whole line in time for the extension
(f,\varrho,u)
.

These two ingredients are required to obtain crucial elliptic estimates in Section
6.5
.

Before stating the next lemma, consider the symbol seminorms (
C.1
)–(
C.2
)–(
C.3
) introduced in the Section
C
in the Appendix: for any
\mathrm{M}\geq 0
and for any symbol
a(t,x,\eta)
with
\eta=(\gamma,\tau,k)\in(0,+\infty)\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}{\setminus}{\{0\}}
, we set

\displaystyle\omega[a]

\displaystyle:=\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{d}\\
\alpha_{i}\in\{0,1\}\end{subarray}}{\sup}\,\left\|(1+t)\partial_{x}^{\alpha}a\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{t,x,\eta}}+\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{d}\\
\alpha_{i}\in\{0,1\}\end{subarray}}{\sup}\,\left\|(1+t)\partial_{t}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}a\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{t,x,\eta}},

\displaystyle\Omega[a]

\displaystyle:=\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{d}\\
\alpha_{i}\in\{0,1\}\end{subarray}}{\sup}\,\left\{\left\||\eta|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\nabla_{\tau,k}a\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{t,x,\eta}}+\left\||\eta|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\nabla_{\tau,k}\partial_{t}a\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{t,x,\eta}}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad+\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{d}\\
\alpha_{i}\in\{0,1\}\end{subarray}}{\sup}\,\left\{\left\||\eta|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{\tau}\nabla_{\tau,k}a\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{t,x,\eta}}+\left\||\eta|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{\tau}\nabla_{\tau,k}\partial_{t}a\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{t,x,\eta}}\right\},

\displaystyle\Xi[a]_{\mathrm{M}}

\displaystyle:=\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq|\alpha|\leq 1+\mathrm{M}\\
\beta=0,1,2,3,4\end{subarray}}{\sup}\,\left\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{\beta}a\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{t,x,\eta}}.

Lemma 6.10
.

For
(t,x,\gamma,\tau,k)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{d}\times(0,+\infty)\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}{\setminus}\{0\}
, set

(6.8)

\displaystyle a_{f}(t,x,\gamma,\tau,k)

\displaystyle:=\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-(\gamma+i\tau)s}ik\cdot\left(\mathcal{F}_{v}\nabla_{v}f\right)(t,x,ks)\,\mathrm{d}s.

The symbol
a_{f}
is (positively) homogeneous of degree zero in
(\gamma,\tau,k)
in the sense that

\displaystyle\forall(t,x),\ \ \forall\eta=(\gamma,\tau,k),\ \ \forall\lambda>0\ \ a_{f}(t,x,\lambda\eta)=a_{f}\left(t,x,\eta\right).

Furthermore, for any
A>0
and
r>d/2+4
, we have

(6.9)

\displaystyle\omega[a_{f}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\underset{i=0,1}{\sup}\|(1+t)\partial_{t}^{i}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R};\mathcal{H}^{\ell}_{r})},\ \ 3+\frac{3d}{2}<\ell,

(6.10)

\displaystyle\Xi[a]_{M}

\displaystyle\lesssim\underset{i=0,1,2,3,4}{\sup}\,\|\partial_{t}^{i}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R};\mathcal{H}^{\ell}_{r})},\ \ 4+M+\frac{d}{2}<\ell,

(6.11)

\displaystyle\Omega[a_{f}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\underset{i=0,1}{\sup}\|\partial_{t}^{i}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R};\mathcal{H}^{\ell}_{r})},\ \ 7+\frac{3d}{2}<\ell.

Proof.

The homogeneity is obtained by performing the change of variable
s=\frac{s^{\prime}}{\lambda}
(for
\lambda>0
) in the integrals in
s
defining
a_{f}(t,x,\eta)
. In what follows, we will rely on the estimate

(6.12)

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}^{\delta}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}\mathcal{F}_{v}\nabla_{v}f(t,x,\xi)|\lesssim\frac{1}{1+|\xi|^{q}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(1+|v|^{2})^{\sigma+|\beta|}|\nabla_{v}\partial_{t}^{\delta}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}(I-\Delta_{v})^{\frac{q}{2}}f(t,x,v)|^{2}\ \mathrm{d}v\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

which is valid for all
\sigma>d/2
,
q>0
and any
(\delta,\alpha,\beta)\in\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}^{d}\times\mathbb{N}^{d}
.

Consequently, for any
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
with
\alpha_{i}=0,1
, we apply (
6.12
) with
\delta=0,1
,
q=2
and
\beta=0
, and obtain for
\chi(v)=(1+|v|^{2})^{\frac{\sigma}{2}}

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}^{\delta}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}a_{f}(t,x,\eta)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(1+|v|^{2})^{\sigma}|\nabla_{v}\partial_{t}^{\delta}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}(I-\Delta_{v})f(t,x,v)|^{2}\ \mathrm{d}v\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{|k|}{1+s^{2}|k|^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\chi\partial_{t}^{\delta}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}f(t,x)\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{3}_{v}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{1+s^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{t}^{\delta}f(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\sigma}^{3+|\alpha|+\frac{d}{2}+\kappa}},

for all
\kappa>0
, thanks to the Sobolev embedding. We then deduce

\displaystyle\omega[a_{f}]\lesssim\|(1+t)f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}\big(\mathbb{R};\mathcal{H}_{\sigma}^{3+d+\frac{d}{2}+\kappa}\big)}+\|(1+t)\partial_{t}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}\big(\mathbb{R};\mathcal{H}_{\sigma}^{3+d+\frac{d}{2}+\kappa}\big)},

and hence the claimed inequality (
6.9
). The inequality (
6.10
) can be obtained in the same way.

Let us now turn to the proof of (
6.11
). First, observe by the homogeneity of
a_{f}
in
\eta=(\gamma,\tau,k)
, it is enough to estimates the quantities

\left\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\nabla_{\tau,k}\partial_{t}^{\delta}a_{f}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{t,x}\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{\eta}(S^{+})}
and

\left\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{\tau}\nabla_{\tau,k}\partial_{t}^{\delta}a\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{t,x}\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{\eta}(S^{+})}
with
\delta=0,1
,
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
with
\alpha_{i}=0,1
and where

S^{+}:=\left\{\widetilde{\eta}=(\widetilde{\gamma},\widetilde{\tau},\widetilde{k})\in(0,+\infty)\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}{\setminus}\{0\}\mid\widetilde{\gamma}^{2}+\widetilde{\tau}^{2}+\widetilde{k}^{2}=1\right\}.

We thus need to estimate the following symbols

\displaystyle I^{\alpha,\delta}_{1}(t,x,\widetilde{\eta})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-(\widetilde{\gamma}+i\widetilde{\tau})s}n\cdot\left(\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{\delta}\mathcal{F}_{v}\nabla_{v}f\right)(t,x,\widetilde{k}s)\,\mathrm{d}s,\ \ n\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\ \ |n|=1,

\displaystyle I^{\alpha,\delta}_{2}(t,x,\widetilde{\eta})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-(\widetilde{\gamma}+i\widetilde{\tau})s}s\widetilde{k}\cdot\left(\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{\delta}\mathcal{F}_{v}\nabla_{v}f\right)(t,x,\widetilde{k}s)\,\mathrm{d}s,\ \ |\beta|\in\{0,1\}.

and

\displaystyle J^{\alpha,\delta}_{1,q_{1}}(t,x,\widetilde{\eta})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-(\widetilde{\gamma}+i\widetilde{\tau})s}s^{q_{1}}n\cdot\left(\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{\delta}\mathcal{F}_{v}\nabla_{v}f\right)(t,x,\widetilde{k}s)\,\mathrm{d}s,\ \ n\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\ \ |n|=1,\ \ q_{1}\in\{1,2\},

\displaystyle J^{\alpha,\delta}_{2,q_{2}}(t,x,\widetilde{\eta})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-(\widetilde{\gamma}+i\widetilde{\tau})s}s^{q_{2}}\widetilde{k}\cdot\left(\partial_{\xi}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{\delta}\mathcal{F}_{v}\nabla_{v}f\right)(t,x,\widetilde{k}s)\,\mathrm{d}s,\ \ q_{2}\in\{2,3\},

for
\widetilde{\eta}\in S^{+}
.

We focus on the terms
J^{\alpha,\delta}_{1,q_{1}}
and
J^{\alpha,\delta}_{2,q_{2}}
by following
[
HKR16
, Lemma 16]
, the treatment of the symbols
I^{\alpha,\delta}_{1}
and
I^{\alpha,\delta}_{2}
being similar and involving fewer derivatives.

If
|\widetilde{k}|\geq 1/2
, invoking (
6.12
) with
q=q_{1}+3
and
\beta=0
yields as before

\displaystyle|J^{\alpha,\delta}_{1,q_{1}}(t,x,\widetilde{\eta})|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(1+|v|^{2})^{\sigma}|\nabla_{v}\partial_{t}^{\delta}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}(I-\Delta_{v})^{\frac{q_{1}+3}{2}}f(t,x,v)|^{2}\ \mathrm{d}v\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{s^{q_{1}}}{1+s^{q_{1}+3}|\widetilde{k}|^{q_{1}+3}}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{t}^{\delta}f(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\sigma}^{4+q_{1}+|\alpha|+{\frac{d}{2}}^{+}}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{s^{q_{1}}}{1+s^{q_{1}+3}}\,\mathrm{d}s,

since
|\widetilde{k}|
is bounded from below. We thus obtain a uniform estimate in this case. Otherwise, if
|\widetilde{k}|\leq 1/2
, then
\widetilde{\gamma}^{2}+\widetilde{\tau}^{2}\geq 3/4
and we can therefore rely on the exponential to integrate by parts in
s
in the integral defining
J^{\alpha,\delta}_{1,q_{1}}(t,x,\widetilde{\eta})
. If
q_{1}=1
, we first get

\displaystyle J^{\alpha,\delta}_{1,1}(t,x,\widetilde{\eta})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\widetilde{\gamma}+i\widetilde{\tau}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-(\widetilde{\gamma}+i\widetilde{\tau})s}n\cdot\left(\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{\delta}\mathcal{F}_{v}\nabla_{v}f\right)(t,x,\widetilde{k}s)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{\widetilde{\gamma}+i\widetilde{\tau}}\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-(\widetilde{\gamma}+i\widetilde{\tau})s}sn\cdot\left(\mathrm{D}_{\xi}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{\delta}\mathcal{F}_{v}\nabla_{v}f\right)(t,x,\widetilde{k}s)\widetilde{k}\,\mathrm{d}s,

and integrating by parts once again yields the estimate (since
\widetilde{\gamma}^{2}+\widetilde{\tau}^{2}\geq 3/4
)

\displaystyle|J^{\alpha,\delta}_{1,1}(t,x,\widetilde{\eta})|

\displaystyle\lesssim|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{\delta}\mathcal{F}_{v}\nabla_{v}f(t,x,0)|+\int_{0}^{+\infty}|\widetilde{k}||\mathrm{D}_{\xi}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{\delta}\mathcal{F}_{v}\nabla_{v}f(t,x,\widetilde{k}s)|\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{+\infty}s|\widetilde{k}|^{2}|\mathrm{D}^{2}_{\xi}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{\delta}\mathcal{F}_{v}\nabla_{v}f(t,x,\widetilde{k}s)|\,\mathrm{d}s.

Using (
6.12
) with
q=2
,
|\beta|=0,1
, or
q=3
,
|\beta|=2
now provides for all
\kappa>0

\displaystyle|J^{\alpha,\delta}_{1,1}(t,x,\widetilde{\eta})|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{t}^{\delta}f(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\sigma}^{4+|\alpha|+\frac{d}{2}+\kappa}}\left(1+\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{|\widetilde{k}|}{1+|\widetilde{k}|^{2}s^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{|\widetilde{k}|^{2}s}{1+|\widetilde{k}|^{3}s^{3}}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{t}^{\delta}f(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\sigma+2}^{4+|\alpha|+\frac{d}{2}+\kappa}}\left(1+\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{1+s^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{s}{1+s^{3}}\,\mathrm{d}s\right),

and hence the uniform estimate for this symbol. If
q_{1}=2
, we use the same strategy with additional integration by parts and (
6.12
) with
q=2
,
|\beta|=0,1
, or
q=3
,
|\beta|=2
, or
q=4
,
|\beta|=3
to get

\displaystyle|J^{\alpha,\delta}_{1,2}(t,x,\widetilde{\eta})|

\displaystyle\lesssim|J^{\alpha,\delta}_{1,1}(t,x,\widetilde{\eta})|+\int_{0}^{+\infty}|\widetilde{k}||\mathrm{D}_{\xi}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{\delta}\mathcal{F}_{v}\nabla_{v}f(t,x,\widetilde{k}s)|\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{+\infty}s|\widetilde{k}|^{2}|\mathrm{D}^{2}_{\xi}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{\delta}\mathcal{F}_{v}\nabla_{v}f(t,x,\widetilde{k}s)|\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{+\infty}s^{2}|\widetilde{k}|^{3}|\mathrm{D}^{3}_{\xi}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{\delta}\mathcal{F}_{v}\nabla_{v}f(t,x,\widetilde{k}s)|\,\mathrm{d}s.

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{t}^{\delta}f(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\sigma+3}^{5+|\alpha|+{\frac{d}{2}}^{+}}}\left(1+\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{|\widetilde{k}|}{1+|\widetilde{k}|^{2}s^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{|\widetilde{k}|^{2}s}{1+|\widetilde{k}|^{3}s^{3}}\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{|\widetilde{k}|^{3}s^{2}}{1+|\widetilde{k}|^{4}s^{4}}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{t}^{\delta}f(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\sigma+3}^{5+|\alpha|+{\frac{d}{2}}^{+}}}\left(1+\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{1+s^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{s}{1+s^{3}}\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{s^{2}}{1+s^{4}}\,\mathrm{d}s\right).

Gathering the two cases together, we deduce the estimate

\displaystyle\left\|J^{\alpha,\delta}_{1,q_{1}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{t,x}\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{\eta}(S^{+})}\lesssim\|\partial_{t}^{\delta}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}\big(\mathbb{R};\mathcal{H}_{\sigma+3}^{6+|\alpha|+\frac{d}{2}+\kappa}\big)},

for all
\kappa>0
. Likewise, we can apply these arguments to
J^{\alpha,\delta}_{2,q_{2}}
, by invoking again (
6.12
) with
q=q_{2}+3
,
\beta=1
if
|\widetilde{k}|\leq 1/2
, or with at most
q=5
,
\beta=4
if
|\widetilde{k}|\geq 1/2
. All in all, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|J^{\alpha,\delta}_{2,q_{1}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{t,x}\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{\eta}(S^{+})}\lesssim\|\partial_{t}^{\delta}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}\big(\mathbb{R};\mathcal{H}_{\sigma+4}^{7+|\alpha|+\frac{d}{2}+\kappa}\big)}.

We have eventually proven the estimate (
6.11
).
∎

In view of Lemma
6.10
, it will be useful to have the following estimates on
f
.

Lemma 6.11
.

For
k>d
and
\sigma>0
, we have

(6.13)

\displaystyle\underset{i=0,1}{\sup}\,\left\|(1+t)\partial_{t}^{i}f\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R};\mathcal{H}^{k}_{\sigma})}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda(1+M_{\mathrm{in}}),\ \ \sigma+4<r,\ \ k+3<m,

(6.14)

\displaystyle\underset{i=0,1,2,3,4}{\sup}\,\left\|\partial_{t}^{i}f\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R};\mathcal{H}^{k}_{\sigma})}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda(1+M_{\mathrm{in}}),\ \ \sigma+4<r,\ \ k+6<m.

Proof.

Thanks to our choice of extension for
(f,\varrho,u)
, and picking
N_{f}=4,N_{\varrho}=3
and
N_{u}=3
, it is sufficient to study the estimates on
[0,T]
with
T\in[0,T^{\star}_{\varepsilon}]
. Here, we shall constantly use
Remarks
2.21

–
2.25
.
Taking all the exponants
k
large enough in the following, we can always assume that the Sobolev spaces that we use are algebras.

We proceed inductively, relying on the equations satisfied by
f
,
\varrho
and
u
. For
i=0
, we directly use Lemma
2.22

with
k\leq m-1
to get

\displaystyle\left\|f\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{k}_{\sigma})}\leq\|f^{\mathrm{in}}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m-1}_{\sigma}}+T^{\frac{1}{4}}\Lambda\left(T,R\right)\leq M_{\mathrm{in}}+1.

For
i=1
, we use Lemma
6.7
to get for
k>d
and
k+1>d/2

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{k}_{\sigma})}\lesssim\|f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{k+1}_{\sigma+1})}+\|f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{k+1}_{\sigma})}\left(\|u\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{k})}+\Lambda\left(\|\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{k+1})}\right)\right),

therefore using Remarks
2.21
–
2.25
and the previous estimate on
f
, we deduce

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{k}_{\sigma})}\leq\Lambda(1+M_{\mathrm{in}}),

if
k\leq m-3
. For
i=2
, we take one derivative in time in the Vlasov equation and get

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{2}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{k}_{\sigma})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{t}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{k+1}_{\sigma+1})}

\displaystyle\quad+\|E_{\mathrm{reg},\varepsilon}^{\varrho,u}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{k})}\|\partial_{t}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{k+1}_{\sigma})}+\|\partial_{t}E_{\mathrm{reg},\varepsilon}^{\varrho,u}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{k})}\|f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{k+1}_{\sigma})}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda(1+M_{\mathrm{in}})+\Lambda(1+M_{\mathrm{in}})\|\partial_{t}E_{\mathrm{reg},\varepsilon}^{\varrho,u}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{k})},

if we take
k\leq m-4
. Since

\displaystyle\partial_{t}E_{\mathrm{reg},\varepsilon}^{\varrho,u}=\partial_{t}u-\partial_{t}\varrho p^{\prime\prime}(\varrho)\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\varrho-p^{\prime}(\varrho)\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\partial_{t}\varrho,

it is enough to estimate
\left\|\partial_{t}u\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{k}}
and
\left\|\partial_{t}\varrho\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{k+1}}
. Thanks to the equation on
u
and on
\varrho
, we easily get the fact that this involves
\rho_{f},j_{f},\varrho
and
u
in
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{k+2})
. Using Lemma
2.22
and
2.21
–
2.25
with
k\leq m-4
shows that

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{2}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{k}_{\sigma})}\lesssim\Lambda(1+M_{\mathrm{in}}).

Now for
i=3
, we observe that
\|\partial_{t}^{3}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{k})}
is estimated by, at most,
\|\partial_{t}^{2}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{k+1}_{\sigma+1})}
and
\|\partial_{t}^{2}E_{\mathrm{reg},\varepsilon}^{\varrho,u}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{k}_{\sigma})}
, thus requiring to estimate at most
\left\|\partial_{t}^{2}u\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{k}}
and
\left\|\partial_{t}^{2}\varrho\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{k+1}}
. Using again the equation on
u
and
\varrho
, this now involves
u
in
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{k+4})
and
\rho_{f},j_{f},\varrho
in
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{k+3})
. Using Lemma
2.22
and Remarks
2.21
–
2.25
with
k\leq m-5
shows that

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{3}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{k}_{\sigma})}\leq\Lambda(1+M_{\mathrm{in}}).

The same can be done for
i=4
, implying for
k\leq m-6

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{4}f\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{k}_{\sigma})}\leq\Lambda(1+M_{\mathrm{in}}).

This allows us to conclude the proof.
∎

We are now in position to prove the following result on the Penrose symbol
\mathscr{P}_{f,\varrho}
, which is, as we recall here:

\displaystyle\mathscr{P}_{f,\varrho}(t,x,\gamma,\tau,\eta)=\frac{p^{\prime}(\varrho(t,x))\varrho(t,x)}{1-\rho_{f}(t,x)}\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-(\gamma+i\tau)s}\frac{ik}{1+|k|^{2}}\cdot\left(\mathcal{F}_{v}\nabla_{v}f\right)(t,x,\eta s)\,\mathrm{d}s,

and is defined for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
. The symbol seminorms (
C.1
)–(
C.2
)–(
C.3
) of
\mathscr{P}_{f,\varrho}
are first estimated as follows.

Lemma 6.12
.

For any
k>d/2
and
\mathrm{M}\geq 0
, we have

(6.15)

\displaystyle\omega[\mathscr{P}_{f,\varrho}]

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda\left(\underset{i=0,1}{\sup}\left\|\partial_{t}^{i}\varrho\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R};\mathrm{H}^{d+k})},\underset{i=0,1}{\sup}\left\|\partial_{t}^{i}\rho_{f}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R};\mathrm{H}^{d+k})}\right)\omega[a_{f}],

(6.16)

\displaystyle\Xi[{P}_{f,\varrho}]_{\mathrm{M}}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda\left(\underset{i=0,1,2,3,4}{\sup}\left\|\partial_{t}^{i}\varrho\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R};\mathrm{H}^{1+M+k})},\underset{i=0,1,2,3,4}{\sup}\left\|\partial_{t}^{i}\rho_{f}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R};\mathrm{H}^{1+\mathrm{M}+k})}\right)\Xi[a_{f}]_{\mathrm{M}},

(6.17)

\displaystyle\Omega[\mathscr{P}_{f,\varrho}]

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda\left(\underset{i=0,1}{\sup}\left\|\partial_{t}^{i}\varrho\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R};\mathrm{H}^{d+k})},\underset{i=0,1}{\sup}\left\|\partial_{t}^{i}\rho_{f}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R};\mathrm{H}^{d+k})}\right)\Omega[a_{f}].

Proof.

Since the symbol
\mathscr{P}_{f,\varrho}
depends only on
(\gamma,\tau,k)
through
a_{f}
, we only prove the estimate (
6.15
), the treatment of (
6.16
) and (
6.17
) being similar.

First, we write the symbol
\mathscr{P}_{f,\varrho}
as

\mathscr{P}_{f,\varrho}(t,x,\gamma,\tau,k)=\mathfrak{m}(\varrho(t,x),\rho_{f}(t,x))\frac{1}{1+|k|^{2}}a_{f}(t,x,\gamma,\tau,k),

where
a_{f}
has been defined in (
6.8
) and where

\mathfrak{m}(\varrho(t,x),\rho_{f}(t,x)):=\frac{p^{\prime}(\varrho(t,x))\varrho(t,x)}{1-\rho_{f}(t,x)}.

We have

\displaystyle\omega[\mathscr{P}_{f,\varrho}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\times\mathbb{N}^{d}\\
\alpha_{i}\in\{0,1\}\end{subarray}}{\sup}\left\|(1+t)\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\left(\mathfrak{m}(\varrho,\rho_{f})a_{f}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{\eta})}+\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\times\mathbb{N}^{d}\\
\alpha_{i}\in\{0,1\}\end{subarray}}{\sup}\left\|(1+t)\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\left(\mathfrak{m}(\varrho,\rho_{f})\partial_{t}a_{f}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{\eta})}

\displaystyle\quad+\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\times\mathbb{N}^{d}\\
\alpha_{i}\in\{0,1\}\end{subarray}}{\sup}\left\|(1+t)\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\left(\partial_{1}\mathfrak{m}(\varrho,\rho_{f})\partial_{t}\varrho+\partial_{2}\mathfrak{m}(\varrho,\rho_{f})\partial_{t}\rho_{f})a_{f}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{\eta})}.

Using Leibniz rule, we get for all
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
such that
\alpha_{i}\in\{0,1\}

\displaystyle(1+t)\left|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\left(\mathfrak{m}(\varrho,\rho_{f})a_{f}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\beta\leq\alpha}|\partial_{x}^{\beta}\left(\mathfrak{m}(\varrho,\rho_{f})\right)||\partial_{x}^{\alpha-\beta}a_{f}|\leq\omega[a_{f}]\sum_{\beta\leq\alpha}|\partial_{x}^{\beta}\left(\mathfrak{m}(\varrho,\rho_{f})\right)|,

and one can observe that for all
k>\frac{d}{2}

\displaystyle\sum_{\beta\leq\alpha}|\partial_{x}^{\beta}\left(\mathfrak{m}(\varrho,\rho_{f})\right)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\mathfrak{m}(\varrho,\rho_{f})\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{d+k}}\leq\Lambda(\left\|\varrho\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}},\left\|\rho_{f}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}})\left\|\varrho\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{d+k}}\left\|\rho_{f}\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{d+k}},

where we have used Proposition
A.3
in the Appendix and the fact that
\mathrm{H}^{k+d}
is an algebra. Doing the same with the term involving
\partial_{t}a_{f}
, we obtain

\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\times\mathbb{N}^{d}\\
\alpha_{i}\in\{0,1\}\end{subarray}}{\sup}\left\|(1+t)\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\left(\mathfrak{m}(\varrho,\rho_{f})a_{f}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{\eta})}+\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\times\mathbb{N}^{d}\\
\alpha_{i}\in\{0,1\}\end{subarray}}{\sup}\left\|(1+t)\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\left(\mathfrak{m}(\varrho,\rho_{f})\partial_{t}a_{f}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{\eta})}\\
\leq\Lambda\left(\left\|\varrho\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R};\mathrm{L}^{\infty})},\left\|\rho_{f}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R};\mathrm{L}^{\infty})}\right)\left\|\varrho\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R};\mathrm{H}^{d+k})}\left\|\rho_{f}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R};\mathrm{H}^{d+k})}\omega[a_{f}].

Likewise, the same kind of computations, that we do not detail, show that for all
k>\frac{d}{2}

\displaystyle\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\times\mathbb{N}^{d}\\
\alpha_{i}\in\{0,1\}\end{subarray}}{\sup}\left\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\left([\partial_{1}G(\varrho,\rho_{f})\partial_{t}\varrho+\partial_{2}G(\varrho,\rho_{f})]\partial_{t}\rho_{f})a_{f}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{\eta})}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda\left(\left\|\varrho\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R};\mathrm{L}^{\infty})},\left\|\rho_{f}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R};\mathrm{L}^{\infty})}\right)
