In what follows, we shall only present the main modifications which have to be added to the strategy used in this article.

10.1.
Modification of the energy estimates and of the bootstrap argument

Our first goal is to adapt the energy estimates of Section
2
and the bootstrap procedure to the density-dependent drag case. The main change comes from the estimate for
f
from Proposition
2.10
.

Indeed, let us set

\mathcal{T}^{u,\varrho}_{\mathrm{drag}}=\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}-\varrho v\cdot\nabla_{v}+E^{u,\varrho}(t,x)\cdot\nabla_{v}-d\varrho\mathrm{Id},

where
E^{u,\varrho}_{\mathrm{drag}}:=\varrho u-\nabla_{x}p(\varrho)
. Observe that because of the term
\varrho v\cdot\nabla_{v}
(coming from friction), a growth in velocity can occur in the analysis, that is if
f
is controlled in
\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r}
, then this term would
a priori
require a control in
\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r+1}
. This explains the additional assumption of compact support in velocity in the next proposition. We mention though that the use of exponential-weighted norms in velocity could relax this assumption (see the work
[
Asa86
]
on the Vlasov-Maxwell equations, and also
[
CJ22a
]
in the context of fluid-kinetic equations).

Proposition 10.2
.

For all
r\geq 0
,
m>3+d/2
,
c>0
and all
T>0
, for all smooth functions
(f,\varrho,u)
with
f
having a compact support in velocity:

\displaystyle\forall t\in[0,T],\ \ \mathrm{supp}\,f(t)\subset\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathrm{B}(0,M(t)),

for some
M\in\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T)
, satisfying

\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{drag}}^{u,\varrho}(f)=0\quad\text{on }[0,T],

and
\varrho\geq c
on
[0,T]
, the following holds. For all
t\in[0,T]
, we have

\displaystyle\|f(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|f(0)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r}}^{2}\exp\Big[C(1+\left\|M\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T)})\Big(T+\sqrt{T}\|u\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})}\|\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\sqrt{T}\Lambda\left(\|\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-2})}\right)\|\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m+1})}\Big)\Big],

Proof.

Let us suppose that there exists
M\in\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T)
sucht that

\displaystyle\forall t\in[0,T],\ \ \mathrm{supp}\,f(t)\subset\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathrm{B}(0,M(t)).

Since
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{drag}}^{u,\varrho}(f)=0
, we have by Lemma
2.9

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{drag}}^{u,\varrho}(\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{v}^{\beta}f)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1\\
\beta_{i}\neq 0\end{subarray}}^{d}\partial_{x}^{\widehat{\alpha}^{i}}\partial_{v}^{\widehat{\beta}^{i}}f+\left[\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{v}^{\beta},\varrho(t,x)v\cdot\nabla_{v}\right]-\left[\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{v}^{\beta},E_{\mathrm{drag}}^{u,\varrho}(t,x)\cdot\nabla_{v}\right]f+d\left[\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{v}^{\beta},\varrho\mathrm{Id}\right]f,

for all
\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
. We now take the scalar product of this equality with
(1+|v|^{2})^{r}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{v}^{\beta}f
, sum for all
|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq m
and then integrate on
\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
. For the left-hand side, we have as in Proposition
2.10

\sum_{|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq m}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}(1+|v|^{2})^{r}\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{drag}}^{u,\varrho}(\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{v}^{\beta}f)\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{v}^{\beta}f\\
=\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|f(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r}}^{2}-\sum_{|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq m}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla_{v}(1+|v|^{2})^{r}\cdot(E_{\mathrm{drag}}^{u,\varrho}-\varrho v)\frac{|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{v}^{\beta}f|^{2}}{2},

the last term satisfying

\displaystyle\sum_{|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq m}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla_{v}(1+|v|^{2})^{r}\cdot(E_{\mathrm{drag}}^{u,\varrho}-\varrho v)\frac{|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{v}^{\beta}f|^{2}}{2}\leq(\left\|\varrho(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}+\|E_{\mathrm{drag}}^{u,\varrho}(t)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}})\|f(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r}}^{2}.

We now look at the four terms of the right-hand side. For the first, third and fourth ones, we proceed as in the proof of Proposition
2.10
(with a variant of (
A.3
)) and get

\sum_{|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq m}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}(1+|v|^{2})^{r}\Bigg(-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1\\
\beta_{i}\neq 0\end{subarray}}^{d}\partial_{x}^{\widehat{\alpha}^{i}}\partial_{v}^{\widehat{\beta}^{i}}f+\left[\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{v}^{\beta},E_{\mathrm{drag}}^{u,\varrho}(t,x)\cdot\nabla_{v}+d\varrho\mathrm{Id}\right]f\Bigg)\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{v}^{\beta}f\\
\lesssim(1+\|E_{\mathrm{drag}}^{u,\varrho}(t)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}+\|\varrho(t)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}})\|f(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r}}^{2}.

The treatment of the third term requires the use of the compact support in velocity of
f
. Invoking the inequality (
A.4
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq m}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}(1+|v|^{2})^{r}\left[\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{v}^{\beta},\varrho(t,x)v\cdot\nabla_{v}\right]\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{v}^{\beta}f\lesssim(1+M(t))\|\varrho(t)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}\|f(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r}}^{2}.

All in all, we obtain

(10.3)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|f(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r}}^{2}\lesssim(1+M(t))(1+\|\varrho(t)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}+\|E^{u,\varrho}(t)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}})\|f(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r}}^{2},

if
m>d/2
. As in the proof of Proposition
2.10
, an by Sobolev embedding, we have

\displaystyle\|E^{u,\varrho}(t)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}\lesssim\|\varrho(t)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}\|u(t)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}+\Lambda\left(\|\varrho(t)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m-2}}\right)\|\varrho(t)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m+1}}.

By integrating in time the inequality (
10.3
), we get

\|f(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r}}^{2}\leq\|f(0)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r}}^{2}\\
+C\int_{0}^{t}(1+M(s))\left(1+\|\varrho(s)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}\|u(s)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}+\Lambda\left(\|\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-2})}\right)\|\varrho(s)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m+1}}\right)\|f(s)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s,

for all
t\in[0,T)
and for some constant
C>0
independent of
\varepsilon
. Using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the Grönwall’s inequality, this implies for all
t\in[0,T)

\displaystyle\|f(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|f(0)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r}}^{2}\exp\Big[C(1+\left\|M\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T)})\Big(T+\sqrt{T}\|u\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})})\|\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\sqrt{T}\Lambda\left(\|\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m-2})}\right)\|\varrho\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m+1})}\Big)\Big],

and this concludes the proof.
∎

The proof of the other estimates from Section
2
is mainly unchanged and details are left to the reader. We need to adapt the bootstrap procedure, by taking into account the need of a compact support in velocity. We thus define the following modified condition.

Definition 10.3
.

Let
T>0
. For any nonnegative functions
f(t,x,v)
and
\varrho(t,x)
on
[0,T]
, we define the property

(B
{}^{\mu,\theta}_{\Theta,M^{\mathrm{in}}}(T)
)

\displaystyle\forall t\in[0,T],\ \left\{\begin{aligned} \rho_{f}(t)\leq\frac{\Theta+1}{2},\ \ \ \frac{\mu}{2}\leq\varrho(t),\ \ \ \frac{\underline{\theta}}{2}\leq(1-\rho_{f}(t))\varrho(t)\leq 2\overline{\theta},\\[5.69054pt]
\mathrm{supp}\,f(t)\subset\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathrm{B}(0,1+M^{\mathrm{in}}),\end{aligned}\right.

where
\Theta,\mu,\underline{\theta},\overline{\theta}
are given in the statement of Theorem
1.5
and where
M^{\mathrm{in}}
has been introduced in (
10.2
).

If
T^{\ast}_{\varepsilon}>0
is the maximal time of existence to the system (

S
ε

) (with density-dependent drag term), we introduce the following time for all
\varepsilon>0
:

(10.4)

\displaystyle T_{\varepsilon}=T_{\varepsilon}(R):=\sup\big\{T\in[0,T^{\ast}_{\varepsilon}[,\ \ \mathcal{N}_{m,r}(f_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon},u_{\varepsilon},T)\leq R\ \ \text{and}\ \ \eqref{def:Bound-HAUTBAS-Compactsupp}\ \ \text{holds}\big\},

where
R>0
has to be chosen large enough and independent of
\varepsilon
. Here, the quantity
\mathcal{N}_{m,r}(f_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon},u_{\varepsilon},T)
is exactly the same as in Definition
2.19
.

10.2.
Modification in the straightening change of variable

As a matter of fact, the main difference in the analysis appears in the part related to characteristics, namely Section
3
. Our purpose here is to explain how to modify the arguments of Section
3
about the straightening change of variable in velocity, that is Lemma
3.1
. It turns out that in the density-dependent drag case, obtaining a suitable diffeomorphism
\psi^{s;t}_{x}
is not as straightforward as in Lemma
3.1
. Indeed, again because of the term
-\varrho v\cdot\nabla_{v}f
, there could be a growth in velocity in the dynamics which prevents our proof of Lemma
3.1
, which is based on a perturbative approach, to directly hold. This is where the assumption compact support in velocity appears to be crucial; we do not know whether it is possible to replace it here by an exponential moment assumption.

With the notations of Section
3
, we will actually directly straighten the total kinetic operator

\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{drag},\mathrm{F}}=\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}-\varrho(t,x)v\cdot\nabla_{v}+\mathrm{F}(t,x)\cdot\nabla_{v}-d\mathrm{Id},

into the free-transport operator

\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{free}}=\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}.

For
(x,v)\in\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathrm{B}(0,1+\mathrm{M}^{\mathrm{in}})
and
t\in[0,T]
, we consider the solution
s\mapsto(\mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{drag}}^{s;t},\mathrm{V}_{\mathrm{drag}}^{s;t})(x,v)
to the following system of differential equations

\left\{\begin{aligned} \frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}\mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{drag}}^{s;t}&=\mathrm{V}_{\mathrm{drag}}^{s;t},\ \ \mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{drag}}^{t;t}(x,v)=x,\\
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}\mathrm{V}_{\mathrm{drag}}^{s;t}&=-\varrho(s,\mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{drag}}^{s;t})\mathrm{V}_{\mathrm{drag}}^{s;t}+\mathrm{F}(s,\mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{drag}}^{s;t}),\ \ \mathrm{V}_{\mathrm{drag}}^{t;t}(x,v)=v.\\
\end{aligned}\right.

Lemma 10.4
.

Let
T>0
and
k\geq 1
. Let
\mathrm{F}\in\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{W}^{k,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))
be a vector field such that

\displaystyle\|\mathrm{F}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{W}^{k,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))}\leq\Lambda(T,\mathrm{R}),

for some
\mathrm{R}>0
.
There exists
\overline{T}(\mathrm{R})>0
such that for all
x\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
and
s,t\in[0,\min(\overline{T}(\mathrm{R}),T)]
, there exists a diffeomorphism
\psi_{s,t}(x,\cdot):\mathrm{B}(0,1+\mathrm{M}^{\mathrm{in}})\rightarrow\mathrm{B}(0,1+\mathrm{M}^{\mathrm{in}})
satisfying for all
v\in\mathrm{B}(0,1+\mathrm{M}^{\mathrm{in}})

\displaystyle\mathrm{X}^{s;t}_{\mathrm{drag}}\left(x,\psi_{s,t}(x,v)\right)=x-(t-s)v,

which furthermore verifies the estimates

\displaystyle\frac{1}{C}\leq\mathrm{det}\left(\mathrm{D}_{v}\psi_{s,t}(x,v)\right)

\displaystyle\leq C,

\displaystyle\underset{s,t\in[0,T]}{\sup}\,\left\|\partial_{x,v}^{\alpha}\left(\psi_{s,t}(x,v)-v\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq\varphi(T)\Lambda(T,\mathrm{R}),\ \ |\alpha|\leq k,

\displaystyle\underset{s,t\in[0,T]}{\sup}\,\left\|\partial_{x,v}^{\beta}\partial_{s}\psi_{s,t}(x,v)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq\varphi(T)\Lambda(T,\mathrm{R}),\ \ |\beta|\leq k-1,

for some
C>0
and some nondecreasing continuous function
\varphi:\mathbb{R}^{+}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
vanishing at
0
.

Proof.

Let us sketch the proof. Dropping the
(x,v)
dependency in the trajectories, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{V}_{\mathrm{drag}}^{s;t}=\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\varrho(\tau,\mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{drag}}^{\tau;t})\,\mathrm{d}\tau\right)v-\int_{s}^{t}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{\tau}\varrho(\tau^{\prime},\mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{drag}}^{\tau^{\prime};t})\,\mathrm{d}\tau^{\prime}\right)\mathrm{F}(\tau,\mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{drag}}^{\tau;t})\,\mathrm{d}\tau,

from which we deduce

\displaystyle\mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{drag}}^{s,t}

\displaystyle=x-\left(\int_{s}^{t}\exp\left(\int_{s^{\prime}}^{t}\varrho(\tau,\mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{drag}}^{\tau;t})\,\mathrm{d}\tau\right)\,\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\right)v+\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s^{\prime}}^{t}\exp\left(\int_{s^{\prime}}^{\tau}\varrho(\tau^{\prime},\mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{drag}}^{\tau^{\prime};t})\,\mathrm{d}\tau^{\prime}\right)\mathrm{F}(\tau,\mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{drag}}^{\tau;t})\,\mathrm{d}\tau\,\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}

\displaystyle=x-(t-s)\Bigg[v+\left(\frac{1}{t-s}\int_{s}^{t}\exp\left(\int_{s^{\prime}}^{t}\varrho(\tau,\mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{drag}}^{\tau;t})\,\mathrm{d}\tau\right)\,\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}-1\right)v

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\frac{1}{t-s}\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s^{\prime}}^{t}\exp\left(\int_{s^{\prime}}^{\tau}\varrho(\tau^{\prime},\mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{drag}}^{\tau^{\prime};t})\,\mathrm{d}\tau^{\prime}\right)\mathrm{F}(\tau,\mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{drag}}^{\tau;t})\,\mathrm{d}\tau\,\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\Bigg].

Taking one derivative in velocity, we observe that because of the term

\left(\frac{1}{t-s}\int_{s}^{t}\exp\left(\int_{s^{\prime}}^{t}\varrho(\tau,\mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{drag}}^{\tau;t})\,\mathrm{d}\tau\right)\,\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}-1\right)v,

we obtain a term where the derivative is not falling on the
v
factor. Hence, the latter is
a priori
unbounded, inducing a potential linear growth in velocity of the derivative. But since we restrict to
v\in\mathrm{B}(0,1+\mathrm{M}^{\mathrm{in}})
, we can however deduce a rough bound on
\nabla_{v}\mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{drag}}^{s;t}
by Grönwall’s inequality, which takes the form

|\nabla_{v}\mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{drag}}^{s;t}|\lesssim\exp(T\Lambda(T,R)(|v|+1))T\Lambda(T,R)\lesssim\exp(T\Lambda(T,R)(2+M^{\mathrm{in}}))T\Lambda(T,R).

We can now check that for
T
small enough, for all
0\leq s,t\leq T
, the map

v\mapsto v+\left(\frac{1}{t-s}\int_{s}^{t}\exp\left(\int_{s^{\prime}}^{t}\varrho(\tau,\mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{drag}}^{\tau;t})\,\mathrm{d}\tau\right)\,\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}-1\right)v\\
-\frac{1}{t-s}\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s^{\prime}}^{t}\exp\left(\int_{s^{\prime}}^{\tau}\varrho(\tau^{\prime},\mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{drag}}^{\tau^{\prime};t})\,\mathrm{d}\tau^{\prime}\right)E^{u,\varrho}_{\mathrm{drag}}(\tau,\mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{drag}}^{\tau;t})\,\mathrm{d}\tau\,\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}

is a
\mathscr{C}^{1}
diffeomorphism from
\mathrm{B}(0,1+\mathrm{M}^{\mathrm{in}})
onto its image, since it is a small Lipschitz perturbation of the identity map. Details are left to the reader.
∎

As a result, for small times, we can directly come down to the case of free-transport case.

10.3.
Modifications in remainder terms and conclusion of the bootstrap

To conclude, let us point out the last main modifications which have to be made to conclude the bootstrap argument.

In Section
5
, one shall be careful when handling the remainder terms
\mathscr{R}_{I}^{\mathrm{Diff}},\mathscr{R}_{I,1}^{\mathrm{Duha}},\mathscr{R}_{I,2}^{\mathrm{Duha}},\mathscr{R}_{I}
in
\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{1})
because they now involve some terms with at most
m+1
derivatives on
\varrho u
stemming from the force field
E^{u,\varrho}_{\mathrm{drag}}
. It was somehow harmless in the linear-drag case because the corresponding terms involved only
u
, which has an additional regularity provided by the Navier-Stokes equation, namely a control in
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})\cap\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m+1})
(see the terms
\mathbf{S_{2}}
,
\mathbf{S_{5}}
,
\mathbf{S_{12}}
,
\mathbf{S_{16}}
). Since we only have a control of
\varrho
in
\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})
, this requires a additional argument.

The main idea is to rely on the same type of decomposition as in Lemma
5.13
combined with the use of the smoothing estimates from Section
4
. The expression
\partial_{x}^{K}(\varrho u)
(
K\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
) will involve terms or sum of terms

•

of the form

\varrho\partial_{x}^{K}u\ \ \text{or}\ \ \partial_{x}^{\overline{K}^{i}-\beta}u\nabla_{x}(\partial_{x}^{\beta}\varrho)\cdot e_{i},

with
i=1,\cdots,d
and
|\beta|=0,\cdots,|\lfloor\frac{|K|-1}{2}\rfloor
. They are treated using
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}
bounds on
\varrho
(with Sobolev embeddings) and
\mathrm{L}^{2}
bounds on
u
;

•

of the form

\nabla_{x}(\partial_{x}^{\beta}\varrho)\cdot e_{i}\partial_{x}^{\overline{K}^{i}-\beta}u,

with
i=1,\cdots,d
and
|\beta|=\lfloor\frac{|K|-1}{2}\rfloor+1,\cdots,|K|-1

These terms are adressed thanks to Proposition
4.4
. We refer to Section
5
for the same kind of procedure for the estimates on remainders via the smoothing estimates of Section
4
.

Let us eventually briefly conclude by showing how the bootstrap procedure ends, when one considers the condition (
B

(
T
)

Θ
,

M
in

μ
,
θ

). Namely, we have to show how to control the compact support in velocity for
f
, for short times. We rely on the Lagrangian structure of the equation which implies a finite propagation in time of the support of
f
in velocity, namely

\displaystyle\forall t>0,\ \ \mathrm{supp}\,f(t)\subset\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathrm{V}^{t;0}_{\mathrm{drag}}(\mathrm{supp}\,f^{\mathrm{in}}).

Since

\displaystyle\mathrm{V}_{\mathrm{drag}}^{t;0}=\exp\left(-\int_{0}^{t}\varrho(\tau,\mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{drag}}^{\tau;0})\,\mathrm{d}\tau\right)v+\int_{0}^{t}\exp\left(-\int_{\tau}^{t}\varrho(\tau^{\prime},\mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{drag}}^{\tau^{\prime};0})\,\mathrm{d}\tau^{\prime}\right)E^{u,\varrho}_{\mathrm{drag}}(\tau,\mathrm{X}_{\mathrm{drag}}^{\tau;0})\,\mathrm{d}\tau,

the same kind of estimates on the trajectories as those performed in the proof of Lemma
10.4
give for all
(x,v)\in\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathrm{B}_{v}(0,M^{\mathrm{in}})

\displaystyle|\mathrm{V}_{\mathrm{drag}}^{t;0}|\leq|v|+T\Lambda(T,R)\leq M^{\mathrm{in}}+T\Lambda(T,R).

As a consequence, choosing
T
small enough (once
R
is given) is sufficient to control the size of the support of
f(t)
for short times.

Appendix A
Some classical (para-)differential inequalities on
\mathbb{T}^{d}
and
\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}

We recall and state several classic inequalities of (para-)differential type. First, we have the following tame estimate for commutators (see
[
MB02
, Lemma 3.4]
).

Proposition A.1
.

Let
s\geq 1
. There exists
C_{s}>0
such that for any functions
g,E\in\mathrm{H}^{s}\cap\mathrm{L}^{\infty}
, we have

\displaystyle\forall|\alpha|\leq s,\ \ \left\|[\partial^{\alpha}_{x},E]g\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}\leq C_{s}\left(\|\nabla_{x}E\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}\|g\|_{\mathrm{H}^{s-1}}+\|E\|_{\mathrm{H}^{s}}\|g\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}\right).

The following result is about tame estimates in Sobolev spaces (see e.g.
[
BCD11
, Corollary 2.86]
).

Proposition A.2
.

Let
s>0
. There exists
C_{s}>0
such that for all
w_{1},w_{2}\in\mathrm{H}^{s}\cap\mathrm{L}^{\infty}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|w_{1}w_{2}\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{s}_{x}}\leq C_{s}\left(\left\|w_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}\left\|w_{2}\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{s}}+\left\|w_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{s}}\left\|w_{2}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}\right).

We also recall the following result of Bony about Sobolev continuity of the composition by a smooth function (see e.g.
[
BCD11
, Corollary 2.87]
or
[
Dan05a
, Proposition 1.4.8]
for a more precise version)

Proposition A.3
.

Let
I
be an open interval of
\mathbb{R}
containing
0
. Let
s>0
and
\sigma
be the smallest integer such that
\sigma>s
. Let
F\in\mathrm{W}^{\sigma+1,\infty}(I;\mathbb{R})
such that
F(0)=0
. If
w\in\mathrm{H}^{s}
has value in
J\Subset I
, then there exists
C_{s}>0
such that

\displaystyle\|F(w)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{s}}\leq C_{s}(1+\|w\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}})^{\sigma}\|F^{\prime}\|_{\mathrm{W}^{\sigma,\infty}(I)}\|w\|_{\mathrm{H}^{s}}.

Remark A.4
.

Note that if
0\notin I
, and
w\in\mathrm{H}^{s}
with value in
J\Subset I
, we can extend
F
outside
I
by a smooth extension such that
F(0)=0
, and the previous proposition remains valid. Indeed, by Faà di Bruno’s formula, we observe that
\|F(w)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{s}}
only involves
F
through its derivatives evaluated at
w
.

Lemma A.5
.

For all
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
g:\mathbb{T}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
such that
0<c\leq g\leq C<1
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{1-g}\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{k}}\leq 1+\mathrm{C}_{k}\left(\left\|g\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}\right)\left\|g\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{k}},

for some non-decreasing continuous functions
\mathrm{C}_{k}:\mathbb{R}^{+}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
.

Proof.

We rely on Proposition
A.3
by writing

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{1-g}\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{k}}=\left\|F(g)-F(0)\right\|_{\mathrm{H}^{k}}+1,

and this directly concludes the proof.
∎

Let us finally state several product and commutator laws using weighted-Sobolev norms.

Lemma A.6
.

Let
s\geq 0
. Consider a smooth nonnegative function
\chi=\chi(v)
such that
|\partial^{\gamma}\chi|\lesssim_{\gamma}\chi
for all
\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
such that
|\gamma|\leq s
.

•

For any functions
f=f(x,v)
,
g=g(x,v)
and
k\geq s/2
, we have

(A.1)

\displaystyle\|\chi fg\|_{\mathrm{H}^{k}_{x,v}}\lesssim\|f\|_{W^{k,\infty}_{x,v}}\|\chi g\|_{\mathrm{H}^{s}_{x,v}}+\|g\|_{W^{k,\infty}_{x,v}}\|\chi f\|_{\mathrm{H}^{s}_{x,v}}.

•

For any functions
a=a(x)
,
F=F(x,v)
and
s_{0}>d
, we have

(A.2)

\displaystyle\|\chi aF\|_{\mathrm{H}^{s}_{x,v}}\lesssim\|a\|_{\mathrm{H}^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|\chi F\|_{\mathrm{H}^{s}_{x,v}}+\|a\|_{\mathrm{H}^{s}_{x}}\|\chi F\|_{\mathrm{H}^{s}_{x,v}}.

•

For any vector field
E=E(x)
and any function
f=f(x,v)
, there holds for all
s_{0}>1+d
and all
\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
satisfying
|\alpha|+|\beta|=s\geq 1

(A.3)

\displaystyle\left\|\chi\left[\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{v}^{\beta},E(x)\cdot\nabla_{v}\right]f\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}_{x,v}}\lesssim\|E\|_{\mathrm{H}^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|\chi f\|_{\mathrm{H}^{s}_{x,v}}+\|E\|_{\mathrm{H}^{s}_{x}}\|\chi f\|_{\mathrm{H}^{s}_{x,v}}.

•

For any functions
a=a(x)
,
f=f(x,v)
such that
f
has a compact suport in velocity, there holds for all
s_{0}>1+d
and all
\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
satisfying
|\alpha|+|\beta|=s\geq 1

(A.4)

\displaystyle\left\|\chi\left[\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{v}^{\beta},a(x)v\cdot\nabla_{v}\right]f\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}_{x,v}}\lesssim(1+M_{f})\|a\|_{\mathrm{H}^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|\chi f\|_{\mathrm{H}^{s}_{x,v}}+\|a\|_{\mathrm{H}^{s}_{x}}\|\chi f\|_{\mathrm{H}^{s}_{x,v}},

where

\displaystyle\mathrm{supp}\,f\,\subset\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathrm{B}(0,M_{f}),\ \ \mathrm{M}_{f}\in(0,+\infty).

Proof.

We refer to
[
HKR16
, Lemma 1, Section 3]
for the proof the (
A.1
)–(
A.2
)–(
A.3
). Let us briefly sketch the proof of (
A.4
). By expanding the commutator, we have to estimate terms of the form

\displaystyle I_{\gamma,\mu}=\left\|\chi\partial_{x}^{\gamma}a\partial_{v}^{\mu}(v_{i})\partial_{v_{i}}\partial_{x}^{\alpha-\gamma}\partial_{v}^{\beta-\mu}f\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}_{x,v}}

for some
1\leq i\leq d
and with
(\gamma,\mu)\neq(0,0)
,
\gamma\leq\alpha
,
\mu\leq\beta
and
|\mu|<2
. If
\gamma\neq 0
and
\mu\neq 0
, then
I_{\gamma,\mu}=0
or
\partial_{v}^{\mu}(v_{i})=1
and we can conclude as for (
A.3
). If
\gamma=0
and
\mu>0
then
\partial_{v}^{\mu}(v_{i})=0
or
1
and we conclude by Sobolev embedding in
x
since

I_{\gamma,\mu}\leq\left\|a\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{x}}\|\chi f\|_{\mathrm{H}^{s}_{x,v}}
. Lastly, if
\gamma>0
and
\mu=0
, we rely on the compact support in velocity for
f
to get

\displaystyle I_{\gamma,\mu}\leq M_{f}\left\|\chi\partial_{x}^{\gamma}a\partial_{v_{i}}\partial_{x}^{\alpha-\gamma}\partial_{v}^{\beta}f\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}_{x,v}},

and we end the proof as for (
A.3
).
∎

Appendix B
Local well-posedness for (

S
ε

): proof of Proposition
2.18

Recall that we want to find a solution
(f_{\varepsilon},\varrho_{\varepsilon},u_{\varepsilon})
(with
\varepsilon>0
) defined on some interval
[0,T_{\varepsilon}^{*})
to the system

(
\widetilde{S}_{\varepsilon}
)

\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{t}f_{\varepsilon}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f_{\varepsilon}-p^{\prime}(\varrho_{\varepsilon})\nabla_{x}\left[(I-\varepsilon^{2}\Delta_{x})^{-1}\varrho_{\varepsilon}\right]\cdot\nabla_{v}f_{\varepsilon}+{\rm div}_{v}[f_{\varepsilon}(u_{\varepsilon}-v)]=0,&\\[5.69054pt]
\partial_{t}(\alpha_{\varepsilon}\varrho_{\varepsilon})+\mathrm{div}_{x}(\alpha_{\varepsilon}\varrho_{\varepsilon}u_{\varepsilon})=0,&\\[5.69054pt]
\partial_{t}u_{\varepsilon}+(u_{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{x})u_{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{\varrho_{\varepsilon}}\nabla_{x}p(\varrho_{\varepsilon})-\frac{1}{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}})}\Big(\Delta_{x}+\nabla_{x}\mathrm{div}_{x}\Big)u_{\varepsilon}=\frac{1}{\varrho_{\varepsilon}(1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}})}(j_{f_{\varepsilon}}-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}}u_{\varepsilon}),&\\[5.69054pt]
{f_{\varepsilon}}_{\mid t=0}=f^{\mathrm{in}},\ \ {\varrho_{\varepsilon}}_{\mid t=0}=\varrho^{\mathrm{in}},\ \ {u_{\varepsilon}}_{\mid t=0}=u^{\mathrm{in}},\end{aligned}\right.

where

\displaystyle\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}}(t,x):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f_{\varepsilon}(t,x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v,\ \ j_{f_{\varepsilon}}(t,x):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f_{\varepsilon}(t,x,v)v\,\mathrm{d}v,\ \ \alpha_{\varepsilon}(t,x)

\displaystyle=1-\rho_{f_{\varepsilon}}(t,x).

To do so, we rely on a standard iterative scheme. We will derive two types of estimates on the inductive solutions to that scheme:

-

first, a
uniform bound in high regularity
which allows to obtain, through a weak compactness argument, a weak converging (sub)sequence in spaces with high regularity. This will be possible if we consider a small enough time of existence.

-

next,
contraction estimates in low regularity
which aim at proving that this sequence of solutions is a Cauchy sequence in spaces with lower regularity, thus strongly converging.

This will be enough in order the pass to the limit in the iterative scheme, obtaining a solution with the aforementioned high order regularity. Note that the first type of estimates is actually required to prove the second one. Uniqueness will classically follow from the same computations leading to the contraction estimates.

We fix
\varepsilon>0
and we now drop the dependency in
\varepsilon
(we can take
\varepsilon=1
for instance): we set

\displaystyle f^{0}=f^{in},\ \ \varrho^{0}=\varrho^{in},\ \ u^{0}=u^{in},

and for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, a triplet
(f^{n},u^{n},\varrho^{n})
and a time
T_{n}>0
being given with

\displaystyle(f^{n},\varrho^{n},u^{n})\in\mathscr{C}(0,T_{n};\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r})\times\mathscr{C}(0,T_{n};\mathrm{H}^{m})\times\mathscr{C}(0,T_{n};\mathrm{H}^{m})\cap\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T_{n};\mathrm{H}^{m+1}),

we consider the system

(
\widetilde{S}^{n+1}
)

\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{t}f^{n+1}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f^{n+1}+\mathrm{div}_{v}\left[f^{n+1}(E^{n}(t,x)-v)\right]=0,\\[5.69054pt]
\partial_{t}\mathfrak{m}^{n+1}+\mathrm{div}_{x}(\mathfrak{m}^{n+1}u^{n})=0,\\[5.69054pt]
\partial_{t}u^{n+1}-\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}^{n}}\Big(\Delta_{x}+\nabla_{x}\mathrm{div}_{x}\Big)u^{n+1}=-(u^{n}\cdot\nabla_{x})u^{n}-\nabla_{x}\pi(\varrho^{n})+\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}^{n}}(j_{f^{n}}-\rho_{f^{n}}u^{n}),\\[5.69054pt]
\varrho^{n+1}=\frac{1}{1-\rho_{f^{n}}}\mathfrak{m}^{n+1},\\[5.69054pt]
E^{n}:=u^{n}-p^{\prime}(\varrho^{n})\nabla_{x}\left[(I-\varepsilon^{2}\Delta_{x})^{-1}\varrho^{n}\right],\\
{f^{n+1}}_{\mid t=0}=f^{\mathrm{in}},\ \ {\mathfrak{m}^{n+1}}_{\mid t=0}=\alpha^{\mathrm{in}}\varrho^{\mathrm{in}},\ \ {u^{n+1}}_{\mid t=0}=u^{\mathrm{in}},\end{aligned}\right.

with

\displaystyle\pi^{\prime}(x):=\frac{p^{\prime}(x)}{x},\ \ \pi(0)=0,

and where

\displaystyle\rho_{f^{n}}(t,x):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f^{n}(t,x,v)\,\mathrm{d}v,\ \ j_{f^{n}}(t,x):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f^{n}(t,x,v)v\,\mathrm{d}v.

We also set

\displaystyle R_{0}:=100\left(\|f^{\mathrm{in}}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r}}+\|\mathfrak{m}^{\mathrm{in}}\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}+\|u^{\mathrm{in}}\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}\right).

Step 1: construction of
(f^{n+1},\varrho^{n+1},u^{n+1})
and uniform estimates
. In what follows, we construct the next iteration of the scheme which is a solution to (

~
S

+
n
1

), thus proving that our inductive scheme is well-defined. We derive at the same time some uniform estimates in high regularity on the sequence of solutions.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
. By induction, we assume that there exists
T>0
(depending on
\varepsilon
) such that for all
k=0,\cdots,n

\displaystyle\|f^{k}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r})}+\|\mathfrak{m}^{k}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})}+\|u^{k}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})\cap\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m+1})}<R_{0},

the functions
f_{k}
and
\varrho_{k}
are nonnegative, and for
t\in[0,T]

(B.1)

\displaystyle\forall t\in[0,T],\ \ \rho_{f_{k}}(t)\leq\frac{\Theta+1}{2},\ \ \ \frac{\mu}{2}\leq\varrho_{k}(t),\ \ \ \frac{\underline{\theta}}{2}\leq\mathfrak{m}_{k}(t)\leq 2\overline{\theta},

where
\Theta,\mu,\underline{\theta},\overline{\theta}
are the constants given in Proposition
2.18
. In particular, we have

\displaystyle 1-\rho_{f_{k}}(t)\geq\frac{1-\Theta}{2}>0.

Note also that via Sobolev embedding, Lemma
A.5
and Lemma
2.1
, we have

\displaystyle\|\varrho^{n}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{1}{1-\rho_{f^{n-1}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})}\|\mathfrak{m}^{n}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\Lambda(\|\rho_{f^{n-1}}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})}\right)\|\rho_{f^{n-1}}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})}\|\mathfrak{m}^{n}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,t;\mathrm{H}^{m})}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\Lambda(\|f^{n-1}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r})}\right)\|f^{n-1}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r})}\|\mathfrak{m}^{n}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda(R_{0}).

In what follows, we rely on the a priori estimates of Section
2
.

\bullet
We can obtain a unique nonnegative solution
f^{n+1}\in\mathscr{C}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r})
to the Vlasov equation by the method of characteristics. Since there is the regularization due to
\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}
, we can invoke Proposition
2.14
so that we have for all
t\in[0,T]

\displaystyle\|f^{n+1}(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r}}

\displaystyle\leq\|f^{\mathrm{in}}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r}}\exp\left[C\left((1+\|u^{n}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,t;\mathrm{H}^{m})})t+\frac{t}{\varepsilon}\Lambda\left(\|\varrho^{n}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,t;\mathrm{H}^{m-2})}\right)\|\varrho^{n}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,t;\mathrm{H}^{m})}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{R_{0}}{100}\exp\left[C\left((1+R_{0})t+\frac{t}{\varepsilon}\Lambda(R_{0})\right)\right].

We then define
T_{[1]}=T_{[1]}(R_{0})>0
with
T_{[1]}(R_{0})<T

(depending on
\varepsilon
) such that

\displaystyle\frac{R_{0}}{100}\exp\left[C\left((1+R_{0})T_{[1]}+\frac{\sqrt{T_{[1]}}}{\varepsilon}\Lambda(R_{0})\right)\right]<\frac{R_{0}}{3}.

\bullet
Since
u^{n}\in\mathscr{C}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m+1})
is given, we can construct a nonnegative solution
\mathfrak{m}^{n+1}
to the second equation in (

~
S

+
n
1

), relying on standard arguments for continuity equations.
We obtain a unique solution
\mathfrak{m}^{n+1}\in\mathscr{C}^{1}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m})
, with for all
t\in[0,T]

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{m}^{n+1}(t)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}

\displaystyle\leq e^{\|\mathrm{div}_{x}\,u^{n}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}((0,t)\times\mathbb{T}^{d})}t/2}\left(\|\mathfrak{m}^{in}\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}+\int_{0}^{t}\|u^{n}(\tau)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m+1}}\|\mathfrak{m}^{n+1}(\tau)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\right),

thanks to Proposition
2.3
. Assuming that
e^{R_{0}t/2}\leq 2
, we infer by Grönwall’s lemma

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{m}^{n+1}(t)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\|\mathfrak{m}^{in}\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}\exp\left(2\int_{0}^{t}\|u^{n}(\tau)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m+1}}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\|\mathfrak{m}^{in}\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}\exp\left(2\sqrt{t}\|u^{n}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,t;\mathrm{H}^{m+1})}\right),

therefore for such times
t
, we have

\displaystyle\forall t\in[0,T],\ \ \ \|\mathfrak{m}^{n+1}(t)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}\leq 2\frac{R_{0}}{100}e^{2\sqrt{t}R_{0}}.

We then define
T_{[2]}=T_{[2]}(R_{0})>0
such that
e^{R_{0}T_{[2]}/2}\leq 2
and such that

\displaystyle 2\frac{R_{0}}{100}e^{2\sqrt{T_{[2]}}R_{0}}<\frac{R_{0}}{3}.

\bullet
We define
u^{n+1}
as the unique solution of the parabolic equation

\displaystyle\partial_{t}w-\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}^{n}}\Big(\Delta_{x}+\nabla_{x}\mathrm{div}_{x}\Big)w=-(u^{n}\cdot\nabla_{x})u^{n}-\nabla_{x}\pi(\varrho^{n})+\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}^{n}}(j_{f^{n}}-\rho_{f^{n}}u^{n}),

starting from
u^{in}
, which satisfies

\displaystyle\|u^{n+1}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,t;\mathrm{H}^{m})}+\|u^{n+1}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,t;\mathrm{H}^{m+1})}

\displaystyle\leq(1+\sqrt{t}\Lambda(t,R_{0}))\Bigg(\|u^{in}\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}}+\sqrt{t}\Lambda(\|\varrho^{n}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,t;\mathrm{H}^{m})})\|\varrho^{n}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,t;\mathrm{H}^{m})}+\sqrt{t}\left\|u^{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,t;\mathrm{H}^{m})}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad\quad\qquad\qquad\quad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\sqrt{t}\left\|\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}^{n}}(j_{f^{n}}-\rho_{f^{n}}u^{n})\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,t;\mathrm{H}^{m-1})}\Bigg).

Here, we have applied the same argument as for the proof of Proposition
2.24
, making
\|\varrho^{n}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,t;\mathrm{H}^{m})}
appear to get a factor
\sqrt{t}
. The second term in the parenthesis is controlled by
\Lambda(R_{0})
while for the third term, we can use the same of kind of arguments (with Remark
A.4
) to get

\displaystyle\Big\|\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}^{n}}(j_{f^{n}}-\rho_{f^{n}}u^{n}

\displaystyle)\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,t;\mathrm{H}^{m-1})}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,t;\mathrm{H}^{m-1})}\left(\left\|j_{f^{n}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,t;\mathrm{H}^{m-1})}+\left\|\rho_{f^{n}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,t;\mathrm{H}^{m-1})}\left\|u^{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,t;\mathrm{H}^{m-1})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda(R_{0}).

We eventually obtain

\displaystyle\|u^{n+1}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,t;\mathrm{H}^{m})}+\|u^{n+1}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,t;\mathrm{H}^{m+1})}

\displaystyle\leq(1+\sqrt{t}\Lambda(t,R_{0}))\frac{R_{0}}{100}+\sqrt{t}\Lambda(R_{0}),

We then define
T_{[3]}=T_{[3]}(R_{0})>0
with
T_{[3]}(R_{0})<T

such that

\displaystyle\left(1+\sqrt{T_{[3]}}\Lambda(T_{[3]},R_{0})\right)\frac{R_{0}}{100}+\sqrt{T_{[3]}}\Lambda(R_{0})<\frac{R_{0}}{3}.

\bullet
Lastly, we can rely on Lemmas
2.16
–
2.17
to find a time
T_{[4]}=T_{[4]}(R_{0})>0
such that the condition (
B.1
) is satisfied for
(f^{n+1},\mathfrak{m}^{n+1},u^{n+1})
on the interval
[0,\min(T_{[4]},T))
.

All in all, we define

\displaystyle T(R_{0}):=\min\left(T_{[1]}(R_{0}),T_{[2]}(R_{0}),T_{[3]}(R_{0}),T_{[4]}(R_{0})\right)<T,

which may depend on
\varepsilon
but which is independent of
n
.
An induction procedure based on the three previous estimate shows that one can obtain a time
T^{[\varepsilon]}>0
and a sequence
(f^{n},\mathfrak{m}^{n},u^{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
satisfying for all
n\in\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle(f^{n},\mathfrak{m}^{n},u^{n})\in\mathscr{C}(0,T^{[\varepsilon]};\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r})\times\mathscr{C}(0,T^{[\varepsilon]};\mathrm{H}^{m})\times\mathscr{C}(0,T^{[\varepsilon]};\mathrm{H}^{m})\cap\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T;\mathrm{H}^{m+1}),

with (
B.1
) and the uniform estimate

(B.2)

\displaystyle\|f^{n}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T^{[\varepsilon]};\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r})}+\|\mathfrak{m}^{n}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T^{[\varepsilon]};\mathrm{H}^{m})}+\|u^{n}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,T^{[\varepsilon]};\mathrm{H}^{m})\cap\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T^{[\varepsilon]};\mathrm{H}^{m+1})}<R_{0}.

Step 2: contraction estimates in
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{T}\mathcal{H}^{0}_{r}\times\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{T}\mathrm{L}^{2}\times\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{T}\mathrm{L}^{2}\cap\mathrm{L}^{2}_{T}\mathrm{H}^{1}
. For
n\in\mathbb{N}{\setminus}\{0\}
, we set

\displaystyle g^{n}:=f^{n+1}-f^{n},\ \ \mathfrak{M}^{n}=\mathfrak{m}^{n+1}-\mathfrak{m}^{n},\ \ w^{n}:=u^{n+1}-u^{n},

which satisfy the system of equations:

(B.3)

\left\{\begin{aligned} \partial_{t}g^{n}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}g^{n}+\mathrm{div}_{v}\left[g^{n}(E^{n}(t,x)-v)\right]+\left(E^{n}-E^{n-1}\right)\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{n}=0,\\[5.69054pt]
\partial_{t}\mathfrak{M}^{n}+\mathrm{div}_{x}(\mathfrak{M}^{n}u^{n})=-\mathrm{div}_{x}(\mathfrak{m}^{n}w^{n-1}),\\[5.69054pt]
\partial_{t}w^{n}-\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}^{n+1}}\Big(\Delta_{x}+\nabla_{x}\mathrm{div}_{x}\Big)w^{n}=\mathfrak{S}^{n},\\[5.69054pt]
\varrho^{n+1}:=\frac{1}{1-\rho_{f^{n}}}\mathfrak{m}^{n+1},\\[5.69054pt]
E^{n}:=u^{n}-p^{\prime}(\varrho^{n})\nabla_{x}\left[(I-\varepsilon^{2}\Delta_{x})^{-1}\varrho^{n}\right],\\
{g^{n}}_{\mid t=0}=0,\ \ {\mathfrak{M}^{n}}_{\mid t=0}=0,\ \ {w^{n}}_{\mid t=0}=0,\end{aligned}\right.

and where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{S}^{n}

\displaystyle:=\left(\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}^{n+1}}-\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}^{n}}\right)\Big(\Delta_{x}+\nabla_{x}\mathrm{div}_{x}\Big)u^{n}-(w^{n-1}\cdot\nabla_{x})u^{n}-(u^{n-1}\cdot\nabla_{x})w^{n-1}-\nabla_{x}\left[\pi(\varrho^{n-1})-\pi(\varrho^{n})\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}^{n}}(j_{f^{n}}-\rho_{f^{n}}u^{n})-\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}^{n-1}}(j_{f^{n-1}}-\rho_{f^{n-1}}u^{n-1}).

Let us derive some
\mathrm{L}^{2}
estimates on
(g^{n},\mathfrak{M}^{n},w^{n})
. They will be satisfied on
[0,\widetilde{T}]
for some
\widetilde{T}^{[\varepsilon]}<T^{[\varepsilon]}
.

\bullet
We perform
\mathrm{L}^{2}_{x,v}
-weighted estimates in the first equation on
g^{n}
and as before (see (
2.3
)), we get

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|g^{n}(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{0}_{r}}^{2}\lesssim(1+\|E^{n}(t)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}})\|g^{n}(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{0}_{r}}^{2}+\left\|E^{n}(t)-E^{n-1}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}\left\|f^{n}(t)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r}}\left\|g^{n}(t)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{0}_{r}}.

For
t\in(0,T)
, we can infer

\displaystyle\|g^{n}(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{0}_{r}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}(1+\|E^{n}(s)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{m}})\|g^{n}(s)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{0}_{r}}\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|E^{n}(s)-E^{n-1}(s)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}\left\|f^{n}(s)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r}}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{t}\Bigg(\left(\sqrt{t}+\left\|E^{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,t;\mathrm{H}^{m})}\right)\|g^{n}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,t;\mathcal{H}^{0}_{r})}+R_{0}\left\|E^{n}-E^{n-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,t;\mathrm{L}^{2})}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{t}\Bigg(\left(\sqrt{t}+\frac{\sqrt{t}}{\varepsilon}R_{0}\right)\|g^{n}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,t;\mathcal{H}^{0}_{r})}+R_{0}\left\|E^{n}-E^{n-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,t;\mathrm{L}^{2})}\Bigg).

Choosing
\widetilde{T}^{[\varepsilon]}<T^{[\varepsilon]}
small enough independent of
n
, we can absorb the first term in the parenthesis in the left-hand side so that for all
t\in(0,\widetilde{T}^{[\varepsilon]})

\displaystyle\|g^{n}(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{0}_{r}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{t}R_{0}\left\|E^{n}-E^{n-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,t;\mathrm{L}^{2})}.

Then observe that by Sobolev embedding and Proposition
A.3
, we have

\displaystyle\|E^{n}-E^{n-1}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,t;\mathrm{L}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq\|u^{n}-u^{n-1}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,t;\mathrm{L}^{2})}+\left\|p^{\prime}(\varrho^{n})\nabla_{x}\left[\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\varrho^{n}\right]-p^{\prime}(\varrho^{n-1})\nabla_{x}\left[\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\varrho^{n-1}\right]\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,t;\mathrm{L}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq\|u^{n}-u^{n-1}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,t;\mathrm{L}^{2})}+\left\|p^{\prime}(\varrho^{n})\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,t;\mathrm{L}^{\infty})}\left\|\nabla_{x}\left[\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}(\varrho^{n}-\varrho^{n-1}\right]\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,t\mathrm{L}^{2})}

\displaystyle\quad+\|p^{\prime}(\varrho^{n})-p^{\prime}(\varrho^{n-1})\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,t;\mathrm{L}^{2})}\left\|\nabla_{x}\left[\mathrm{J}_{\varepsilon}\varrho^{n-1}\right]\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,t;\mathrm{L}^{\infty})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u^{n}-u^{n-1}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,t;\mathrm{L}^{2})}+\Lambda(R_{0})\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left\|\varrho^{n}-\varrho^{n-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,t\mathrm{L}^{2})}

\displaystyle\quad+\Lambda(t,R_{0})\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|p^{\prime}(\varrho^{n})-p^{\prime}(\varrho^{n-1})\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,t;\mathrm{L}^{2})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u^{n}-u^{n-1}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,t;\mathrm{L}^{2})}+\Lambda(t,R_{0})\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|\mathfrak{m}^{n}-\mathfrak{m}^{n-1}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,t;\mathrm{L}^{2})}.

This yields for all
t\in(0,\widetilde{T}^{[\varepsilon]})

\displaystyle\|g^{n}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,t;\mathcal{H}^{0}_{r})}\lesssim\sqrt{t}\left(R_{0}\|w^{n-1}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,t;\mathrm{L}^{2})}+\Lambda(t,R_{0})\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|\mathfrak{M}^{n-1}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,t;\mathrm{L}^{2})}\right).

\bullet
An
\mathrm{L}^{2}
energy estimate on the second equation on
\mathfrak{M}^{n}
also leads to

\displaystyle\left\|\mathfrak{M}^{n}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,t;\mathrm{L}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq e^{\left\|\mathrm{div}_{x}\,u_{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,t;\mathrm{L}^{\infty})}t}\int_{0}^{t}\left\|\mathrm{div}_{x}\,\mathfrak{m}^{n}w^{n-1}(\tau)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\leq e^{R_{0}t}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\left\|w^{n-1}\cdot\nabla_{x}\mathfrak{m}^{n}(\tau)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}\tau+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|\mathfrak{m}^{n}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,w^{n-1}(\tau)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{t}e^{R_{0}t}\left(\left\|\nabla_{x}\mathfrak{m}^{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,t;\mathrm{L}^{\infty})}\left\|w^{n-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,t;\mathrm{L}^{2})}+\left\|\mathfrak{m}^{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,t;\mathrm{L}^{\infty})}\left\|\mathrm{div}_{x}w^{n-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,t;\mathrm{L}^{2})}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{t}\Lambda(t,R_{0})\left\|w^{n-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,t;\mathrm{H}^{1})},

for all
t\in(0,\widetilde{T}^{[\varepsilon]})
.

\bullet
Performing an
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{T}\mathrm{L}^{2}\cap\mathrm{L}^{2}_{T}\mathrm{H}^{1}
estimate by multiplying by
w_{n}
in the parabolic equation satisfied by
w^{n}
yields

\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|w^{n}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}^{2}+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}^{n+1}}\left(|\nabla_{x}w^{n}|^{2}+|\mathrm{div}_{x}w^{n}|^{2}\right)\\
=\sum_{k=1}^{5}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\mathfrak{S}^{n}_{k}\cdot w^{n}-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left\{\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\nabla_{x}w^{n}_{i}\cdot\nabla_{x}\left(\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}^{n+1}}\right)w^{n}_{i}-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\mathrm{div}_{x}\,w^{n}\partial_{i}\left(\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}^{n+1}}\right)w^{n}_{i}\right\},

with

\displaystyle\mathfrak{S}^{n}_{1}:=\left(\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}^{n+1}}-\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}^{n}}\right)\Big(\Delta_{x}+\nabla_{x}\mathrm{div}_{x}\Big)u^{n},\ \ \mathfrak{S}^{n}_{2}:=-(w^{n-1}\cdot\nabla_{x})u^{n},\ \ \mathfrak{S}^{n}_{3}:=-(u^{n-1}\cdot\nabla_{x})w^{n-1},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{S}^{n}_{4}:=-\nabla_{x}\left[\pi(\varrho^{n-1})-\pi(\varrho^{n})\right],\ \ \mathfrak{S}^{n}_{5}:=\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}^{n}}(j_{f^{n}}-\rho_{f^{n}}u^{n})-\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}^{n-1}}(j_{f^{n-1}}-\rho_{f^{n-1}}u^{n-1}).

For all
\eta>0
, we obtain by Young’s inequality

\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|w^{n}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}^{2}+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}^{n+1}}-\eta\left\|\nabla_{x}\left(\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}^{n+1}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\right)\left(|\nabla_{x}w^{n}|^{2}+|\mathrm{div}_{x}w^{n}|^{2}\right)\\
\leq\sum_{k=1}^{5}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\mathfrak{S}^{n}_{k}\cdot w^{n}+\frac{1}{\eta}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}|w^{n}|^{2}.

Let us focus on the source terms
\mathfrak{S}^{n}_{k}
. We have by Sobolev embedding

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\mathfrak{S}^{n}_{1}\cdot w^{n}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|(\Delta_{x}+\nabla_{x}\mathrm{div}_{x})u^{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\left\|\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}^{n+1}}-\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}^{2}+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}|w^{n}|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim R_{0}^{2}\left\|\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}^{n+1}\mathfrak{m}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\left\|\mathfrak{m}^{n+1}-\mathfrak{m}^{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}^{2}+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}|w^{n}|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda(R_{0})\left\|\mathfrak{M}^{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|w^{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}^{2}.

We next have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\mathfrak{S}^{n}_{2}\cdot w^{n}\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}|\mathfrak{S}^{n}_{2}|^{2}+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}|w^{n}|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\nabla_{x}u^{n-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\left\|w^{n-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}^{2}+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}|w^{n}|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq R_{0}^{2}\left\|w^{n-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|w^{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}^{2},

again by Sobolev embedding while for all
\delta_{1}>0
, we have by integration by parts and Sobolev embedding

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\mathfrak{S}^{n}_{3}\cdot w^{n}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\nabla_{x}u^{n-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\left\|w^{n-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|w^{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{\delta_{1}}\left\|u^{n-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\left\|w^{n-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}^{2}+\delta_{1}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}|\nabla_{x}w^{n}|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda_{\delta_{1}}(R_{0})\left\|w^{n-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}+\left\|w^{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}^{2}+\delta_{1}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}|\nabla_{x}w^{n}|^{2}.

We also have for all
\delta_{2}>0

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\mathfrak{S}^{n}_{4}\cdot w^{n}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\delta_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}|\left[\pi(\varrho^{n-1})-\pi(\varrho^{n})\right]|^{2}+\delta_{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}|\mathrm{div}_{x}\,w^{n}|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda_{\delta_{2}}(R_{0})\left\|\varrho^{n-1}-\varrho^{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}^{2}+\delta_{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}|\mathrm{div}_{x}\,w^{n}|^{2}.

For
\mathfrak{S}^{n}_{5}
, we write

\mathfrak{S}^{n}_{5}=\left(\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}^{n}}-\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}^{n-1}}\right)(j_{f^{n}}-\rho_{f^{n}}u^{n})+\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}^{n-1}}(j_{f^{n}}-j_{f^{n-1}})\\
+\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}^{n}}\left(\rho_{f^{n-1}}-\rho_{f^{n}}\right)u^{n-1}+\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}^{n-1}}\rho_{f^{n}}(u^{n-1}-u^{n}),

therefore by Sobolev embedding and Lemma
2.1

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\mathfrak{S}^{n}_{5}\cdot w^{n}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|j_{f^{n}}-\rho_{f^{n}}u^{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\left\|\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}^{n}}-\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}^{n-1}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\ \left\|j_{f^{n}}-j_{f^{n-1}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}^{n}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\left\|u^{n-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\left\|\rho_{f^{n}}-\rho_{f^{n-1}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}^{n-1}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\left\|\rho_{f^{n}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\left\|u^{n-1}-u^{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}|w^{n}|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda(R_{0})\left(\left\|\mathfrak{m}^{n}-\mathfrak{m}^{n-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|f^{n}-f^{n-1}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{0}_{r}}^{2}+\left\|u^{n-1}-u^{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)+\left\|w^{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}^{2}.

We finally obtain

(B.4)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|w^{n}(t)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}^{2}+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\left(\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}^{n+1}}-\eta R_{0}^{2}-\delta_{1}-\delta_{2}\right)\left(|\nabla_{x}w^{n}|^{2}+|\mathrm{div}_{x}w^{n}|^{2}\right)\\
&\leq\Lambda_{\delta_{1,2},\eta}(R_{0})\left(\left\|\mathfrak{M}^{n-1}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|g^{n-1}(t)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{0}_{r}}^{2}+\left\|w^{n-1}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)+\Lambda(R_{0})\left\|\mathfrak{M}^{n}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}^{2}\\
&\quad+\Lambda_{\eta}\|w^{n}(t)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}^{2}.\end{split}

for some constant
\Lambda_{\eta}>0
. A good choice of
\eta,\delta_{1}
and
\delta_{2}
with respect to
\inf\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}^{n+1}}
combined with Grönwall’s lemma shows that

\displaystyle\|w^{n}(t)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}^{2}\lesssim e^{\Lambda_{\eta}t}\int_{0}^{t}\left(\left\|\mathfrak{M}^{n-1}(\tau)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|g^{n-1}(\tau)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{0}_{r}}^{2}+\left\|w^{n-1}(\tau)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\mathfrak{M}^{n}(\tau)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

Reducing
\widetilde{T}^{[\varepsilon]}
such that
\widetilde{T}^{[\varepsilon]}\leq 1/\Lambda_{\eta}
, we can integrate in time the previous differential inequality (
B.4
) to obtain for all
t\in(0,\widetilde{T}^{[\varepsilon]})

\|w^{n}(t)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,t;\mathrm{L}^{2})}^{2}+\|w^{n}(t)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,t;\mathrm{H}^{1})}^{2}\\
\lesssim\Lambda(R_{0})t\left(\left\|\mathfrak{M}^{n-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,t;\mathrm{L}^{2})}^{2}+\left\|g^{n-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,t;\mathcal{H}^{0}_{r})}^{2}+\left\|w^{n-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,t;\mathrm{L}^{2})}^{2}\right)+\Lambda(R_{0})t\left\|\mathfrak{M}^{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,t;\mathrm{L}^{2})}^{2}.

Combining the three previous points, we reach the following inequality valid for all
t\in(0,\widetilde{T}^{[\varepsilon]})

\displaystyle\|g^{n}(t)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,t;\mathcal{H}^{0}_{r})}+\left\|\mathfrak{M}^{n}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,t;\mathrm{L}^{2})}+\|w^{n}(t)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,t;\mathrm{L}^{2})\cap\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,t;\mathrm{H}^{1})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{t}\left(R_{0}\sqrt{t}\|w^{n-1}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,t;\mathrm{L}^{2})}+\Lambda(t,R_{0})\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|\mathfrak{M}^{n-1}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,t;\mathrm{L}^{2})}\right)+\sqrt{t}\Lambda(t,R_{0})\left\|w^{n-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,t;\mathrm{H}^{1})}

\displaystyle\quad+\Lambda(R_{0})t\left(\left\|\mathfrak{M}^{n-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,t;\mathrm{L}^{2})}+\left\|g^{n-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,t;\mathcal{H}^{0}_{r})}+\left\|w^{n-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,t;\mathrm{L}^{2})}\right)+\Lambda(R_{0})t\left\|\mathfrak{M}^{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,t;\mathrm{L}^{2})}.

Reducing again
\widetilde{T}^{[\varepsilon]}
, it means that that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}{\setminus}\{0\}

(B.5)

\|g^{n}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,\widetilde{T}^{[\varepsilon]};\mathcal{H}^{0}_{r})}+\left\|\mathfrak{M}^{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,\widetilde{T}^{[\varepsilon]};\mathrm{L}^{2})}+\|w^{n}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,\widetilde{T}^{[\varepsilon]};\mathrm{L}^{2})\cap\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,\widetilde{T}^{[\varepsilon]};\mathrm{H}^{1})}\\
\leq\frac{1}{2}\left(\|g^{n-1}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,\widetilde{T}^{[\varepsilon]};\mathcal{H}^{0}_{r})}+\left\|\mathfrak{M}^{n-1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,\widetilde{T}^{[\varepsilon]};\mathrm{L}^{2})}+\|w^{n-1}\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,\widetilde{T}^{[\varepsilon]};\mathrm{L}^{2})\cap\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,\widetilde{T}^{[\varepsilon]};\mathrm{H}^{1})}\right).

Note that
\widetilde{T}^{[\varepsilon]}
has been chosen independent of
n\in\mathbb{N}{\setminus}\{0\}
.

Step 3: obtaining a unique solution to
(

S
ε

)
. Combining the uniform bound (
B.2
) with the contraction estimate (
B.5
), we deduce that the sequence
(f_{n},\mathfrak{m}_{n},u_{n})
is weakly
(-\star)
compact in the space

\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,\widetilde{T}^{[\varepsilon]};\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r})\times\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,\widetilde{T}^{[\varepsilon]};\mathrm{H}^{m})\times\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,\widetilde{T}^{[\varepsilon]};\mathrm{H}^{m})\cap\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,\widetilde{T}^{[\varepsilon]};\mathrm{H}^{m+1}),

and is a Cauchy sequence in the space

\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,\widetilde{T}^{[\varepsilon]};\mathcal{H}^{0}_{r})\times\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,\widetilde{T}^{[\varepsilon]};\mathrm{L}^{2})\times\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(0,\widetilde{T}^{[\varepsilon]};\mathrm{L}^{2})\cap\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,\widetilde{T}^{[\varepsilon]};\mathrm{H}^{1}).

This shows that the whole sequence converges weakly
(-\star)
in the first space. The limit
(f,m,u)
belongs in particular to the first space with high regularity. Using weak-strong convergence principles then allows to prove that the limit is a solution to the system
(

S
ε

)
on
(0,\widetilde{T}^{[\varepsilon]})
in the sense of distribution. We don’t detail this part of the proof. Using the equation and the time derivative of the solution, one can show that the solution actually belongs to

\mathrm{X}_{\widetilde{T}^{[\varepsilon]}}^{m}:=\mathscr{C}(0,\widetilde{T}^{[\varepsilon]};\mathcal{H}^{m}_{r})\times\mathscr{C}(0,\widetilde{T}^{[\varepsilon]};\mathrm{H}^{m})\times\mathscr{C}(0,\widetilde{T}^{[\varepsilon]};\mathrm{H}^{m})\cap\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,\widetilde{T}^{[\varepsilon]};\mathrm{H}^{m+1}).

The uniqueness of the Cauchy problem for
(

S
ε

)
in the former space is eventually obtained by mimicking the contraction estimates of the Step 2. In fact, if
(f_{1},\mathfrak{m}_{1},u_{1})
and
(f_{2},\mathfrak{m}_{2},u_{2})
are two solutions in
\mathrm{X}_{\widetilde{T}^{[\varepsilon]}}^{m}
starting at the same initial condition, performing the same computations prove that there exists
\overline{T}^{[\varepsilon]}
(depending on
\|f_{1,2},\mathfrak{m}_{1,2},u_{1,2}\|_{\mathrm{X}_{\widetilde{T}^{[\varepsilon]}}^{m}}
) and with
\overline{T}^{[\varepsilon]}<\widetilde{T}^{[\varepsilon]}
such that for all
t\in[0,\overline{T}^{[\varepsilon]}]
, we have

\|(f_{1}-f_{2})(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{0}_{r}}^{2}+\left\|(\mathfrak{m}_{1}-\mathfrak{m}_{2})(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}^{2}+\|(u_{1}-u_{2})(t)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}^{2}\\
\leq\frac{1}{2}\left(\|(f_{1}-f_{2})(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{0}_{r}}^{2}+\left\|(\mathfrak{m}_{1}-\mathfrak{m}_{2})(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}^{2}+\|(u_{1}-u_{2})(t)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}}^{2}\right),

from which we directly infer
(f_{1},\mathfrak{m}_{1},u_{1})=(f_{2},\mathfrak{m}_{2},u_{2})
on
[0,\overline{T}^{[\varepsilon]}]
. Repeating this procedure starting from
\overline{T}^{[\varepsilon]}<T_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}
, we obtain
(f_{1},\mathfrak{m}_{1},u_{1})=(f_{2},\mathfrak{m}_{2},u_{2})
on
[0,2\overline{T}^{[\varepsilon]}]
. After a finite number of steps, we eventually obtain uniqueness on
[0,\widetilde{T}^{[\varepsilon]}]
.

Appendix C
Pseudodifferential calculus with a large parameter on
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{d}

In this section, we collect several results on pseudodifferential calculus that we shall need in this article. We refer to
[
AG07
,
Mét01
]
for a more general and complete approach. Here, our framework is adapted to the physical space
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{d}
.

For any symbol of the form
a(t,x,\gamma,\tau,k)
defined on
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{d}\times(0,+\infty)\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}{\setminus}\{0\}
, we use the quantization

\displaystyle\mathrm{Op}^{\gamma}(a)(h)(t,x)=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d+1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}^{d}}e^{i(\tau t+k\cdot x)}a(t,x,\gamma,\tau,k)\mathcal{F}_{t,x}h(\tau,k)\,\mathrm{d}\tau\,\mathrm{d}k.

Here, we use the discrete measure on
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.
Above, the variable
\gamma>0
should be seen as a parameter. The Fourier transform of any function
h(t,x)
defined on
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{d}
is denoted by

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{t,x}h(\tau,k)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}^{d}}e^{-i(\tau t+k\cdot x)}h(t,x)\,\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{d}x,

while the Fourier transform of any symbol
a(t,x,\gamma,\tau,k)

defined on
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{d}\times(0,+\infty)\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}{\setminus}\{0\}

is written as

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{t,x}a(\theta,\ell,\gamma,\tau,k)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}^{d}}e^{-i(\theta t+\ell\cdot x)}a(t,x,\gamma,\tau,k)\,\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{d}x.

For the sake of readibility, we introduce the notation

\displaystyle\eta

\displaystyle:=(\gamma,\tau,k)\in(0,+\infty)\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}{\setminus}{\{0\}},

\displaystyle|\eta|

\displaystyle:=\left(\gamma^{2}+\tau^{2}+|k|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

and we will use the notation
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{t,x,\eta}
to denote
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{d}\times(0,+\infty)\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}{\setminus}{\{0\}})
.

We state
\mathrm{L}^{2}
continuity results of Calderón-Vaillancourt-type: namely, we shall ask for
\mathrm{L}^{\infty}
bounds in all the variables for the symbols of the operators (see
[
CV71
,
Hwa87
]
). We first introduce the following family of seminorms for our symbols.

Notation C.1
.

For any
\mathrm{M}\geq 0
and for any symbol
a(t,x,\eta)
with
\eta=(\gamma,\tau,k)
, we set

(C.1)

\displaystyle\omega[a]

\displaystyle:=\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{d}\\
\alpha_{i}\in\{0,1\}\end{subarray}}{\sup}\,\left\|(1+t)\partial_{x}^{\alpha}a\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{t,x,\eta}}+\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{d}\\
\alpha_{i}\in\{0,1\}\end{subarray}}{\sup}\,\left\|(1+t)\partial_{t}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}a\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{t,x,\eta}},

(C.2)

\displaystyle\Omega[a]

\displaystyle:=\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{d}\\
\alpha_{i}\in\{0,1\}\end{subarray}}{\sup}\,\left\{\left\||\eta|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\nabla_{\tau,k}a\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{t,x,\eta}}+\left\||\eta|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\nabla_{\tau,k}\partial_{t}a\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{t,x,\eta}}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad+\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{d}\\
\alpha_{i}\in\{0,1\}\end{subarray}}{\sup}\,\left\{\left\||\eta|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{\tau}\nabla_{\tau,k}a\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{t,x,\eta}}+\left\||\eta|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{\tau}\nabla_{\tau,k}\partial_{t}a\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{t,x,\eta}}\right\},

(C.3)

\displaystyle\Xi[a]_{\mathrm{M}}

\displaystyle:=\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq|\alpha|\leq 1+\mathrm{M}\\
\beta=0,1,2,3,4\end{subarray}}{\sup}\,\left\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{\beta}a\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{t,x,\eta}}.

The following result states the
\mathrm{L}^{2}
continuity of pseudodifferential operator with symbol having a finite seminorm
\omega[\cdot]
. We refer to
[
Hwa87
, Theorem 1]
for a proof for symbols with compact support in
x
, whose adaptation to the physical space
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{d}
is fairly straightforward
5
5

5

More precisely, one can introduce a weight
(1+t)^{-2}
in
[
Hwa87
, proof of Theorem 1, eq
\mathrm{(2.5)}
]
to get some integrability in time.
This turns out to be sufficient to consider the seminorm
\omega[\cdot]
in our statement.
.

Theorem C.2
.

There exists
C_{d}>0
such that if
a
is a symbol satisfying
\omega[a]<\infty

then the following holds: for every
\gamma>0
, we have

\displaystyle\forall h\in\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{d}),\ \ \|\mathrm{Op}^{\gamma}(a)(h)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{d})}\leq C_{d}\omega[a]\|h\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{d})},

Next, we have the following symbolic calculus result, with the use of the parameter
\gamma>0
. Note that taking
\gamma
large can obviously be useful in view of an absorption argument. Here, we need to assume that one symbol has a compact support in time because the time variable
t\in\mathbb{R}
in unbounded.

Proposition C.3
.

There exists
C_{d}>0
and continuous nonnegative and nondecreasing function
\Lambda
such that for any symbols
a,b
satisfying

\displaystyle\Omega[a],\Xi[b]_{\mathrm{M}}<\infty,\ \ \mathrm{M}>1+2d,

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}b\text{ has compact support in time},

the following holds: for all
\gamma>0
and all
h\in\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{d})
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathrm{Op}^{\gamma}(a)\mathrm{Op}^{\gamma}(b)(h)-\mathrm{Op}^{\gamma}(ab)(h)\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{d})}\leq\frac{C_{d}}{\gamma}\Lambda(|\mathrm{supp}_{t}\,\nabla_{x}b|)\Omega[a]\Xi[b]_{\mathrm{M}}\|h\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{d})}.

Proof.

A standard formula about the composition of pseudodifferential operators first shows that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Op}^{\gamma}(a)\mathrm{Op}^{\gamma}(b)=\mathrm{Op}^{\gamma}(c),

with

\displaystyle c(t,x,\gamma,\tau,k)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d+1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}^{d}}e^{i(\tau^{\prime}-\tau)(t-t^{\prime})}e^{i(k^{\prime}-k)\cdot(x-x^{\prime})}a(t,x,\gamma,\tau^{\prime},k^{\prime})b(t^{\prime},x^{\prime},\gamma,\tau,k)\,\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\mathrm{d}\tau^{\prime}\mathrm{d}k^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d+1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}^{d}}e^{i(\tau^{\prime}t+k^{\prime}\cdot x)}a(t,x,\gamma,\tau+\tau^{\prime},k+k^{\prime})\mathcal{F}_{t,x}b(\tau^{\prime},k^{\prime},\gamma,\tau,k)\,\mathrm{d}\tau^{\prime}\mathrm{d}k^{\prime}.

Therefore for
\eta=(\gamma,\tau,k)\in(0,+\infty)\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}{\setminus}{\{0\}}
, we have

\displaystyle c(t,x,\eta)-a(t,x,\eta)b(t,x,\eta)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d+1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}^{d}}e^{i(\tau^{\prime}t+k^{\prime}\cdot x)}\mathcal{F}_{t,x}b(\tau^{\prime},k^{\prime},\eta)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\left\{\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{\tau,k}a(t,x,\gamma,\tau+s\tau^{\prime},k+sk^{\prime})\cdot(\tau^{\prime},k^{\prime})\,\mathrm{d}s\right\}\,\mathrm{d}\tau^{\prime}\mathrm{d}k^{\prime}

\displaystyle=:\frac{1}{\gamma}\mathrm{m}(t,x,\eta),

where

\displaystyle\mathrm{m}(t,x,\eta):=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d+1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}^{d}}e^{i(\tau^{\prime}t+k^{\prime}\cdot x)}\mathcal{F}_{t,x}b(\tau^{\prime},k^{\prime},\eta)\mathcal{J}(a)(t,x,\eta,\tau^{\prime},k^{\prime})\,\mathrm{d}\tau^{\prime}\mathrm{d}k^{\prime},

with

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}(a)(t,x,\eta,\tau^{\prime},k^{\prime}):=\int_{0}^{1}\gamma\nabla_{\tau,k}a(t,x,\gamma,\tau+s\tau^{\prime},k+sk^{\prime})\cdot(\tau^{\prime},k^{\prime})\,\mathrm{d}s.

Since
\mathrm{Op}^{\gamma}(a)\mathrm{Op}^{\gamma}(b)-\mathrm{Op}^{\gamma}(ab)=\frac{1}{\gamma}\mathrm{Op}^{\gamma}(\mathrm{m})
, and in view of the continuity property stated in Theorem
C.2
, it remains to estimate the seminorm
\omega[\mathrm{m}]
. For all
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
such that
\alpha_{i}\in\{0,1\}
, we now have

\displaystyle(1+t)\mathrm{m}(t,x,\eta)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d+1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}^{d}}(1-i\partial_{\tau^{\prime}})\big(e^{i\tau^{\prime}t}\big)e^{ik^{\prime}\cdot x}\mathcal{F}_{t,x}b(\tau^{\prime},k^{\prime},\eta)\mathcal{J}(a)(t,x,\eta,\tau^{\prime},k^{\prime})\mathrm{d}\tau^{\prime}\mathrm{d}k^{\prime},

and

\displaystyle(1+t)\partial_{t}\mathrm{m}(t,x,\eta)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d+1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}^{d}}i\tau^{\prime}(1-i\partial_{\tau^{\prime}})\big(e^{i\tau^{\prime}t}\big)e^{ik^{\prime}\cdot x}\mathcal{F}_{t,x}b(\tau^{\prime},k^{\prime},\eta)\mathcal{J}(a)(t,x,\eta,\tau^{\prime},k^{\prime})\mathrm{d}\tau^{\prime}\mathrm{d}k^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\quad\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d+1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}^{d}}(1-i\partial_{\tau^{\prime}})\big(e^{i\tau^{\prime}t}\big)e^{ik^{\prime}\cdot x}\mathcal{F}_{t,x}b(\tau^{\prime},k^{\prime},\eta)\partial_{t}\left(\mathcal{J}(a)\right)(t,x,\eta,\tau^{\prime},k^{\prime})\mathrm{d}\tau^{\prime}\mathrm{d}k^{\prime}.

Tedious but standard computations then show that for
\mathrm{M}>d
, we have

\displaystyle\omega[\mathrm{m}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\Omega[a]\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{d}\\
\alpha_{i}\in\{0,1\}\end{subarray}}{\sup}\,\left\|(1+|\tau^{\prime}|^{2})|k^{\prime}|^{1+|\alpha|}\mathcal{F}_{t,x}\big[(1+t)b\big]\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau^{\prime}}\times\mathbb{Z}^{d}_{k^{\prime}};\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{\eta})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Omega[a]\left(\underset{1\leq|\alpha|\leq 1+\mathrm{M}}{\sup}\,\left\|\mathcal{F}_{t,x}\big[(1+t)\partial_{x}^{\alpha}b\big]\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{1}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}^{d};\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{\eta})}+\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq|\alpha|\leq 1+\mathrm{M}\\
\beta=1,2\end{subarray}}{\sup}\,\left\|\mathcal{F}_{t,x}\big[(1+t)\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{\beta}b\big]\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{1}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}^{d};\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{\eta})}\right).

As a consequence, we obtain for
\mathrm{M}>1+2d

\displaystyle\omega[\mathrm{m}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\Omega[a]\left(\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq|\alpha|\leq 1+\mathrm{M}\\
\beta=0,1,2\end{subarray}}{\sup}\,\left\|\mathcal{F}_{t,x}\big[(1+t)\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{\beta}b\big]\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}^{d};\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{\eta})}+\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq|\alpha|\leq 1+\mathrm{M}\\
\beta=0,1,2,3,4\end{subarray}}{\sup}\,\left\|\mathcal{F}_{t,x}\big[(1+t)\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{\beta}b\big]\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}^{d};\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{\eta})}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Omega[a]\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq|\alpha|\leq 1+\mathrm{M}\\
\beta=0,1,2,3,4\end{subarray}}{\sup}\,\left\|(1+t)\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{\beta}b\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{1}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{d};\mathrm{L}^{\infty}_{\eta})},

and therefore

\displaystyle\omega[\mathrm{m}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda(|\mathrm{supp}_{t}\,\nabla_{x}b|)\Omega[a]\Xi[b]_{\mathrm{M}}.

From Theorem
C.2
, we have for all
h\in\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{d})

\displaystyle\|\mathrm{Op}^{\gamma}(\mathrm{m})h\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{d})}\leq C_{d}\omega[\mathrm{m}]\|h\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{d})}\lesssim C_{d}\Lambda(|\mathrm{supp}_{t}\,\nabla_{x}b|)\Omega[a]\Xi[b]_{\mathrm{M}}\|h\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{d})}.

We obtain the conclusion since
\mathrm{Op}^{\gamma}(a)\mathrm{Op}^{\gamma}(b)-\mathrm{Op}^{\gamma}(ab)=\frac{1}{\gamma}\mathrm{Op}^{\gamma}(\mathrm{m})
.

∎
